# ApplyPilot — Claude Code Operating Manual

## Mission

ApplyPilot is an autonomous job application pipeline.
Claude's job is to **operate, monitor, and fix the pipeline** — not to manually do what the pipeline automates.

**The goal**: Discover jobs → Score them → Tailor resumes → Generate cover letters → Auto-apply. All automated.

---

## Claude's Role (READ THIS FIRST)

**Claude is the pipeline engineer and operator. Claude does NOT manually apply to jobs.**

### What Claude Does
1. Run pipeline commands (`applypilot run ...`, `applypilot apply`, `applypilot status`)
2. Monitor logs and output for errors
3. Diagnose root causes when things break
4. Fix the source code (`src/applypilot/`)
5. Re-run and verify fixes work
6. Keep this CLAUDE.md updated with decisions and learnings

### What Claude Does NOT Do
- Open a browser via Playwright and manually fill out application forms
- Act as the "apply agent" — that's what `applypilot apply` spawns Claude Code subprocesses for
- Skip the automation and do things by hand "just this once"

### Daily Operating Loop
```
1. applypilot status              # Where are we? What's the funnel?
2. applypilot run discover        # Find new jobs
3. applypilot run enrich          # Fetch full descriptions
4. applypilot run score           # AI scoring
5. applypilot run tailor          # Tailor resumes for 7+ scores
6. applypilot run cover           # Generate cover letters
7. applypilot apply               # Auto-apply (Tier 3 — uses Claude Code credits)
8. applypilot dashboard           # Generate HTML dashboard for review
```

When a stage fails: stop, read logs, find root cause, fix code, re-run.

---

## Architecture

### Three Tiers
- **Tier 1** (Discovery): No API key. Scrapes job boards.
- **Tier 2** (AI Processing): Gemini/OpenAI API. Score, tailor, cover letters.
- **Tier 3** (Auto-Apply): Claude Code CLI as subprocess. Fills forms via Playwright.

### Two Credit Systems (IMPORTANT)
- **Tier 2**: Gemini API (free tier) + OpenAI fallback. Keys in `~/.applypilot/.env`
- **Tier 3**: Claude Code CLI with a Pro plan (2026-09-09 correction: prior docs/decisions in this file say "Max plan" throughout -- the account is actually Pro, meaningfully lower usage headroom than Max; don't assume Max-plan-scale budget when reasoning about `claude_cli`/interactive-Claude usage). IMPORTANT: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` must be stripped from subprocess env (launcher.py does this) or it overrides subscription auth with API billing. No Gemini browser agent exists — the Gemini/OpenAI cascade is Tier 2 only. `--strict-mcp-config` is required to prevent Docker MCP's Playwright (which can't access host files) from interfering with resume uploads.

### LLM Client (`src/applypilot/llm.py`)

Multi-provider fallback with two-tier model strategy:
- **Fast** (scoring, HN extraction): Gemini Flash → OpenAI → Anthropic Haiku
- **Quality** (tailoring, cover letters): Gemini Pro → OpenAI → Anthropic Sonnet

Key behaviors:
- `get_client(quality=False)` for fast, `get_client(quality=True)` for quality
- On 429: marks model exhausted for 5 min, falls to next in chain
- `config.load_env()` MUST be called before importing `llm` (env vars read at module import)
- Gemini 2.5+ thinking tokens consume max_tokens budget — set much higher than visible output needs

### Database (`src/applypilot/database.py`)

SQLite with WAL mode. Thread-local connections.
- `ensure_columns()` auto-adds missing columns via ALTER TABLE
- URL normalization at insert time (resolves relative URLs via `sites.yaml` base_urls)
- `company` column extracted from `application_url` domain (Workday, Greenhouse, Lever, iCIMS patterns)
- `acquire_job()` uses company-aware prioritization to spread applications across employers

### Pipeline Stages

| Stage | Condition | Tab |
|-------|-----------|-----|
| `discovered` | no description, no error | active |
| `enrich_error` | has `detail_error` | archive |
| `enriched` | has description, no score | active |
| `scored` | score < 7 | archive |
| `scored_high` | score >= 7, not tailored | active |
| `tailor_failed` | attempts >= 5, no result | archive |
| `tailored` | has resume, no cover letter | active |
| `cover_ready` | has cover letter, not applied | active |
| `applied` | `apply_status = 'applied'` | applied |
| `apply_failed` | permanent apply error | archive |
| `apply_retry` | retryable apply error | active |

---

## Candidate Profile

Loaded from `~/.applypilot/profile.json` at runtime. See `applypilot init` to create one.

---

## Key Commands

```bash
# Tier 2 pipeline (safe, uses Gemini/OpenAI)
applypilot run discover                        # Find new jobs
applypilot run enrich                          # Fetch full descriptions
applypilot run score --limit 100               # AI scoring
applypilot run tailor --limit 50               # Tailor resumes (score >= 7)
applypilot run cover                           # Generate cover letters
applypilot run score tailor cover --stream     # All stages concurrently
applypilot status                              # Pipeline funnel stats
applypilot dashboard                           # Generate HTML dashboard

# Tier 3 apply (uses Claude Code credits)
applypilot apply --dry-run --url URL           # Test one job (no submit)
applypilot apply                               # Auto-apply to cover_ready jobs
```

---

## Orchestration Strategy

When running the pipeline:
1. **Throughput** — use `--stream` for concurrent stages
2. **Quality** — highest scores get tailored first, company diversity in applications
3. **Error handling** — if > 30% failure rate, stop and fix before continuing
4. **Bottleneck focus** — priority is building the apply-ready queue

Error patterns:
- Gemini 429: automatic fallback, no intervention needed
- Tailor validation failures > 30%: investigate validator settings
- Apply credit exhaustion: alert user, cannot auto-fix
- `hn://` URLs or malformed data: check hackernews.py sanitization

---

## Security Decisions

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|----------|-----------|
| 0 | Never paste API keys in chat | Keys go directly into `~/.applypilot/.env` |
| 1 | Display name from profile.json | `preferred_name` in profile.json. Legal name for background checks only. |
| 3 | No real password in profile.json | Embedded in plaintext in LLM prompts |
| 4 | Tier 2 only until pipeline is stable | Auto-apply (bypassPermissions) = prompt injection risk |
| 5 | Review tailored resumes before using | `resume_facts` pins facts but still check |
| 6 | Gemini free tier + OpenAI fallback | Free primary, cheap fallback |
| 7 | Location from searches.yaml | Search radius and accept patterns are config-driven |
| 12 | Two-tier model strategy | Flash for speed, Pro for quality writing |
| 13 | High max_tokens for thinking models | Scoring: 8192, Tailoring: 16384, Cover: 8192 |
| 17 | Skip Gmail MCP / CapSolver | Too much attack surface with bypassPermissions |
| 18 | URL normalization at discovery | Resolves relative URLs via sites.yaml base_urls |
| 19 | Banned words = warnings not errors | LLM judge handles tone |
| 20 | Jobs without application_url = manual | LinkedIn Easy Apply marked `apply_status='manual'` |
| 23 | Company-aware apply prioritization | ROW_NUMBER() PARTITION BY company spreads applications across employers |
| 25 | Apply uses Claude Code CLI, not Gemini | Separate billing system. Spawns `claude` subprocess. |
| 26 | HN URL sanitization | Only stores http(s) URLs, deobfuscates emails, synthetic URLs for contact-only posts |
| 27 | Basic prompt injection defense | LLM prompts instruct to treat input as untrusted. Minimal — not a sandbox. |
| 28 | `--strict-mcp-config` for apply subprocess | Docker MCP Toolkit exposes duplicate Playwright tools that run in containers (can't access host files). Strict mode ensures only our local npx Playwright is available. |
| 29 | Funnel config: min_score=8, age=14d, cap=3/30d | 2026-04-23 funnel-optimization spec (docs/superpowers/specs/). Replaces score=7 threshold and soft-sort deprioritization. All paid stages gate on `fit_score >= min_score` AND `discovered_at` within age cutoff. Hard per-company cap enforced in `acquire_job` via YAML overrides at `~/.applypilot/company_limits.yaml`. Disable age filter with `--max-age-days 0`. Per-company overrides: set `max_in_flight: 0` to block, `-1` to unlimit. |
| 30 | Explicit state machine replaces implicit status derivation | 2026-04-24. `jobs.state` column (23 canonical values: discovered → enriched → scored → tailored → ready_to_apply → applying → applied → responded → interview → offer, with _failed/archived branches). Transitions via `database.transition_state(conn, url, to_state, reason, metadata, force)` — atomic UPDATE + audit row insert to `job_state_transitions`. Validated against `VALID_TRANSITIONS`. `apply_category` + `apply_status` + `tracking_status` kept for backward compat (DEPRECATED — read from `state` instead). `posted_at` column now captures employer-reported posting date (Amazon + Greenhouse scrapers fill it; other scrapers TODO). Retry-counter renames: `detail_retry_count → enrich_attempts`, `detail_next_retry_at → enrich_next_retry_at`, `score_retry_count → score_attempts`. |
| 31 | P0 state-machine rollout shipped | 2026-04-25. Tier-2 pipeline (discover/enrich/score/tailor/cover) and apply-stage launcher mark/reset functions all emit transitions. All 6 scrapers (jobspy/hackernews/costco/smartextract/amazon/greenhouse) emit NULL→initial transitions. 218 tests pass; 53 added in test_state_machine/test_stage_transitions/test_launcher_state_transitions/test_scraper_contracts. Known follow-up leaks (P0.5, NOT shipped — adjacent systems, single follow-up commit): (a) `update_tracking_status` (database.py:1843) bypasses transitions on email-driven status changes; (b) manual-ATS skip in launcher.py:1254; (c) stale-lock release in launcher.py:1133/2423/3107; (d) HITL re-queue startup path. |
| 32 | Per-worker resume dir | 2026-04-25. `build_prompt` now writes the resume copy to `APPLY_WORKER_DIR/worker-{wid}/` instead of the shared `APPLY_WORKER_DIR/current/`. Fixes audit #9 from the apply UX overhaul spec — concurrent workers no longer cross-pollute clean-filename uploads. `gen_prompt` debug helper updated to thread `worker_id` through. Plan 1 of 5 for the apply UX overhaul. |
| 33 | Apply uses Chrome for Testing | 2026-04-25. `config.get_chrome_path()` now prefers `~/.applypilot/chrome-for-testing/chrome-linux64/chrome` over system `google-chrome` on Linux. CfT is the only branded Chromium build that still accepts `--load-extension` after Chrome 137 (Stable/Beta/Dev/Canary all silently reject it). Existing chrome.py extension-load block at line 1144 finally works in production. CHROME_PATH env var still overrides. Plan 2 of 5 for the apply UX overhaul. |
| 34 | HITL paths collapsed | 2026-04-25. Three near-duplicate HITL paths in `_worker_loop_body` (generic `needs_human`, `login_required`, `HITL_AUTO_ROUTE`) collapsed into one `_run_hitl(worker_id, port, job, reason, instructions, navigate_url, duration_ms, ...)` helper at the top of launcher.py. ~150-line reduction. Each call site now parameterizes its differences (e.g. `login_required` clears the ATS session up front, generic threads `nh_detail` into instructions) and delegates to the same code. Screening-questions HITL stays separate — its mechanism is the TUI Q&A queue, not a banner. Module extraction to `apply/hitl.py` and the terminal-stdin Done-button fallback (audit #6) are deferred. Plan 3 of 5 for the apply UX overhaul. |
| 35 | Standalone human-review deleted | 2026-04-25. `applypilot human-review` CLI command and the 567-line standalone HTTP server (`serve`, `_start_hitl_chrome`, `_run_agent_for_job`, `_build_ui_html`) were removed. Audit #5 — duplicated the in-pipeline HITL banner+wait flow. Jobs parked as `needs_human` are now picked up automatically by the next `applypilot apply` run. `human_review.py` shrunk from 1,044 → 474 lines; remaining helpers (`_inject_banner`, `_navigate_chrome`, `_start_done_watcher`, `_build_banner_js`) still imported by `_run_hitl` in launcher.py. Plan 5 of 5 for the apply UX overhaul. |
| 36 | patchright launch flags + stdin Done fallback | 2026-04-25. (a) Added 15 patchright/playwright launch-time flags from `chromiumSwitches.js` to `launch_chrome` in chrome.py: `--disable-field-trial-config`, `--disable-background-networking`, `--disable-background-timer-throttling`, `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`, `--disable-breakpad`, `--disable-dev-shm-usage`, `--enable-features=CDPScreenshotNewSurface`, `--disable-hang-monitor`, `--disable-prompt-on-repost`, `--disable-renderer-backgrounding`, `--force-color-profile=srgb`, `--use-mock-keychain`, `--no-service-autorun`, `--export-tagged-pdf`, `--disable-search-engine-choice-screen`, `--edge-skip-compat-layer-relaunch`. Plus a longer `--disable-features` list including `AvoidUnnecessaryBeforeUnloadCheckSync`, `BoundaryEventDispatchTracksNodeRemoval`, `DestroyProfileOnBrowserClose`, etc. Stealth-flags-only — no patchright Python API integration (CDP-side init scripts deferred to extension rewrite). Verified CfT 148.0.7778.56 still launches via `/json/version` probe. (b) Audit #6: added a module-level `_stdin_fallback_lock` in launcher.py that gates a one-shot stdin reader inside `_run_hitl`. When a worker pauses, if no other worker holds the lock, a daemon thread reads one line from stdin; typing `done` (or empty) sets `hitl_event` so the user can override broken banner Done buttons. Multi-worker contention handled by the lock — second paused worker falls back to banner-only. |
| 37 | P0.5 status path leaks closed (audit #10) | 2026-04-25. All four leaks documented in decision #31 now emit `transition_state` calls so the canonical `state` column stays in sync: (a) `database.update_tracking_status` — when email signals raise `tracking_status` (ghosted/rejection/confirmation/follow_up/interview/offer), now also transitions `state` via `_TRACKING_TO_STATE` mapping. (b) `acquire_job` manual-ATS skip path (launcher.py:1257) — when a job is skipped because `is_manual_ats(url)` is True, transitions `state` to `manual_only`. (c) Stale-lock release in `acquire_job` (launcher.py:1135) — when bulk-clearing in_progress locks older than 30 min, transitions each affected URL back to `ready_to_apply`. (d) HITL re-queue startup path (launcher.py:3115) — when launcher.main re-queues parked needs_human jobs at startup, transitions each back to `ready_to_apply`. All four use `force=True` since the from-state isn't always one that legally permits the transition. Closes audit #10 in the apply UX overhaul spec. |
| 38 | Per-install random extension key | 2026-04-25. `chrome._get_or_create_extension_key()` generates a fresh RSA-2048 key pair on first call (via `openssl genpkey` subprocess) and persists the public key (DER + base64) at `~/.applypilot/extension_key.b64`. `setup_worker_profile()` patches each per-worker `manifest.json`'s `key` field with this value before Chrome loads the extension. Same key across all workers on this install (stable extension ID for our flows) but unique across users — defeats LinkedIn's bulk fingerprint of ApplyPilot's extension. Falls back to the manifest's hardcoded key if openssl is missing or the patch fails (logged at debug, non-fatal). Closes the per-install random extension key item from spec §3.1. |
| 39 | Extension per-job tab tracking + action log | 2026-04-25. (a) `extension/background.js` now tracks per-worker tab sets via `chrome.tabs.onCreated` + `openerTabId` chains. The worker's primary tab is seeded on SW init from the active tab; any tab opened from the primary or its descendants joins the in-set; tabs opened by the user manually (no opener) stay out. State persisted in `chrome.storage.local.tabSet`. Closes audit #3 (multi-window tracking via `document.hasFocus` was unreliable). (b) `extension/content.js` rewritten: on every page, asks the SW via `runtime.sendMessage({type: 'is-in-set'})`. If the tab is in-set, installs filtered event capture — clicks on `<button>` / `<a>` / `[role=button]` / `[type=submit]`, form submits (with field values, passwords masked), `history.pushState/replaceState/popstate` navigations, and an `initial` page-load marker. Events sent to SW via `append-action` message; SW maintains the ring buffer in `chrome.storage.local.actionLog` keyed by worker_id, capped at 200 events / 64KB per worker. Existing "Add to ApplyPilot" pill (the only previous content-script feature) is preserved on job-pattern URLs. (c) `window.__ap_form_snapshot()` exposed for future Done POST: scrapes all `<input>/<select>/<textarea>` final values (passwords + hidden skipped, capped at 200 chars per field). Spec §3.2, §4.4. |
| 40 | Pause-cycle action-log wiring | 2026-04-25. Closes the loop on the action-log capture from decision #39 — the agent now actually sees what the user did during HITL pause. (a) New `/api/action-log/{hash}` endpoint on each worker's always-on HTTP server (launcher.py). POST body `{events, snapshots}` is stashed in module-level `_action_log_cache` (with `_action_log_cache_lock`). (b) Content-script polling: every 500ms while in-set, content.js checks `window.__ap_hitl_done` (set to the hash by the existing CDP banner Done click). On 0→hash transition, it fetches the action log from the SW (new `get-action-log` message handler), takes a form snapshot of the current tab, and POSTs to `/api/action-log/{hash}`. (c) `_run_hitl` post-resume: after `hitl_event` fires and `reset_needs_human` runs, it pops the cache entry by hash, formats it via new `_format_action_log()` helper into a `USER ACTIONS DURING PAUSE:` block (timeline of clicks/navs/submits + form values per tab, capped at last 50 events), and threads it via the existing `extra_context` parameter into all post-resume `run_job` calls. The agent's resume prompt now says exactly what the user did, so it doesn't re-fill or contradict already-completed steps. 5 new tests in `tests/test_action_log_format.py`. Spec §4.1 / §4.4. |
| 41 | HITL toggle indicator in popup | 2026-04-25. `_start_worker_listener(worker_id, no_hitl=...)` now stores `no_hitl` in worker state and exposes it in `/api/status` as `noHitl: bool`. Popup renders a small amber `⏭ no-hitl` chip next to status when set. `worker_loop` threads its `no_hitl` parameter into the listener. Closes the spec §3.1 popup item. |
| 42 | Stealth init scripts via chrome.scripting (spec §2.2) | 2026-04-25. Background SW now injects an anti-fingerprint init script into the MAIN world of every http(s) page on `chrome.tabs.onUpdated` (status === 'loading') via `chrome.scripting.executeScript({world: 'MAIN', injectImmediately: true})`. Overrides applied: `navigator.webdriver` returns undefined, `chrome.runtime` populated with PlatformOs/PlatformArch/etc. on pages that lack it, `navigator.plugins` returns 2 fake plugins (matches real Chrome), `navigator.languages` non-empty, WebGL vendor/renderer disguised (Intel instead of SwiftShader), notification-permission query mirrors `Notification.permission` instead of headless-deterministic 'denied'. Manifest gains `scripting` permission. Patchright launch flags from decision #36 plus these init scripts cover both ends of the fingerprint stack: launch-time + runtime-MAIN-world. NOTE: declarative `world: "MAIN"` content scripts and `<script>`-tag injection from isolated content scripts both fail on real-world targets (CSP blocks); only `chrome.scripting.executeScript` works because it has extension privilege. Verified all 5 stealth checks pass via MAIN-world `chrome.scripting` reads (patchright's `page.evaluate` reads from isolated world by design, so it cannot be used to verify the overrides — that's a feature, not a bug). |
| 43 | launcher.py decompose | 2026-04-26. The 3,410-line `apply/launcher.py` split across three new modules, all re-exported back through `launcher` for backwards compat (cli.py + tests untouched): (a) `apply/result_handlers.py` — agent-output parsers (`_parse_account_created`, `_parse_qa_lines`, `_infer_result_from_output`), failure classification (`PERMANENT_FAILURES`, `HITL_AUTO_ROUTE`, `RETRYABLE_AUTH_FAILURES`, `_is_permanent_failure`), action-log file appenders (`_log_failed_attempt`, `_log_manual_action`), per-worker history (`_record_job_history`). (b) `apply/hitl.py` — full HITL pause/resume cycle: state (`_action_log_cache`, `_stdin_fallback_lock`, `_hitl_servers`, `_waiting_workers`), helpers (`mark_needs_human`/`reset_needs_human`, `notify_human_needed`, `_inject_banner_for_worker`, `_format_action_log`), and `_run_hitl` itself. (c) `apply/orchestrator.py` — runtime control flow: `worker_loop`, `_worker_loop_body`, `_prompt_user_for_qa`, `main`, `_probe_for_reconnect`, `POLL_INTERVAL`. Cycle resolution: each new module lazy-imports launcher's shared state (`_worker_state`, `_stop_event`, etc.) inside functions; launcher's `from .orchestrator import …` re-export sits at the very bottom of the file so by the time it fires, all of launcher's defs are complete. Final: launcher.py 1,843 lines (-46% from 3,410), orchestrator 809, hitl 669, result_handlers 357. 247 unit tests + 3 extension e2e all green through every step. |
| 44 | Settings options page (extension) | 2026-04-26. Full-page extension UI at `chrome-extension://{id}/options.html`, opened via `chrome.runtime.openOptionsPage()` from the popup's ⚙ button. Sidebar nav with sections: Integrations, Preferences, Q&A Knowledge, Credentials, ATS Sessions, About. | 45 | Embedded-ATS URL canonicalization | 2026-04-26. Many companies (Databricks, Stripe, Pinterest, Airbnb, ~470 rows in the queue) embed the Greenhouse application form as an iframe on their own careers page; the parent URL carries `?gh_jid=N`. Iframe forms are 2-3× slower to fill (parent-page noise, iframe-relative refs). Now: `discovery/url_normalize.py:canonicalize_application_url(url)` rewrites those parent URLs to `https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/{slug}/jobs/N` at insert time (`database.store_jobs`) and at enrich-result write time (`enrichment/detail._record_enrichment_result`). Slug source 1: curated `GREENHOUSE_HOST_SLUGS` map (19 hosts). Slug source 2: runtime cache `~/.applypilot/greenhouse_slugs_runtime.json`, populated by `enrichment/detail._learn_greenhouse_slug_from_iframe` which scrapes any rendered page for a `<iframe src="*greenhouse.io*">` and extracts the slug via `parse_greenhouse_slug_from_iframe_src` (handles both `/{slug}/jobs/{id}` and `/embed/job_app?for={slug}` forms). Aggregator-discovered URLs (LinkedIn, Indeed, BuiltIn) on unknown employer hosts thus teach the canonicalizer organically. One-time backfill rewrote 405 url + 454 application_url rows. Two prompt-side belt-and-suspenders companions: (a) `prompt.py` step 1b — agent runs a `browser_evaluate` after first navigate that scans for ATS iframes and navigates to the iframe src directly if found (covers cases discovery missed). (b) `extension/noise-filter.css` — document_start CSS that hides ~30 analytics/ad/social iframes (GTM, doubleclick, Hotjar, Bing UET, Facebook Pixel, LinkedIn Insight, Cloudflare Insights, Twitter, YouTube embeds, etc.) so `browser_snapshot` returns smaller a11y trees. Captcha iframes explicitly NOT hidden — apply prompt's CAPTCHA DETECT script needs them for `querySelector('iframe[src*="recaptcha"]')`. |
| 46 | Per-ATS successful-path memoization | 2026-04-26. When run_job ends in `applied` (literal RESULT:APPLIED or `_infer_result_from_output`), persist the ordered tool-call sequence to `~/.applypilot/successful_paths/{ats_slug}.json` keyed by `detect_ats(application_url)`. Next first-of-its-kind apply for that ATS gets a "PRIOR SUCCESSFUL PATH" section prepended to the agent prompt (after APPLICANT PROFILE, before YOUR MISSION) — explicitly framed as "guide, not a script" so the agent doesn't blindly replay it on a different employer's form. New module `apply/successful_paths.py` with `save_path` / `load_path` / `format_path_for_prompt`. Capped at MAX_STEPS=60 (most value is in the form-fill tail). Latest-wins overwrite (TODO #20 will switch to keep-fastest). Within-session warm-cache effect was already real (today's second Databricks job ran 400s vs the first's 857s on identical Greenhouse form); this commit makes it persist across pipeline restarts and across employers on the same ATS. |
| 47 | Default the apply web driver to sonnet | 2026-04-26. Function-level defaults in `run_job`/`worker_loop` were already sonnet; only the CLI flag in `cli.py:198` was haiku. Now aligned. Haiku saved per-call cost but spent so much time iterating on iframe-embedded forms (Databricks: $1.58/job over 14 min on the first Greenhouse-iframe encounter) that the total bill exceeded sonnet's likely budget for the same work. Sonnet handles form-state reasoning in fewer turns. Pass `-m haiku` explicitly to revert. |
| 48 | Extension ID computed at runtime, not hardcoded | 2026-04-26. `chrome.py:_suppress_restore_nag` had a hardcoded literal `APPLYPILOT_EXT_ID = "almfihgbac…"` for the static-key extension ID. Decision #38 generates a per-install random RSA key, so the actual loaded extension ID differs across installs (`jpeceibldblpkjcgpkfnkmhhjpljekbb` here). The pinning code added the wrong ID to `pinned_extensions` every run, so the actually-loaded extension was never pinned — user kept seeing it disappear. Fix: new helpers `_compute_extension_id(pubkey_b64)` (Chrome's `sha256(b64decode)[:16bytes]→a-p` mapping) and `_applypilot_ext_id()` (pulls key from `_get_or_create_extension_key`); `APPLYPILOT_EXT_ID = _applypilot_ext_id()` at runtime. Existing installs flush the stale literal via the `_EXTRA_STALE` cleanup pass. |
| 49 | Active no-hitl toggle in popup | 2026-04-26. The chip rendered by `popup.js:renderCard` is now a button (`data-action="toggle-no-hitl"`) instead of passive text. POSTs `/api/no-hitl` (new endpoint: `body {enabled: bool}`, mutates `worker_state["no_hitl"]`). `hitl._run_hitl` reads the live `worker_state["no_hitl"]` at the start of each pause and overrides the caller's CLI-time default — so flipping the toggle takes effect on the very next needs_human trigger without restarting the pipeline. Click handler stops event propagation so the chip doesn't double as the card-top focus target. |
| 50 | Worker-log buffering = line-buffered | 2026-04-26. `apply/launcher.run_job` opened `worker-{wid}.log` with default text-mode buffering (~8KB block buffer). Tool-call lines are ~30 bytes each; on a long iframe-form session the file looked empty for 10+ minutes while data sat in the user-space buffer. `open(..., buffering=1)` now forces a flush after every newline, making `tail -f` actually live during runs. |
| 51 | Cover-letter quality overhaul | 2026-06-10. Audit of all 3,823 generated letters found: median 138 words (target 300-400), 87 letters pitching LinkedIn as the employer, 62% containing the prompt's own example sentence ("Happy to walk through..."), 28% containing "aligns with", and the 4-paragraph structure collapsing to 3. Five fixes: (a) `tailor.display_company(job)` — company column → ATS-tenant slug via `resolve_company_key` → ''; `site` is NEVER presented as the employer. Used in both tailor and cover prompts; unknown company is labeled `COMPANY: unknown` with prompt instructions to infer from the description and never name the job board. (b) `generate_cover_letter` now returns `(letter, validation)`; `_cover_one_job` refuses to ship failed letters — rejected drafts go to `{prefix}_CL_rejected.txt` and the job parks as `cover_failed` (retried via `pending_cover` until `cover_attempts >= 5`). Previously failed letters shipped as `ready_to_apply`. (c) Too-short retries get explicit per-paragraph expansion targets in `avoid_notes` instead of the generic error string. (d) `validator.CL_BANNED_PATTERNS` — stem-based regex error tier (`align(s|ed|ing)? with`, `demonstrat\w*`, `happy to walk (you )?through`, `resonat\w*`) that forces regeneration, unlike warning-tier `BANNED_WORDS`. Prompt example closer replaced with a description ("name the actual system/migration/metric; no stock closers"). (e) Structure check: <3 substantial paragraphs (≥15 words) = error, 3 = warning. `run_cover_letters` returns/logs a separate `rejected` count. 22 tests in `test_cover_letter_quality.py`. KNOWN GAP: validator can't catch fabricated *metrics/facts* (e.g. invented "12B+ workflows/month"), only fabricated tools — Security Decision #5 review still applies. |
| 52 | Vast.ai GPU backend evaluated, then reverted (superseded by #53) | 2026-08-22/23. Explored using a Vast.ai RTX 4090 instance as a remote Ollama backend for the local-first tailoring planner, reached via SSH local port-forward, with all ApplyPilot state (DB, profile, keys, resumes, cover letters, sessions, logs) kept on Windows. Live validation worked (tunnel established, Vast Ollama reachable), but the instance's `workspace_is_volume: false` meant nothing on it was durable, its public IP/port were reassigned per-instance, and the ongoing engineering cost (tunnel lifecycle, instance persistence, GPU model management, server bootstrap) wasn't justified for ApplyPilot's actual workload. **Decision: reverted.** `scripts/vast_connect.ps1` deleted; Vast-specific env vars, hints, docs, and tests removed. The one durable improvement kept: `_try_openai_compat` (llm.py) and `get_local_tailoring_plan` (local_tailor.py) now catch `httpx.ConnectError` (previously only `httpx.TimeoutException`) and fall through to the next provider instead of aborting the whole chain — generically useful for any unreachable local/self-hosted endpoint, not Vast-specific, so it stayed. See #53 for the replacement architecture (hosted API only, no local/remote model server). |
| 53 | Hosted-API-only tailoring; local/Vast model server dropped | 2026-08-23. Following #52's revert, ApplyPilot's tailoring/scoring workload runs exclusively on the existing hosted-provider cascade (`llm.py`) — Gemini free tier primary, OpenAI/DeepSeek cheap fallback, Anthropic quality fallback, optional Claude CLI (Max plan) — with no local or remote model server required. This was already the pipeline's default behavior even during the Vast experiment: `local_tailor.py`'s local-first planner and the local-LLM fallback tier are both opt-in (`APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` unset by default, `--local-first`/`APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_PLAN` off by default) and were never on the required path. **Provider choice:** evaluated the four already-integrated hosted providers against the workload (structured job-description extraction, instruction-following resume tailoring, cost at hundreds-of-jobs scale). Gemini Flash/Flash-Lite remains the primary: free tier covers most volume, low per-token cost beyond that, strong structured-output/instruction-following, and it's already the OpenAI-compatible default in `_build_fallback_chain`. DeepSeek stays a fallback tier only (not promoted to primary) despite being the cheapest option — ApplyPilot sends resume/PII content in tailoring prompts, and DeepSeek's data-handling terms/jurisdiction are a weaker fit for that than Gemini/OpenAI/Anthropic. No new provider was added and no code in `llm.py`'s cascade changed — the existing multi-provider abstraction (retries, cross-provider fallback, exhaustion tracking, per-stage model overrides) already satisfies the "reliable, cheap at scale, structured-instruction-following, straightforward Python API" requirement, which is exactly why nothing beyond the #52 revert was needed to complete this move. `local_tailor.py`'s Ollama-native planner code itself is left in place (`get_local_tailoring_plan`, gated behind `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL`) since it isn't Vast-specific — it works with any genuine local Ollama endpoint, opt-in, off by default — but its docs/warnings no longer mention Vast or SSH tunnels. |
| 54 | Cognitive-linguistic schema layer for tailoring/cover letters | 2026-08-23. New `scoring/schemas.py`: a small, deterministic (zero-LLM-call) layer that computes ONE structured representation per job — requirement lines, ranked candidate evidence, and a rhetorical schema assigned to each SUPPORTED requirement — reusing `local_tailor.py`'s existing grounding primitives (`_split_requirement_lines`, `rank_profile_evidence`, `_auto_resolve_requirements`) rather than duplicating them. Audit before building found `local_tailor.py` already implemented ~80% of the "job concepts → candidate evidence" half of this (requirement extraction, evidence ranking, literal-vs-synonym matching, 0/1/2+-candidate confidence tiers) but nothing for the rhetorical/discourse half — bullets and summaries were governed by static prose instructions in `tailor.py`'s prompt, identical for every requirement regardless of evidence type. Library: 4 cognitive schemas (`action_object_outcome`, `problem_diagnosis_repair_verification`, `evidence_claim`, `domain_transfer`), 3 bullet schemas, 2 summary schemas. Selection is rule-based on evidence type + literal/synonym match-kind (a synonym/paraphrase match always gets `domain_transfer`, never a direct-match schema, regardless of requirement topic — prevents overclaiming). A schema is assigned ONLY to requirements the deterministic grounding already marked `supported`; ambiguous (2+ tied candidates) and unsupported requirements get no schema and are omitted from the rendered guidance — this layer never upgrades an uncertain match into confident-looking structure. `get_or_build_job_schema(job, profile)` caches the representation in-process, keyed by `(url, description_hash)`, so `tailor.py` and `cover_letter.py` share one computation per job instead of each doing independent (and, for cover letters, previously nonexistent) extraction — `generate_cover_letter` had zero structured evidence grounding before this; it sent the raw description + full resume text into prose instructions. Both callers prepend `format_schema_guidance(...)` to their existing user message, additively — no change to either's JSON/prose output contract, validation, or the existing `--local-first` local_plan text block. Exact job-posting keywords are preserved via the already-existing `matched_terms` (literal hits surfaced as "use these exact terms"; synonym hits surfaced separately as "transferable evidence, do not overclaim identical domain"). Zero new LLM calls anywhere in this module (verified by test) — does not touch the score-stays-cloud-only routing from decision history, and doesn't reintroduce local Qwen into any default path. Also fixed in the same change: `assemble_resume_text` (`tailor.py`) did `data["summary"]`/`data["skills"]` as direct dict subscripts — a malformed final-attempt LLM response missing either key (already flagged by `validate_json_fields`) still reached this line via `tailor_resume`'s "last attempt, assemble whatever we got" path and raised `KeyError`, crashing the whole job instead of returning `status="failed_validation"`. Now `.get()` with explicit defaults, logs a warning when either key is missing. 38 new tests (32 in `tests/test_schemas.py`, 6 in `tests/test_resume_assembly_robustness.py`) covering schema selection, composition, keyword preservation, evidence-to-schema mapping, unsupported-claim prevention, resume/cover-letter realization (guidance verified present in the actual outgoing LLM message), caching/reuse, no-LLM-call routing, and the exact `KeyError` reproduction. |
| 55 | DEGRADED MODE realizes schema-bounded content, not a full resume | 2026-08-23. Real-world testing after #54 found the normal cloud path much faster (~35s/job, 1-2/min) but DEGRADED MODE (all cloud tailoring models exhausted, local Qwen last resort) still took 10-15+ min/job and frequently timed out — `tailor_resume()`'s degraded branch was still asking `qwen3:1.7b` to generate an ENTIRE resume JSON via `local_tailor._build_compact_local_prompt`, now WITH the #54 schema guidance appended on top as extra context rather than as a smaller task. Investigated the reported `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_TIMEOUT=600` vs. observed ~120s-interval timeouts: the code (`llm.py`'s `_try_openai_compat`) reads the env var fresh on every call and was verified correct — the mismatch is almost certainly the var not being present in the actual pipeline process (a different shell session), not a code bug; moot either way once the task shrank. **Fix:** new `local_tailor.compose_degraded_resume_json(client, resume_text, job, profile, job_schema)` — Python deterministically parses the ORIGINAL resume via the existing, already-trusted `scoring/pdf.py` parser (`parse_resume`/`parse_entries`/`parse_skills` — the same parser the DOCX/PDF renderer trusts) and asks the local model for only a small, schema-bounded REALIZATION step: short bullet sentences (capped at 5, via new `_build_realization_prompt`) plus one summary, for requirements #54's schema layer already marked `supported` — never asked to invent headers, dates, company names, or skills categorization, all of which stay verbatim from the original. `_fuzzy_evidence_match` splices a realized bullet into its matching experience/project entry (substring match either direction between the parsed header and the evidence name, since profile.json's inventory `name` and the rendered resume header aren't guaranteed byte-identical); an unmatched entry just keeps its original bullets — never a crash. A failed/malformed local-model response (checked via `_parse_plan`, the same tolerant parser `get_local_tailoring_plan` already uses) degrades to keeping the original resume's sections verbatim rather than raising. Still exactly one LLM call, just a much smaller one — `tailor_resume()`'s degraded branch now calls this once (no multi-attempt retry loop; a retry can't fix a mostly-deterministic composition) and feeds the result through the SAME `validate_json_fields`/`assemble_resume_text`/`extract_facts_from_resume_json`/`is_auto_approvable`/`judge_tailored_resume` sequence the normal path uses, unchanged — existing anti-fabrication/anti-overclaiming validation is fully preserved. `tailor.py`'s `_build_compact_local_prompt` full-generation path is no longer called by the pipeline (kept in `local_tailor.py` for reference, not deleted — no test depended on it). 19 new tests (`tests/test_degraded_mode_realization.py`): prompt construction (bounded, skips the LLM call entirely when nothing is schema-supported), fuzzy header matching, composition (happy path, LLM timeout, malformed JSON, profile-skills-boundary fallback, never fabricating an unrealized bullet), and `tailor_resume()` integration (uses the new composer not the old one, exactly one local-model call regardless of `max_retries`, still validates, normal cloud path unaffected). |
| 56 | Degraded-mode stale-exhaustion-check fix + explicit base/realization/merge split | 2026-08-23. Follow-up to #55: a real 3-job pipeline run showed the redesign hadn't actually taken effect for one job — the log showed THREE ~5-6min local calls for one job and `assemble_resume_text` warnings about missing `summary`/`skills` keys. Root cause: `tailor_resume()`'s DEGRADED MODE gate (`is_local_configured() and not client_has_cloud_available()`) was checked ONCE, before the retry loop, using exhaustion state that's necessarily stale at that point — a model is only marked exhausted as a side effect of actually being tried inside `client.chat()`. Concretely: job 2's attempt 1 saw "cloud available" (nothing tried yet in THIS call, even though gpt-4.1-mini/gpt-4.1-nano were about to get exhausted for real), so it took the normal HEAVY `_build_tailor_prompt` path — which internally cascaded past the newly-exhausted cloud entries all the way to local anyway (a genuine local call, ~5-6 min, `max_tokens=16384`), got back a response missing required JSON keys (unsurprising — a 1.7B model asked to reproduce an entire resume), failed validation, and `continue`d to retry the SAME heavy prompt against local TWICE more. Job 3, later in the same run, correctly hit #55's new bounded composer because by then the exhaustion state was already known — proving the redesign itself worked, only its entry gate didn't fire at the right time. **Fix 1 (repeated-call bug):** the exhaustion check now runs fresh on every loop iteration instead of once before it, and the DEGRADED branch always `return`s immediately (never loops). One heavy "discovery" call is still unavoidable the first time a process encounters cloud exhaustion (there's no way to know a model is rate-limited without trying it) — job 2 now costs 1 heavy call + 1 bounded degraded call instead of 3 heavy calls, and any job after the first (same process, same singleton `LLMClient`, exhaustion state persists) goes straight to the bounded composer with zero heavy calls, matching job 3's already-correct behavior. **Fix 2 (max_tokens):** `llm.py`'s `_try_openai_compat` now clamps `max_tokens` to `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_MAX_TOKENS` (default 2048) for ANY call landing on `entry.provider == "local"` — protects even the unavoidable discovery call from requesting a 16384-token budget a slow CPU-bound model has no business being asked for. **Fix 3 (data-shape architecture, per explicit review request):** `local_tailor.compose_degraded_resume_json` split into three explicit pieces so "always structurally complete" is independently testable rather than implicit: `build_base_resume_model(resume_text, profile)` — deterministic, no LLM, always returns all 6 keys (parses the ORIGINAL resume via `scoring/pdf.py`, falls back to `profile.skills_boundary` if the skills section doesn't parse); `request_local_realization(client, job, job_schema)` — the one LLM call, returns an explicitly PARTIAL `{"summary", "bullets"}` dict (or `None`), now logs prompt char count, ~token estimate, `max_tokens`, requirement count, and call duration for diagnosability; `merge_realization(base_resume, realization, job)` — deterministic, `realization=None` or a missing key always means "keep the base model's value," never drops an untouched section. `compose_degraded_resume_json` is now a 4-line orchestrator over these three. Note: the observed "missing summary/skills" warnings were from the OLD heavy-prompt path's malformed Qwen response, not from the degraded composer itself (which always emits all 6 keys by construction, verified by test) — Fix 1 prevents that path from ever being retried against local, which is what actually eliminates the symptom in practice. 18 new tests added to `tests/test_degraded_mode_realization.py` (37 total in that file): exact reproduction of the stale-check bug (≤1 heavy call + exactly 1 bounded call) and the already-known-exhaustion case (0 heavy calls), `build_base_resume_model`/`merge_realization`/`request_local_realization` unit tests (always-complete shape, partial-realization-never-erases-untouched-sections, realization-is-partial-not-a-full-resume), prompt-boundedness assertions (char-size ceiling, never contains resume/job-description text), and malformed-realization-falls-back-to-complete-base tests. |
| 57 | Cloud-only discovery attempt eliminates the expensive degraded-mode discovery call | 2026-08-23. Follow-up to #56: a real run showed #56's fix working (no more 3 heavy calls; job 3 correctly skipped straight to degraded mode) but job 2 — the FIRST job in the process to hit cloud exhaustion — still spent ~5m38s in one heavy full-tailoring-prompt call that happened to cascade through to `local/qwen3:1.7b` before `client.chat()` gave up, and only THEN switched to degraded mode; the resulting resume also had zero schema-supported requirements and failed validation — a genuinely separate, not-yet-addressed problem (no evidence matched this job's requirements at all), unrelated to the call-routing issue fixed here. **Fix:** `LLMClient.chat()` (`llm.py`) gains an `exclude_providers: frozenset[str] | None` parameter — a per-call filter applied before the exhaustion/cooldown logic, deliberately NOT implemented by mutating `self._fallback_chain` (that's shared, unsafe across `--workers > 1` threads). `tailor_resume()`'s heavy cloud-quality-prompt call now passes `exclude_providers=frozenset({"local"})` and wraps the call in `try/except RuntimeError`: if every non-local entry is exhausted, `chat()` now fails FAST (each rejection is a ~1-2s 429, not a multi-minute local generation) instead of silently falling through to local with a prompt/token-budget combination that was never intended for it; the caught `RuntimeError` redirects immediately into the SAME bounded degraded composer from #56 (extracted into a `_run_degraded_mode()` closure, called from both the fast-path gate and this new except-branch, so the logic isn't duplicated). Net effect: the "discovery" cost for the first job to hit exhaustion in a process drops from one multi-minute heavy local call to a couple of seconds of failed cloud attempts — the heavy prompt now NEVER reaches local, not even once. No new LLM/network call added to the normal cloud-success path — `exclude_providers` only changes behavior once every non-local provider is already exhausted, which is exactly the condition that used to trigger the expensive fallthrough. 4 new tests in `test_llm_cascade.py` (`TestChatExcludeProviders`: excluded provider never attempted, all-non-excluded-exhausted raises without touching local, no `_fallback_chain` mutation — thread-safety guard, backward-compat when the param is omitted); one test in `test_degraded_mode_realization.py` rewritten to verify the heavy attempt is given `exclude_providers` and never reaches local even in the discovery case. |
| 58 | Degraded mode distinguishes no-supported-evidence / realization-failed / genuinely-invalid | 2026-08-23. Follow-up to #57 in the same run: with #56/#57's fixes both working (no repeated heavy calls, discovery no longer reaches local), a job with ZERO schema-supported requirements still came back `status="failed_validation"` — confusing, since nothing was actually generated to validate. Root cause: degraded mode always ran the (verbatim, since nothing was realized) merged resume through `validate_json_fields`/`assemble_resume_text` regardless of WHY nothing was realized — collapsing three semantically different outcomes ("grounding found nothing safe to tailor", "the local model's call/response failed", "a real merged result was genuinely invalid") into one status, and answering "is the base resume valid" (unrelated to tailoring) instead of "did tailoring introduce a problem". **Fix:** `tailor_resume()`'s `_run_degraded_mode` now calls `build_base_resume_model`/`request_local_realization`/`merge_realization` directly (not the `compose_degraded_resume_json` orchestrator, which still exists standalone/tested but always merges regardless of outcome) so it can see the raw realization result before deciding what to do. When `realization is None`: if `request_local_realization`'s `meta["llm_called"]` is `False` (the deterministic schema layer found zero supported requirements — local Qwen was never even called), status is `"no_supported_evidence"`; if `True` (Qwen was called but the response failed/timed out/didn't parse/was empty), status is `"local_realization_failed"`. Either way `resume_text` is returned LITERALLY unchanged (not re-parsed-and-reassembled) and `validate_json_fields`/`judge_tailored_resume` are skipped entirely — there is nothing new to check, and skipping the round-trip through the resume parser also avoids the base resume's own formatting quirks masquerading as a tailoring failure. Both statuses flow through the EXISTING `_mark_tailor_result` generic else-branch unchanged (still `tailor_failed` + retry-eligible on a later run, when cloud may have recovered — deliberately not treated as `"approved"`, since no job-specific tailoring happened) — no new DB/state-machine code, just a more informative `reason` in the audit trail. `run_tailoring`'s summary log line and returned `"failed"` count now include both new statuses (previously they'd have silently vanished from the aggregate — `stats` dict already handled arbitrary status keys via `.get(..., 0) + 1`, but the printed summary and returned dict only enumerated the original four). 10 new tests in `test_degraded_mode_realization.py` (`TestNoSupportedEvidenceAndRealizationFailureStatuses`, 47 total in that file): no-supported-evidence → correct status, zero local calls, byte-identical resume, validation/judge both `None`, no retry loop; realization-exception and malformed-JSON → `local_realization_failed`, exactly one local call, validation skipped; a contrast test proving a genuine post-realization validation failure still reports `failed_validation` (cases 1/2 aren't masking real problems). |
| 59 | Cognitive-linguistic architecture expansion: frame/event/claim-strength/viewpoint/salience layers | 2026-08-24. Requested investigation of ~25 cognitive-linguistic systems (frame semantics, event structure, causal structure, force dynamics, image schemas, binding, prototype theory, domain-transfer typing, viewpoint, figure-ground, profiling, granularity, salience, information structure, construction grammar, conceptual metaphor, metonymy, blending, claim-strength/entailment, discourse coherence, construal operations) for `scoring/schemas.py`'s job-schema representation (#54-58). Assessment found several systems were redundant relabelings of the SAME extractable signal (a migration's SOURCE-PATH-GOAL image schema *is* frame=migration + an event-structure transition; force dynamics *is* frame + causal structure under different vocabulary) — deliberately DEFERRED with written rationale in `schemas.py`'s module docstring rather than implemented as parallel fields: force dynamics, image schemas, conceptual metaphor, metonymy-as-separate-structure, blending-as-separate-structure, granularity-as-separate-system (covered by existing exact_keywords/synonym_concepts), construal-operations-as-a-named-layer (their effects are what viewpoint/salience/frame compute directly). **Implemented** (all deterministic, zero new LLM calls, extending the existing per-job representation): FRAME (`FRAMES`, ~9-entry situational ontology, generalizes the old single-purpose troubleshooting regex — `select_frame`); EVENT TYPE (`classify_event_type` — accomplishment vs. activity, from whether the evidence's own text states a completion/outcome verb); CATEGORY TIER (`classify_category_tier` — graded prototype/near_prototype/peripheral/unsupported, replacing the old binary literal/synonym match_kind); VIEWPOINT (`select_viewpoint` — one coarse job-title-derived professional lens per job: engineering/data/operations/support/leadership/general — same facts, different foregrounding); SALIENCE (`rank_salience` — merges figure-ground/profiling/information-structure into one ordering of which element opens the sentence); CONSTRUCTION (`BULLET_SCHEMAS` entries now carry a concrete example `pattern`, not just abstract slot names); DISCOURSE (`flag_repetition` — marks requirements whose (frame, cognitive_schema) repeats 3+ times, nudging phrasing variety without changing schema assignment); PROVENANCE (each requirement's representation carries the literal evidence source text, truncated to 300 chars). **CLAIM-STRENGTH LATTICE — the centerpiece safety layer** (`CLAIM_TIERS = (participation, execution, implementation, design, authority)`, `detect_claim_tier`, `claim_ceiling_for_evidence`, `check_claim_strength`): each supported requirement's evidence gets a ceiling derived from the VERB ALREADY PRESENT in that evidence's own `description`/`factual_concepts` text (a human already vetted that wording) — never a stronger default when no verb is found. `check_claim_strength(realized_text, ceiling)` is the deterministic post-generation check. **CAUSAL CLAIM CHECK** (`check_causal_claim`) is a second, orthogonal check: a realized bullet using an outcome verb (reduced/eliminated/increased/improved/etc.) must have that SAME outcome vocabulary already present in the evidence text, or it's rejected as an invented causal claim — "used Python" needs no such support, "used Python, reducing manual effort" does. **Enforcement:** `local_tailor.request_local_realization` runs both checks on every degraded-mode bullet AND the summary (summary ceiling = the max ceiling across the job's supported evidence) — a violation drops that bullet/summary outright (never downgrades/rewrites, which risks an ungrammatical patch); `merge_realization` then falls back to the entry's original content, always safe. The advisory ceiling is also shown to the model in the realization prompt (`_REALIZATION_SYSTEM` now explains the 5-tier lattice), but the deterministic check is the actual safety net for a model that ignores it — verified live: a mocked response claiming "Architected the full vehicle diagnostic system" against participation-tier evidence was correctly dropped. The CLOUD path has no per-bullet evidence tag to check against that precisely, so it gets a coarser WARNING-tier check instead: `validator.check_claim_inflation` flags (never hard-rejects, per decision #19's warnings-not-errors precedent) when the generated resume uses an authority-tier verb anywhere while NOTHING in the candidate's own profile evidence earns that tier at all. **Two real bugs caught by manual verification before writing tests** (both fixed): (a) the verb-detection regexes only matched past-tense forms (`reduced`) — LLM output commonly uses gerunds (`reducing`) — silently missing half of realistic phrasings; rewritten as explicit base/past/-ing/3rd-person form lists (not loose `\w*` stems, which would false-positive on unrelated words like "user"/"usable" matching a naive `us\w*`). (b) `claim_ceiling_for_evidence`/`classify_event_type` were called with the RANKED-evidence wrapper dict (`{"type","name","score","matched_terms","item"}`) instead of the nested raw profile item (`wrapper["item"]`) — silently always defaulted to "participation"/"activity" regardless of the evidence's actual text; caught because a description literally containing "Automated..." still produced ceiling="participation" until fixed. 70 new tests in `tests/test_cognitive_linguistic_expansion.py` covering every category from the review request (evidence safety matrix including illegitimate-transfer-never-gets-a-direct-schema; construal/viewpoint/salience; event structure incl. gerund-form detection; claim strength incl. the exact "used cannot become architected" / "participated in cannot become led" examples; causal-claim rejection and legitimate-causal-claim-passes; provenance; cloud-path claim-inflation warning) plus the vocabulary-gap regression cases found during manual verification. Full suite green after both bug fixes. |
| 60 | Round-2 expansion: agency axis split, frame widening, prevention/force_relation, metric-fabrication + passive-voice checks | 2026-08-25. Follow-up requesting a systematic audit (not incremental additions) of ~21 further cognitive-linguistic systems (frame semantics widening, event structure, force dynamics, image schemas, construction grammar, information structure, figure-ground, profiling, construal operations, granularity, conceptual metaphor, metonymy, blending, prototype refinement, claim-strength decomposition, causality, viewpoint, salience, repetition, provenance, a mega multidimensional-evidence representation) against #54-59's existing layer, with an explicit orthogonality matrix and conservative implement/defer/reject triage. **Rejected as redundant** (documented in `schemas.py`'s module docstring): image schemas (subsumed by frame+event_type — a migration's SOURCE-PATH-GOAL schema is nothing frame=migration doesn't already say), conceptual metaphor as a system (already handled by `validator.BANNED_WORDS`), blending (redundant with the existing `domain_transfer` schema/synonym-match path), "functional_neighbor" as a 5th category tier (not reliably distinguishable from peripheral without embeddings), dynamicity/subjectification/scanning construal operations (no deterministic extraction path and no clear differential effect on output). **Deferred**: a formal "scope" claim axis (task/component/system/org — relative-size extraction from free text isn't reliable enough to gate on), post-generation verb-diversity enforcement (would need a bullet-regeneration capability the merge flow doesn't have), a formal granularity ladder (the existing `exact_keywords`/`synonym_concepts` mechanism already captures the practically useful part). **Implemented**, all deterministic / zero new LLM calls: (1) FRAME widened from 9 to 14 entries (`security`, `optimization`, `monitoring`, `integration`, `training_mentorship` added to `FRAMES`), with `_FRAME_PRIORITY` ordering `leadership_project` before `training_mentorship` so an evidence phrase containing both "lead" and "mentor" resolves to the more specific leadership frame, not the training one. (2) FORCE_RELATION — deliberately narrow, not a full force-dynamics system: `detect_force_relation()` recognizes only a "prevention" relation (risk identified + intervention + avoided outcome); `select_schema_for_requirement` routes prevention evidence to a new `prevention_intervention_result` bullet schema ("Identified [risk] and [intervention], preventing [outcome]...") ahead of the normal frame dispatch. (3) DISCOURSE_FUNCTION — each `BULLET_SCHEMAS` entry now states its rhetorical purpose (e.g. "foreground candidate agency"), surfaced in `format_schema_guidance` and the realization prompt. (4) AGENCY AXIS — the centerpiece of this round, closing a real gap: `_CLAIM_VERB_PATTERNS["authority"]` was narrowed to ONLY `architected` (previously also matched led/managed/owned/directed/spearheaded), and those people-authority verbs moved to a new, independent `AGENCY_TIERS = (individual_contributor, owner, team_lead, director)` lattice (`detect_agency_tier`, `agency_ceiling_for_evidence`, `check_agency_strength`) — because "architected the payments platform" (technical-depth authority) does NOT license "led the team that architected the platform" (agency authority) unless the evidence separately supports people-management. Both ceilings are now computed per-requirement (`entry["claim_ceiling"]`/`entry["agency_ceiling"]`) and enforced independently in `local_tailor.request_local_realization`, which now also threads a new `profile` parameter through from its one call site in `tailor.py`. (5) `claim_ceiling_for_evidence` gained a fallback to the evidence item's `evidence_level`/`proficiency` field when no verb is found in description/factual_concepts. (6) PROVENANCE restructured from a bare string to `{"source_type": ..., "text": ...}` so the source (experience/project/profile/etc.) is auditable, not just the text — this broke `local_tailor.py`'s old `provenance_by_evidence[name] = provenance[0]` (now extracts `.get("text", "")`, with a str() fallback for defensive compatibility). (7) METRIC-FABRICATION CHECK (`check_metric_fabrication`) — a second, sharper check than causal-claim: a legitimately-grounded verb ("reduced", supported by evidence) can still carry a fabricated NUMBER ("by 30%") the causal check alone wouldn't catch; every number-bearing token in realized text must appear verbatim in the evidence text or in `profile.resume_facts.real_metrics` (the same ground-truth list the cloud prompt's existing "do NOT change real numbers" hard rule already uses — this closes the equivalent gap for degraded mode, which had no numeric check at all). (8) PASSIVE-VOICE CHECK (`check_passive_voice`) — deliberately a SOFT/logged signal, not a hard reject, given real false-positive risk (a bullet describing a system's prior state as context, e.g. "the legacy system was outdated," is legitimate); primary enforcement is a new prompt constraint (both `format_schema_guidance` and `_REALIZATION_SYSTEM`: "candidate must be the grammatical subject of every bullet"). (9) VIEWPOINT_EMPHASIS — each of the 6 viewpoints now maps to a short emphasis phrase actually rendered in guidance, not just the bare label. (10) `validator.check_agency_inflation` — a companion to the existing `check_claim_inflation`, since the authority-tier narrowing in (4) silently stopped that check from catching people-authority overclaiming; same coarse WARNING-tier, whole-resume pattern, flags a team_lead/director-tier agency verb with no profile evidence earning that agency tier. **Prompt-size discipline**: the expanded `_REALIZATION_SYSTEM` initially pushed the bounded realization prompt to 3719 chars against the existing 3000-char test ceiling (`TestPromptBoundedness`) — trimmed the explanatory prose (kept the two ceiling definitions, cut the worked example) back to 2960 total, preserving the existing bound rather than raising it. **Two regressions caught by the existing test suite** (both fixed before considering this done): the frame-priority reorder above (a pre-existing test asserted "Lead and mentor a team" resolves to `leadership_project`, which the naive append-to-end insertion of `training_mentorship` broke), and the prompt-size regression just described. **A third bug found by manual end-to-end smoke testing** (not by any existing test): `_CLAIM_VERB_PATTERNS`'s "design" and "authority" tiers included the bare 3rd-person-singular `-s` forms `engineers`/`architects` — both are far more often the PLURAL-NOUN job title ("a team of on-call engineers", "collaborated with senior architects") than the rare present-tense verb, so evidence text merely mentioning colleagues by job title was silently earning a "design" or "authority" ceiling it never should have — the most dangerous instance of exactly the vocabulary-gap class of bug #59 already found once (loose stems, e.g. `us\w*` matching "user") and thought it had fully guarded against with explicit form lists; this shows an explicit form list can still collide with a common noun. Fixed by dropping just those two bare `-s` forms (the `-ed`/`-ing` verb forms already cover realistic past-tense/gerund resume phrasing) — `designed/designing`, `engineered/engineering`, `architected/architecting` unaffected. 3 new regression tests. 992+ tests green (full suite) after all three fixes, including 5 new `TestAgencyAxis`-adjacent unit tests for `check_metric_fabrication`/`check_passive_voice`, 5 new `TestCloudPathAgencyInflationWarning` tests, 4 new `TestRequestLocalRealization` integration tests exercising the agency/metric checks end-to-end through the mocked local-model call path, and 3 new noun/verb-collision regression tests. |
| 61 | Markerless-paragraph requirement extraction (Direction 1 of the extraction audit) | 2026-08-25. A dedicated architecture audit (no code changes in that turn) traced three real `NO_SUPPORTED_EVIDENCE` incidents (Axon "Sr. Full Stack Member of Technical Staff", Twilio "Sr AI Architect - Conversational AI", Pinterest "Staff Software Engineer, Observability") to `local_tailor._REQUIREMENT_MARKER_RE` finding ZERO requirement lines — not evidence-matching correctly refusing to fabricate qualifications. Root cause: these postings' itemized requirements were flattened by HTML-to-text conversion into plain, unmarked lines (Greenhouse: single-newline-separated; a live Workday sample: blank-line-separated) with no bullet/numeric marker at all. Live DB measurement found this affected ~20% of the active pipeline (1,960/9,939 rows), 75% of it `workday_api` (the single largest job source). **Fix:** `_split_requirement_lines` now tries marker-based extraction first (unchanged, refactored into `_extract_marker_lines`) and only falls back to a new `_extract_paragraph_lines` when marker extraction finds literally nothing. The fallback splits on every physical line (handles both separator shapes uniformly — a blank line is just an empty string after stripping) and keeps a line only if (a) it structurally looks like one discrete item (`_looks_like_list_item`: length 2-220, no trailing colon, no INTERNAL sentence boundary via `[.!?]\s+[A-Z]` — a marker-equivalent proxy for "one item" since there's no marker to rely on) AND (b) it's part of a CONTIGUOUS RUN of ≥3 such qualifying lines (blank-line gaps tolerated, any rejected line breaks the run) — this run requirement is what separates a genuine unmarked list from an isolated short sentence or section header elsewhere in the posting; an earlier design without it let header noise ("Who we are", "About the job") crowd out the real list entirely on the Axon/Twilio fixtures. Re-measured after the fix: zero-line rows dropped from 2,166 → 81 (96% recovered) live against the database, including the entire stale pre-fix Greenhouse backlog **with no backfill needed** (extraction runs fresh on every read of the stored text, confirmed via `test_stale_row_from_before_the_fix_is_not_retroactively_repaired`). Remaining 81: mostly postings that write requirements as one giant run-on paragraph with no line breaks at all between sentences (a materially different, out-of-scope problem — would need sentence-level splitting) plus a few rows with unescaped HTML entities from a separate, undiagnosed source bug. Explicitly did NOT touch: evidence matching, ambiguous-tier resolution (Direction 2), claim/agency/causal/metric safety checks, or any schemas.py system — this only recovers requirement TEXT that already existed in the source posting. 8 required tests plus real-fixture regressions in `tests/test_local_llm.py::TestParagraphFallbackExtraction`; 2 pre-existing tests that hard-coded the old zero-extraction behavior as their expected result were updated to assert the new, correct recovery (with commentary explaining why, not just a changed number). Full suite green (1028 passed). |
| 62 | Profile-authority hardening: title/skill/seniority/standup fabrication fixes | 2026-08-25. Explicit user correction citing real bad output: an invented "Sr Architect - Emerging Technologies" resume title, UPS's authoritative "Packaging Associate / Warehouse Associate" upgraded to "High-Volume Operations Specialist", and stand-up comedy appearing on technical-role resumes. Root-caused each against the actual prompt/validator code (not assumed): (1) **Title verbatim-copy instruction** — `_build_tailor_prompt`'s TITLE rule literally said "Use the job title verbatim from the posting" with zero grounding check, directly causing the Sr Architect case. (2) **Experience-header titles ungrounded** — `_build_canonical_inventory_block` never rendered `role_title`/`role_type` at all, and 3 profile.json `experience_inventory` entries (UPS, AMP Smart, Alex Prosperity Group) had no `role_title` field, only a coarse `role_type` category tag — the LLM had zero authoritative title to anchor to and invented one from category words ("operations, high-volume work, reliability" → "High-Volume Operations Specialist"). (3) **`validate_factual_anchors` was silently inert** — it pipe-split headers ("Role \| Company"), but the actual JSON contract produces "Title at Company" (no pipe, per `_build_tailor_prompt`'s own OUTPUT schema), so its company check never matched a single real cloud-generated header, and it never checked title at all. (4) **`classify_standup_relevance` contradicted the new instruction** — it treated "help desk"/"technical support" titles as a POSITIVE inclusion signal (prior design rationale: those roles are fundamentally about audience-adapted communication); explicit user correction overrides this for this candidate's resumes specifically. (5) No seniority gate existed anywhere. **Fixes, all profile-driven (not hardcoded to any one candidate):** profile.json gained `role_title` on the 3 missing entries (exact `employment_history` titles) and AMP Smart's stale `end_date: null`/"current work experience" corrected to match its actual `employment_history` end date (2025-07). `_build_canonical_inventory_block` now renders `AUTHORITATIVE TITLE: ...` per experience entry and an explicit `DO NOT LIST AS SKILLS` line for `resume_allowed=false` skills. `_build_tailor_prompt`: TITLE rule rewritten to forbid copying the posting's title or any seniority/professional-identity word not in an AUTHORITATIVE TITLE; new EXPERIENCE TITLES rule hard-requires the exact authoritative title per employer; SKILLS rule's "closely related tools" loophole (the mechanism that let "You Power You" project's "HTML/CSS/JavaScript" get promoted into the general Languages skill list) removed outright. `classify_standup_relevance` gained `_TECHNICAL_EXCLUDE_TITLE_RE` — a hard, UNCONDITIONAL title-based override (checked before any description-level scoring, never outweighed by generic "customer-facing"/"explain technical issues" boilerplate the way the old soft score could be) for the user's exact enumerated category list (software engineering/architecture, IT/help desk/desktop support, sysadmin, infrastructure, cybersecurity, data/ML engineering); 3 pre-existing tests whose fixtures asserted the old contradicted behavior were updated with commentary explaining the deliberate override. New `validator.py` checks, all wired into `validate_json_fields`: `check_title_inflation` (top-level resume headline vs. every authoritative title in the profile — the Sr Architect regression case), `validate_factual_anchors` extended with a per-employer title check (parses BOTH "Title at Company" and legacy "Role \| Company \| Dates", the UPS regression case) plus `_ELEVATED_TITLE_WORDS_RE` (senior/sr/staff/principal/.../architect/engineer/.../consultant — words that imply a step up, never flagging a truthful SUBSET/rewording of the authoritative words), `check_unsupported_technical_skills` (flags `skills_inventory` `resume_allowed=false` items AND project-only `factual_concepts` terms never separately vetted as a skill — the JS/HTML/CSS regression case). All new title/skill checks are ERROR-tier (hard reject), not warnings, per explicit instruction that these be blocking, not advisory; each has a documented early-exit when the profile has no authoritative data to compare against (mirrors `validate_factual_anchors`' existing preserved-companies guard) -- without it, generic test-fixture profiles with no `experience_inventory` at all (many pre-existing, unrelated tests) would have every bare "Engineer"-style placeholder title incorrectly flagged. **New seniority gate**: `classify_seniority_mismatch(job, profile)` — deterministic, zero-LLM-call, checked in `tailor_resume` BEFORE any LLM call at all (`report["status"] = "seniority_mismatch"`, flows through the existing generic `_mark_tailor_result` else-branch unchanged, counted in `run_tailoring`'s stats/log/returned dict alongside the existing degraded-mode statuses). Fires when the job's title/description explicitly demands a senior/staff/principal/architect/director tier (title match, or an explicit "8+ years of professional software engineering/architecture/technical experience" phrase) that NO authoritative title anywhere in the candidate's profile supports — profile-driven, so a candidate whose OWN history later includes a matching senior title is never falsely gated. Direct response to the extraction audit's own finding that the LLM-based scoring gate is occasionally over-generous (Twilio's "Sr AI Architect" posting scored fit_score=8 despite the candidate having no professional ML/architecture history at all) — this is a deterministic backstop specifically for the seniority dimension, not a replacement for scoring. **Cover letters** got the same `ApplyPilot`-leak backstop the resume path already had: `validate_cover_letter` gained an optional `profile` parameter (backward compatible, defaults to None) that reuses the existing `_append_profile_integrity_errors` helper; `generate_cover_letter`'s call site now passes `profile` through; the cover-letter prompt also gained explicit "never mention ApplyPilot" / "never claim an unsupported professional title" instructions as defense-in-depth alongside the new validator check. 33 new tests in `tests/test_profile_authority.py` covering every named regression case by name (Sr Architect, UPS→High-Volume Operations Specialist, Mavis→Technical Support Specialist, AMP Smart→Solutions Consultant) plus the seniority gate, canonical-inventory rendering, and cover-letter leak checks; 3 tests in `test_standup_relevance.py` updated for the deliberate behavior change. Full suite green (1066 passed, up from 1028 at the end of decision #61). |
| 63 | Adversarial review of #62 found and fixed 5 real gaps -- 3 in #62's own new code, 2 pre-existing but newly-exposed | 2026-08-25. Explicit instruction to verify #62 against actual code/tests/real data rather than trust the summary, including inspecting REAL previously-generated resumes and attempting a real tailoring run. Traced the full production call path (`pipeline._run_tailor` → `tailor.run_tailoring` → `_tailor_one_job` → `tailor_resume`, confirmed the ONLY call site anywhere in `src/`) and confirmed `classify_seniority_mismatch` runs before any LLM call, `validate_json_fields`'s new checks are unconditional on every cloud attempt including the final retry, and the judge's "advisory on final attempt" override only ever applies AFTER validation already passed — never to a validation failure. No bypass path exists. **Two real, confirmed false positives introduced by #62's own seniority/standup title regexes** (found via direct regex testing before trusting them): (a) `_SENIOR_TITLE_RE` matched bare "director"/"chief"/"vp" anywhere in a title, wrongly gating "Funeral Director"/"Camp Director"/"Program Director"/"Chief of Staff"/"Store Director" as seniority mismatches — narrowed to require an engineering/technical/software qualifier. (b) `_SENIOR_YEARS_RE`'s trailing-context alternation included bare "technical", matching "8+ years of technical support experience" — exactly the IT-support role class this candidate's own evidence (Mavis, CompTIA A+) supports — removed, kept only "software engineering/engineering/architecture". **Two real, confirmed false NEGATIVES found by testing the fix against the ACTUAL real regression jobs pulled from the live database** (not synthetic fixtures): (c) the director/chief fix over-corrected and broke matching for the exact real Axon posting that produced the original bad output, "Sr. Full Stack Member of Technical Staff" — "staff" now required "engineer/architect/developer/programmer" immediately after it, which "Member of Technical Staff" (a real, established tech-industry senior-tier title convention distinct from "Chief of Staff") doesn't have; added a dedicated `member of (?:technical )?staff` alternative. (d) `_TECHNICAL_EXCLUDE_TITLE_RE` (standup hard-exclude) only matched "software architect"/"solutions architect", never bare "architect" — so BOTH primary named regression titles ("Sr Architect - Emerging Technologies", "Sr AI Architect - Conversational AI") failed to hard-exclude, and with the REAL job description text (not an empty stand-in, which trivially excludes via the separate "no description" rule regardless of title coverage) `classify_standup_relevance` returned INCLUDE for the real Axon job — the exact mechanism that produced "Public Speaker & Performer at Stand-up Comedy" as a fabricated employer entry on that resume. Fixed by adding bare `architect` plus a broader technical-title set (full-stack/frontend/backend/mobile/platform/SRE/"member of staff") the user's "other primarily technical roles" clause covers but wasn't literally enumerated. **A fifth, more architecturally significant gap**, found by directly reading the actual pre-#62 generated resume files on disk (`data/resumes/applypilot/*_b8766a09.txt`, `*_a5c23a14.txt` — real historical output, not hypothetical): "Kubernetes (Exposure)" appeared on both real resumes with ZERO grounding anywhere in the profile (not skills_inventory, not any project's factual_concepts) -- traced to the OLD tailoring prompt's own removed "you MAY add 2-3 closely related tools (Kubernetes if Docker...)" instruction and, independently, `FABRICATION_WATCHLIST`'s own comment explicitly documenting "reasonable stretches (K8s, Terraform, Redis, Kafka etc.) are ALLOWED" -- a DELIBERATE prior design decision (predating this session) that directly contradicts the current, explicit, more conservative policy. Also found: the judge prompt's CONTEXT section listed "Change the title to match the target role" as ALLOWED and its TOLERANCE RULE explicitly told the judge "a closely related tool... is a MINOR STRETCH, not fabrication" / "any LEARNABLE skill... is a MINOR STRETCH" -- inert for content `validate_json_fields` already rejects (judge is never reached on a validation failure), but a real residual gap for anything creative enough to slip past the deterministic checks. Fixed: `FABRICATION_WATCHLIST` expanded with common cloud/infra/ML terms verified absent from the real profile.json (kubernetes, terraform, ansible, jenkins, aws, azure, gcp, postgresql, mysql, mongodb, redis, kafka, spark, hadoop, react, vue, node.js, typescript, graphql, flask, spring, tensorflow, pytorch), comment rewritten to document the policy reversal; judge prompt's CONTEXT item reworded to require title/seniority grounding (with a new numbered fabrication item 8 for unsupported seniority/professional-identity claims anywhere in the resume, not just the title field) and TOLERANCE RULE's "closely related tool"/"LEARNABLE skill" exemptions removed outright. **Real-execution verification** (no code changes, direct evidence): ran the actual unmodified `tailor_resume()` against the 3 real regression jobs (URLs pulled from `job_state_transitions` where `reason='no_supported_evidence'`) with the real profile.json -- all three returned `status="seniority_mismatch"` in under 50ms (no LLM call attempted); the same function against a real eligible "IT Support Technician" job correctly proceeded past the gate and raised `RuntimeError: No LLM provider configured` from `get_stage_client` -- proving the gate genuinely discriminates rather than always short-circuiting. **Environment note**: no LLM API keys are configured anywhere in this environment (`~/.applypilot/.env` doesn't exist, no relevant shell env vars) -- a real LLM-*generating* run was not possible; this was verified directly rather than assumed, and is reported as a stated limitation rather than glossed over. 24 new regression tests added (9 in `test_profile_authority.py`, 6 in `test_standup_relevance.py`, plus the watchlist/judge-prompt tests) reproducing each of the 5 gaps against both synthetic fixtures and, where practical, the real job text pulled from the database. Full suite green (1085 passed, 6 skipped) after all fixes. |
| 64 | `classify_seniority_mismatch` consolidated onto the pre-existing canonical `applypilot.eligibility` predicate | 2026-08-25. Discovered while responding to an unrelated request (a script invoking `applypilot revalidate-seniority`) that `applypilot/eligibility.py` already existed as THE canonical, production-wired seniority-disqualifier module — used by `scorer.py` (pre-LLM scoring gate) and `apply/launcher.py` (apply-time re-check) — built specifically after a 2026-08-21 incident where seniority logic drifted across three independent copies (a stale PayPal "Sr Software Engineer" score reached `ready_to_apply` because nothing re-validated it against a rule added after it was scored). Decision #62/#63's `classify_seniority_mismatch` (added earlier the same day) was an undiscovered FOURTH independent copy, with its own separately-narrowed title regex — exactly the anti-pattern `eligibility.py`'s own docstring warns about.
