Consolidated: `classify_seniority_mismatch` now calls `eligibility.seniority_disqualifier` for title checks (both for the job and for the candidate's-own-history exemption check), dropping the locally-maintained `_SENIOR_TITLE_RE` entirely; `_SENIOR_YEARS_RE` (the description-level "8+ years of professional engineering experience" signal) is kept, since `eligibility.py`'s predicate is deliberately title-only ("purely a function of the title string" per its own docstring) — genuinely new coverage, not a duplicate. This reintroduces the bare "director"/"chief"/"manager"/"head" breadth into `classify_seniority_mismatch` that decision #63 had narrowed away (e.g. "Funeral Director" is "mismatched" again) — checked `tests/test_eligibility_false_positives.py`'s docstring first, which explicitly documents a prior 2026-08-21 audit result: "The canonical seniority guard (applypilot.eligibility) was confirmed clean." Given that explicit, prior, deliberate confirmation, this breadth is accepted as intentional policy (scorer.py's own comment: "always disqualifying regardless of keyword overlap elsewhere") rather than unilaterally re-narrowed a second time — consistency with the rest of the pipeline's already-live behavior was judged more valuable than a locally "smarter" but independently-drifting check. One narrow, unambiguous fix WAS made to the canonical pattern itself (benefits all four consumers, not just tailor.py): added bare `cto` — found missing while testing (the removed duplicate had a `\b(?:cto|chief technology officer|chief architect)\b` case the canonical pattern lacked); unlike director/chief/manager/head, "CTO" has no meaningful non-technical reading, so this is a safe addition, not a repeat of the same policy question. 2 tests in `test_profile_authority.py` (`test_funeral_director_not_mismatched` etc.) updated to reflect the consolidated (broader) behavior, with commentary distinguishing this from the earlier narrowing as a deliberate architectural tradeoff, not a regression. Full suite green (1082 passed, 6 skipped) after consolidation. |
| 65 | `pending_tailor` selection query gained a real `state` filter -- fixes a live archived/tailor_failed infinite oscillation | 2026-08-25. User observed a running `applypilot run tailor --auto-reject-titles` loop re-processing the same handful of senior-titled jobs indefinitely instead of settling. Traced (not assumed) via one real job's full `job_state_transitions` history (`axon/jobs/7815534003`, "Sr. Site Reliability Engineer I"): 5 full cycles of `archived(title_pattern_reject) → tailor_failed(seniority_mismatch) → archived → ...` in ~90 seconds before its `tailor_attempts` budget ran out. Root cause: `database.get_jobs_by_stage`'s `"pending_tailor"` WHERE clause had **no `state` check at all** — only `fit_score`/`tailored_resume_path`/`tailor_attempts`/`eligibility` — so an archived job (for ANY reason: title-reject, revalidate-seniority, non_us_only, ...) stayed selectable forever, since archiving doesn't touch those other columns. Compounding it, `reject_jobs_by_title_patterns`'s `protected_states` set didn't include `tailor_failed`, so a job the seniority gate had just rejected was immediately re-archivable by the next `--auto-reject-titles` pass, closing the loop. Live DB check found 69 archived jobs currently sitting in the `fit_score>=8, untailored` pool the old query would keep re-offering, vs. only 2 genuinely retry-eligible `tailor_failed` rows. **Fix, chosen after tracing the canonical state machine (`VALID_TRANSITIONS` in database.py) rather than patching the specific symptom**: `"archived": frozenset()` is the one true terminal state (zero legal outgoing transitions anywhere) — must never be reachable from this query. `"tailor_failed": frozenset({"tailoring", "tailored", "archived"})` legally transitions back to `"tailoring"` — this is the existing, intentional bounded-retry design (`tailor_attempts < 5`) and must stay selectable. Positive-selected `COALESCE(state, '') IN ('scored', 'tailor_failed')`, matching the exact idiom `"pending_cover"`'s condition already uses two lines below it in the same dict (`COALESCE(state, '') IN ('tailored', 'cover_failed')`) — this inconsistency (one sibling condition already did state-filtering correctly, the other never had) is why the bug existed in the first place. Confirmed empirically zero current rows have `fit_score` set with a NULL `state`, so no legacy-NULL carve-out was added, matching `pending_cover`'s precedent exactly. **`reject_jobs_by_title_patterns`'s `protected_states` investigated, left unchanged** (explicit request: explain, don't auto-change) — adding `tailor_failed` wasn't necessary (the `pending_tailor` fix alone closes the oscillation) and `"tailor_failed" → "archived"` is an explicitly legal, sanctioned transition in the state machine, so a title-reject pass archiving a title-disqualified `tailor_failed` job isn't an architecture violation — arguably it's a MORE correct outcome (short-circuits doomed retries) than waiting out the full attempt budget; reasoning documented in a code comment at the `protected_states` definition. **Regression**: existing `pending_tailor`-related tests across `test_batch_identity.py`/`test_stale_filter.py` seeded jobs via the shared `seed_job` fixture with no `state` override, silently relying on the SQLite column default (`'discovered'`) — which the old (bug) query never checked but the new one does. Fixed via a local fixture override in `test_batch_identity.py` (`seed_job` → `overrides.setdefault("state", "scored")`, mirroring the exact precedent already established in `test_acquire_job_dedup.py` for its own stage) and explicit `state="scored"` added to `test_stale_filter.py`'s call sites. 6 new tests in `tests/test_pending_tailor_state_selection.py`: archived never returned, tailor_failed returned within budget and excluded once exhausted, a genuine scored job still returned, all 20 other non-eligible states individually confirmed excluded, and a realistic mixed pool returning only the two eligible states. Full suite green (1088 passed, up from 1082, 6 skipped). |
| 66 | Sentence-diversity bake-off: cross-encoder wins, wired in as an optional two-tier tier in semantic_match.py | 2026-09-04. Continuation of the 2026-09-03 night's finding that a local-model-generated sentence pool for one thin `experience_inventory` entry (Alex Prosperity Group / UST Logistics) was mostly near-duplicates even after fabrication checks. Built `semantic_match.select_diverse_indices`/`diversity_threshold()` (greedy MMR-style dedup, 0.90 default, empirically grounded against real survivor-set cosine scores) and confirmed the exact predicted gap: same-template/synonym-substituted duplicates (e.g. "customer specifications and company protocols" vs "customer requirements and company procedures") score only ~0.82-0.89 cosine, indistinguishable from genuine variety at that threshold, on BOTH local and cloud (`gemini-3.6-flash`) output -- cloud is not immune, just not yet observed failing this way. Root cause is embedding anisotropy (a real, documented phenomenon: transformer sentence embeddings collapse toward a narrow cone, inflating all pairwise similarities) confirmed via live web search, not assumed. Ran a formal bake-off with a 12-pair hand-labeled ground-truth set (`data/experiments/deterministic_slotfiller_20260902/diversity_groundtruth_pairs.json`) comparing 4 techniques: raw cosine (0.75 acc @ shipped 0.90 threshold; 1.00 @ a since-shown-likely-overfit 0.84), per-batch mean-centering (`center_embeddings`, new function -- no better than raw cosine at its own best threshold, real negative result), local-LLM-as-judge (0.917 acc, ~18s/pair, its one wrong answer was the single pair humans themselves disagreed on), and a `cross-encoder/quora-distilroberta-base` cross-encoder (1.00 acc at its own untrained-on-this-data natural 0.5 threshold, wide real margin 0.82-0.98 vs 0.0-0.42, ~67s one-time load + 0.17s/pair after). **Cross-encoder wired into production-quality code** (not just the experiment): `semantic_match.py` gained `cross_encoder_score()` (lazy singleton loader, degrades to None if unavailable), `is_duplicate_pair()` (two-tier: trust cosine outside a 0.60-0.90 escalation band, only pay for the cross-encoder inside it) and `select_diverse_indices_verified()`. New optional `sentence-transformers` extra in pyproject.toml (`pip install -e ".[cross_encoder]"`, NOT a base dependency) -- real disk cost measured: ~865MB (torch ~545MB + model weights ~318MB). Separately found and fixed: frequency/presence-penalty passthrough added to `llm.py`'s `chat()` (new optional kwargs, additive, tested) to test whether generation-time penalties reduce duplication -- real A/B on the same job found no improvement on the metric that matters (near-duplicate pair count) and one literal exact-duplicate the no-penalty baseline didn't have; not adopted. None of this (bake-off tooling, cross-encoder tier) is wired into the live tailoring pipeline -- still confined to `semantic_match.py`'s new functions and `data/experiments/deterministic_slotfiller_20260902/`. |
| 67 | Real production bugs found and fixed live, plus a test-infra leak that was corrupting real pipeline state | 2026-09-04. In the course of running real `applypilot run score`/`run tailor` batches to sanity-check the pipeline (25 jobs scored, 0 errors; real fallback chain correctly escalated `gemini-3.6-flash` → `gemini-3.5-flash` on a live daily-quota hit): **(a) date-fabrication crash** -- `validator.check_date_fabrication` did `int(start[:4])` unconditionally on `experience_inventory`/`historical_experience_inventory` start/end dates; a real entry ("Lowe's Foods") has `start_date="circa 2016"` (legitimate approximate-date text), crashing the entire tailoring job with `ValueError`. Fixed with a new `_extract_year()` helper reusing the module's own `_YEAR_RE`, degrading to None (skip) rather than crashing on any unparseable string. **(b) 503-fallthrough crash** -- `llm.py`'s `_try_openai_compat` handled 429 with a correct three-way branch (retry while attempts remain / fall through to next chain entry if not last / raise only if truly last), but the 503 branch only ever had the first and third cases -- a persistent 503 on a NON-last fallback-chain entry (confirmed live: `gemini-3.5-flash`, with `gpt-4.1-mini`/`gpt-4.1-nano`/`qwen3:1.7b` still available) crashed the whole tailoring job with an uncaught `httpx.HTTPStatusError` instead of moving to the next provider. Fixed to mirror the 429 branch's structure exactly. **(c) Real, separate account-level finding**: this environment's OpenAI account has $0 credits (`gpt-4.1-mini`/`gpt-4.1-nano` both hit real "insufficient_quota" billing failures, correctly marked exhausted 30 days per the existing 2026-09-01 billing-failure handling) -- not a code bug, but means the real fallback chain right now is Gemini + local only until funded. **(d) Test-infra leak**: `tests/test_local_llm.py::TestDailyExhaustionNotReset` drove a real 429-handling code path without isolating `llm._EXHAUSTION_STATE_PATH` (unlike `test_llm_cascade.py`, which had already hit and fixed this exact class of leak for itself on 2026-09-02) -- writing fake provider names (`cloud-0`/`cloud-1`) into the user's REAL `~/.applypilot/llm_exhaustion_state.json`, which then self-poisoned a later run of the same test. Promoted the isolation fixture from file-local to a suite-wide `autouse` fixture in `tests/conftest.py`; deleted the real polluted file (safe -- code's own documented best-effort-cache contract). **Also corrected**: decision #63's "no LLM API keys configured in this environment" is now stale -- `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` ARE configured, in the repo-root `.env` (loaded by `config.load_env()`'s second, bare `load_dotenv()` call), not `~/.applypilot/.env` (which only has two empty local-LLM vars) -- the earlier claim was a real research gap (only checked one of the two files `load_env()` reads), not a guardrail or a genuine account-state change. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is still genuinely unset. |
| 68 | Evidence-matching precision hardening: 388 → 39 false-positive-inflated matches for one profile entry, plus a discovered gap in the ambiguous-term safety net | 2026-09-04. While building the deterministic selector (#69), a live scan of 1477 real DB jobs against Alex Prosperity Group / UST Logistics evidence found 388 "supported" matches -- implausible for one thin appliance-installation entry. Traced and fixed three distinct real root causes in `local_tailor._item_terms`/`_is_generic_evidence_term`, each verified by re-running the same 1477-job scan after the fix: **(a) missing stopwords** -- `_NAME_TOKEN_STOPWORDS` only ever covered words that happened to appear in early profile text, not ordinary English function words; "throughout" (a bare preposition) single-handedly matched a benefits blurb ("Pay Increases throughout the first year...") to installation evidence. Expanded to a real, standard-sized English stopword list (388 → 248). **(b) generic nouns via IDF** -- "customer" (and "data", "operations", "using", ...) are real words, not stopwords, but appear in such a large fraction of job postings that they carry near-zero discriminating signal alone; reused `idf_weights.json` (a real artifact computed from 30,184 job postings during a PRIOR, separate investigation, `v6_idf_gate_validation_report.md`, which explicitly flagged this exact reuse as a deferred idea after finding IDF didn't work for the different, broader problem it was built for) -- new `_idf_weights()` lazy loader + `_IDF_GENERIC_THRESHOLD = 2.0`, calibrated against real computed values (customer=1.80, technical=1.35, it=1.63 cluster below; troubleshooting=2.48, python=2.68, alignment=3.19 cluster above). Shipped `idf_weights.json` into `src/applypilot/config/` (new `pyproject.toml` artifact glob) rather than leaving it in `data/experiments/`. (248 → 58). **(c) business-entity-name suffixes** -- "Group" (from the employer's own name, "Alex Prosperity **Group**") matched an unrelated "Senior Manager, MLOps Engineering" job purely via that job's own use of "group"; idf=2.90, ABOVE the 2.0 threshold from (b), so a different mechanism was needed -- added "group"/"inc"/"llc"/"corp"/etc. to `_NAME_TOKEN_STOPWORDS` (58 → 39). **(d) Landed but found NOT to reduce the count**: extended the existing `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS`/`_context_senses_agree` mechanism (built 2026-09-04 earlier the same night for "alignment" -- wheel alignment vs. corporate alignment) to cover "install"/"installation" (physical appliance installation vs. software/system installation, the dominant remaining false-positive shape at 39/1477). Verified live: still 39/1477 after landing it, unchanged. **Root cause of why it didn't help, found by direct trace**: `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS` only ever demotes `schemas.py`'s own reported `category_tier`/`exact_keywords` fields (confidence LABELS) -- it does not flip the top-level `supported` flag that `local_tailor._auto_resolve_requirements` sets independently and that real consumers (including the new `build_pool_realization`, #69) actually gate on. This is a REAL, PRE-EXISTING gap, not introduced by tonight's "install" addition -- the original "alignment" case has the exact same limitation and, per this finding, has likely never actually blocked a bad match either, only cosmetically relabeled it. **Not yet fixed** -- would need `_auto_resolve_requirements`/`build_job_schema_representation` to propagate an ambiguous-term rejection back into `supported` itself, a real design decision (could affect the cloud-tailoring prompt path too, since `format_schema_guidance` injects this same `supported`/`exact_keywords` data into the LIVE cloud tailoring prompt, not just the new selector) -- flagged for next session, not attempted. **Also not done**: only Alex Prosperity Group (1 of 6 experience_inventory entries) was audited this way -- the other 5 have not been checked for the same false-positive classes. |
| 69 | Deterministic selector built and proven end-to-end on real jobs -- not wired into the live pipeline, and only has real pool material for one entry | 2026-09-04. Direct response to "what have we actually built toward deterministic tailoring" -- an `applypilot run tailor`-style LLM-off experiment (`data/experiments/deterministic_slotfiller_20260902/llm_off_experiment_20260904.py`) confirmed that turning the LLM off with NOTHING else changed just returns the verbatim master resume for every job, regardless of which requirements the (working, real) schema/evidence-matching layer found supported -- proving the missing piece was never "does matching work" but "does anything ACT on a match." Built `local_tailor.build_pool_realization(job_schema, sentence_pools)`: deterministic (zero LLM calls), reuses `semantic_match.embed_texts`/`cosine_similarity` to rank an already-generated, already-diversity-filtered sentence pool (the #66 pipeline's output) against each schema-supported requirement's own text, and returns the SAME `{"bullets": {...}}` shape `request_local_realization` already returns -- plugs into the existing, unchanged `merge_realization` rather than a new merge path. **Real bug caught only by testing against a REAL job's schema output, not synthetic fixtures**: initial version read `req.get("text")`, but the actual key `build_job_schema_representation` returns is `"requirement"` -- `"text"` was schemas.py's own internal local-variable name during construction, not its output key; unit tests (hand-built with the wrong key) never caught this, only a real end-to-end run did. Fixed in both the code and the tests. **Verified end-to-end, real jobs, real rendering**: two different real DB jobs ("Field Technician", "Early Career Field Service Technician") pulled two DIFFERENT sentences from the same 8-sentence Alex Prosperity Group pool, through the actual production `build_base_resume_model` → `build_pool_realization` → `merge_realization` → `tailor.assemble_resume_text` chain. **Known, real quality gap, not swept under the rug**: one of the two selections ("Set up home appliances from Lowe's" for a "Smart home tech knowledge to support and educate customers" requirement) is a weak match -- true, but doesn't substantively address the requirement; a follow-up bake-off (`selector_cosine_vs_crossencoder_20260904.py`) confirmed the #66 cross-encoder is NOT a fix for this specific step (it's trained for duplicate-question detection, not requirement-relevance ranking, and scored ~0.0 near-uniformly across almost every real candidate pair tested -- wrong tool, confirmed with real data, not assumed). **Scope, to be explicit**: only Alex Prosperity Group has real pool material (1 of 6 experience_inventory entries); every other entry falls back to its original, unmodified bullets when this runs. Not called anywhere in `tailor.py`/the real pipeline -- still a standalone, tested library function plus experiment scripts. |
| 70 | Editor mode built: reword one already-true sentence instead of writing a new one from evidence -- safety net proven live twice, local-model reliability still open | 2026-09-04. Direct response to a real near-miss surfaced by #69's own evidence: asking the local model to WRITE a bullet from Mavis's evidence for an unrelated MLOps job produced "Built and implemented the tracking system... managing lanes, shoes, and other equipment" -- `check_claim_strength` passed (Mavis's own ceiling allows "implementation"-tier language) but `check_agency_strength` correctly caught it (both proposed bullets read as team_lead-tier ownership claims against an individual_contributor ceiling) and would drop both. Real, working safety net -- but a dropped bullet and no bullet produce the identical resume, so the near-miss cost real local-model time for zero net gain. New `local_tailor.edit_sentence_for_requirement()` (one bounded LLM call: reword ONE given sentence toward a requirement's phrasing, explicit hard rule against adding any fact/tool/number/role/authority not already in the input -- an editing task, not a synthesis task, by construction) and `edit_sentence_with_retry()` (bounded retries, re-running the SAME claim/agency/causal/metric checks per attempt, falling back to the ORIGINAL untouched sentence -- guaranteed true, guaranteed non-empty -- if every attempt fails). 8 new unit tests (mocked client), all passing, including a direct regression pin using the exact real near-miss sentence. **Verified live, twice, against the real failing Mavis/MLOps case**: at the default `max_tokens=300`, all 3 attempts returned empty content (same failure class as #67's degraded-mode `max_tokens=600` finding) and correctly fell back to the safe original -- the retry+fallback mechanism worked exactly as designed. At `max_tokens=4096`, a DIFFERENT real attempt timed out entirely after ~370s (multiple 120s per-request timeouts) rather than erroring empty -- a harder case (a very unrelated sentence/requirement pairing) for the model to converge on, not a simple "just raise the budget" fix like #67's realization case turned out to be. **Net state**: the safety guarantee (never lies, never silently does nothing useful without saying so, always returns something) is proven with two different real failure modes. Whether the local model can be tuned (token budget, timeout, or something else) to reliably SUCCEED at the editing task, rather than just safely fail, is unresolved -- not attempted further given session time constraints. Noted but not explored: editor mode doesn't have to be local-only -- it's a small, cheap task a cloud model could also do, which would sidestep the local timing problem for any case where cloud is actually available (editor mode was designed for the degraded/cloud-exhausted path specifically, so this wasn't tested tonight). Not wired into any production code path -- library functions + ad hoc verification scripts only. |
| 71 | Evidence-matching round 2: the `supported`-flag gap closed (narrowly), a real corroboration attempt reverted after breaking real evidence, and the actual fix -- an identity-vs-incidental term distinction | 2026-09-05. Follow-up to #68's still-open gap: a live 31,644-job scan (isolating each of the other 5 experience_inventory entries the same way Alex Prosperity Group was in #68) found `supported` rates of 51-58% against a RANDOM job corpus for Waffle House/AMP Smart/UPS/Mavis -- one coincidental generic word (`quality`, `approach`, `multiple`, `established`, ...) was enough, since `_pair_candidate_evidence` only ever required score > 0, no minimum. **First fix, narrow and safe**: `build_job_schema_representation`'s `supported` flag now actually reads `category_tier` back (previously `entry["supported"] = True` was set unconditionally, before `category_tier` even existed) -- a match reduced to zero real keywords (e.g. "alignment" correctly dropped by `_context_senses_agree`) now correctly means not-supported. **Second fix, attempted then reverted**: also excluding `near_prototype` (exactly 1 matched keyword) from `supported` closes most of the remaining volume, but broke real tests immediately -- a skills_inventory entry literally named "Database" has exactly ONE matchable term BY DESIGN and can never reach 2-keyword "prototype" tier. Reverted same session, root-caused rather than patched around. **Buzzword list, round 1+2**: `_GENERIC_EVIDENCE_TERMS` expanded via real-data whack-a-mole (`quality`, `standards`, `multiple`, `execute`, `conduct`, `approach`, `efficiency`, `established`, `appropriate`, `performed`, etc.), cross-validated against the linguistics "light verb"/"delexical verb" category (do/give/have/make/take/conduct/perform -- verbs that carry almost no meaning of their own) and published resume-buzzword lists (novoresume/monster/livecareer/resumegenius/teal), not just guessed. **Five direct mistakes found and reverted in the same list, by re-reading profile.json's actual `relevance_categories` rather than waiting for more test failures**: `sales` (AMP Smart), `communications` (AMP Smart + Freelance Photography), `content`/`creative` (Freelance Photography), `reliability` (UPS) were ALL real, deliberately-hand-typed category labels, not incidental filler -- caught because a single-word NOUN naming a domain (sales, content, reliability) is exactly the vocabulary a human uses for a category tag, while the words correctly kept (quality, approach, established, efficiently) are verb/adverb/adjective MANNER words nobody would ever type as a category. **The actual, correct fix, built from that distinction**: `local_tailor._item_identity_terms(item)` -- a term is trusted alone (a single keyword suffices for `supported`) only when it comes from the item's own deliberately-curated name/`relevance_categories`/`factual_concepts`, never from a word merely split out of a long `responsibilities` sentence (which still requires the existing corroboration). `rank_profile_evidence`'s wrapper dict gained an `identity_terms` field; `build_job_schema_representation`'s near_prototype exclusion is now provenance-aware instead of a blanket rule -- this is what let `sales`/`communications`/`content`/`creative`/`reliability` go back in safely, protected by being real identity labels rather than needing case-by-case exclusion-list judgment forever. **Validated end-to-end on the real eligible pool** (390 real jobs at `fit_score>=8`, small enough to scan whole, no sampling needed): Waffle House 57.7%→1.5%, Mavis 53.1%→3.3%, Alex Prosperity Group 19.1%→3.6%, AMP Smart 11.0%→13.1% (went UP -- correct, `sales`/`communications` restored), UPS 11.0%→18.2% (went up -- correct, `reliability` restored), Freelance Photography 0.8%→11.0% (went up -- correct, `content`/`creative`/`communications` restored). Higher isn't always worse: the goal was accuracy, not minimization. **Escalating-batch validation methodology** (user-suggested, adopted for the rest of the session and going forward): instead of always re-scanning the full ~31,644-job corpus (~50 min/run, done 3x before this was adopted), start at n=500 per entry, compute a 99%-confidence Wilson-score interval, only escalate to a bigger sample (2,000 / 8,000 / full) for entries still ambiguous against the known before-rate -- got the same statistically solid before/after comparison in under 2 minutes instead of an hour. **Known, deliberately deferred**: Mavis's `troubleshooting`/`hands-on`/`equipment` and Waffle House's `servers` are genuinely ambiguous (mean different things in different contexts, like "alignment"), not generic -- candidates for `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS`' context-agreement check, not this list; not built this session. |
| 72 | Phrase bank ("Stage 0") built and wired into both tailoring paths -- generation moved from "fresh every job" to "generate once, select+edit per job" | 2026-09-05. Direct response to "shouldn't there be an iterator that expands a resume into reusable phrasings before scoring even runs" -- correct: no such stage existed anywhere in `src/applypilot/`, only a one-off, hardcoded-to-one-entry experiment script (`data/experiments/deterministic_slotfiller_20260902/inventory_expansion_pilot_v2_diversity.py`) whose output was never persisted. Four-part build, all covered by new/updated tests (1868 total passing after this + #71, up from ~1814 before): **(1) Multi-bullet shapes**: `build_pool_realization`/`request_local_realization`/`merge_realization` all changed from `dict[str, str]` (one bullet per evidence item) to `dict[str, list[str]]` -- closes a real, separate bug in the same pass: `request_local_realization`'s bullet loop did a plain dict assignment that silently kept only the LAST bullet whenever the model returned more than one for the same evidence item. `build_pool_realization` now scores each pool sentence by its MAX similarity across every requirement citing that evidence (not just the first one resolved) and keeps up to 3 distinct sentences above a `score > 0` relevance floor -- caught and fixed a real index-arithmetic bug here too, where `list.extend()` consuming a generator that read `len(accepted)` live shifted mid-iteration and silently mis-selected survivors after the first. **(2) The bank builder**: new `local_tailor.build_phrase_bank(item, client, profile)` generalizes the experiment script's proven technique (generate variants, reject fabrication-check failures, filter for diversity) to run for ANY item, PER ORIGINAL BULLET rather than treating an entry's whole fact list as one undifferentiated bag -- preserves which bullet a variant restates. New `scoring/phrase_bank.py` module (mirrors `apply/successful_paths.py`'s save/load pattern exactly) persists to `~/.applypilot/phrase_bank/{slug}.json`, staleness detected via a content hash of the item's own source facts (not age -- a bank stays valid forever until the facts it was built from actually change, the correct invalidation trigger for "I updated my resume" or "I handed this to someone else's profile"). New CLI command `applypilot expand-bank [--entry NAME] [--force]`, explicit and run-by-hand per this session's decision (automatic/lazy regeneration deliberately deferred, not built). **(3) Degraded mode wiring**: `select_and_edit_bank_bullets(client, job_schema, profile)` (shared helper, used by both paths) loads whatever banks exist for the job's supported evidence, selects via `build_pool_realization`, polishes each pick with `edit_sentence_with_retry` toward that evidence's specific requirement text. When bank coverage is COMPLETE for every currently-supported requirement, `request_local_realization` -- the one call whose empty-output/timeout unreliability was never fully solved (#67/#70) -- is skipped ENTIRELY; partial or zero coverage falls back to calling it for the full job_schema exactly as before, so this can only ever reduce local-model calls, never add one. **(4) Cloud path wiring**: the same shared selector+editor pipeline also overlays onto the NORMAL cloud tailoring path (user's explicit scope choice over a degraded-mode-only option) -- new `local_tailor.overlay_bank_bullets(entries, bullets_by_evidence)` prepends bank-selected bullets directly onto the cloud JSON's own `{"header","subtitle","bullets"}`-shaped entries (a different key convention than `merge_realization`'s internal `{"title","subtitle","meta","bullets"}` base-resume shape -- confirmed by reading both, not assumed compatible -- so this is a sibling function reading "header" natively, not a shape adapter bolted onto `merge_realization`). Wrapped defensively (any failure anywhere in the phrase-bank pipeline leaves the cloud's own output untouched, never breaks tailoring) and runs before `validate_json_fields`, so the existing safety net checks the FINAL overlaid text regardless of which source a bullet came from. **Scope note, not yet done**: no bank has actually been generated for any real entry yet (needs `applypilot expand-bank` run against local Ollama) -- everything above is built and tested with mocked clients; real end-to-end verification (real bank → real selection → real edited bullet in a real tailored resume) is the natural next step, sequenced deliberately AFTER this land so Ollama generation load doesn't run concurrently with anything else CPU-heavy. |
| 73 | Phrase bank proven end-to-end on a real job; four real bugs found and fixed closing the gap between "#72 is tested" and "#72 actually works" | 2026-09-05, later same night. Direct response to "put 5b into actual use, see how the degraded path does with it in place." Generated a real bank for Mavis (`~/.applypilot/phrase_bank/`) via `applypilot expand-bank`, then ran the real, unmodified `tailor_resume()` against a real Intel "Module Equipment Technician" job resolving to Mavis evidence. First attempts crashed/failed validation; traced each failure to its root cause rather than patching symptoms, fixing four real, previously-undiscovered bugs: **(a)** `build_base_resume_model` had no SUMMARY fallback at all (unlike skills/experience/projects/education, which already had one) -- degraded mode always shipped an empty summary, failing validation every time bank coverage was complete enough to skip the writer. Fixed with a deterministic fallback built from `profile.skills_boundary`. **(b)** that same skills-boundary fallback dumped every category indiscriminately, including one literally named `learning_or_exposure_not_expertise` -- rendering APIs/Docker/JSON/REST as claimable skills against the profile's own explicit non-expertise labeling. Fixed to skip any category with "not_expertise" in its name. **(c)** `_append_profile_integrity_errors`'s unfinished-project check scanned the ENTIRE document for success/completion language, not just each project's own text -- a real UPS bullet using the ordinary word "accuracy" falsely flagged an unrelated, bulletless "Sunburn" project elsewhere in the same resume. First fix attempt (blank-line paragraph splitting) failed against `validate_json_fields`'s actual call shape (`str(data)`, no blank lines); properly fixed with an optional `structured_data` parameter that checks each project's own structured fields directly when available, falling back to paragraph-scoping only for the two genuinely text-only callers (`validate_tailored_resume`/`validate_cover_letter`). **(d)** both judge call sites (cloud path and degraded-mode path) had zero exception handling -- a transient local-model-unavailability during the judge step raised an uncaught `RuntimeError` and crashed an ALREADY-VALIDATED job instead of degrading like every other advisory check in the pipeline. Fixed both to catch any exception and degrade to a `verdict: "ERROR"` result, accepted exactly like a failed judge verdict (never overrides validation, never crashes). **Final confirmed result**: `STATUS: approved`, `bank_covered: True`, `llm_called: False` (the unreliable local writer was correctly skipped entirely), real phrase-bank sentences visibly present in the shipped Mavis resume section. **Also confirmed, exactly as predicted going in**: the local EDITOR (`edit_sentence_for_requirement`, decision #70) is equally unreliable in this environment -- all 9 edit attempts in the same real run failed with "Null/empty content from local/qwen3:1.7b," and the existing safety net (fall back to the original, true, unedited bank sentence) correctly absorbed every one of them with zero visible impact on the final resume. **Also confirmed**: Ollama is running on this machine with `all-minilm` (a dedicated 23M-parameter embedding model, ~46MB, NOT the same as the qwen3:1.7b chat/writer model) already pulled, and is almost certainly what powered this run's embedding-based bullet selection -- `semantic_match.embed_texts`'s Ollama-first, Gemini-fallback design (decision from earlier the same night) means this only becomes a real gap if BOTH Ollama and Gemini's embedding endpoint are unavailable in the same run, which did not happen here. **Reframing this result implies, not yet acted on**: since the deterministic bank+selector path alone (zero LLM calls) produced an approved, well-grounded resume, the local model's necessary role may shrink from "write grounded content from scratch" (the hard task it keeps failing) to "polish the phrasing of an already-selected, already-true sentence" (a much easier task, not yet re-tested on its own). See CLAUDE.md's Future Work list, items 7-8, for the follow-ups this suggests (local-model-as-editor retest; pulling more raw source facts from external sites like GitHub during profile setup) -- both explicitly shelved by the user for a later, dedicated session rather than pursued tonight. Full test suite green after all four fixes. |
| 74 | Editor-as-writer hypothesis confirmed: `/no_think` replaced with the real `think` API field, editor reliability and speed both fixed with real testing, native-Ollama fast path added opt-in | 2026-09-06. Direct response to #73's item 7 ("retest the local model as editor now that the deterministic layer works"). A pre-existing, previously-undiscovered experiment (`data/experiments/specialized_model_prompt_bakeoff_20260901T174855Z/`, 2026-09-01 -- predates #66-73) had already proven the bounded single-sentence-rewrite architecture is safe (zero fabrication across 4 models incl. qwen3:1.7b) using Ollama's NATIVE `/api/chat` with the real `"think": false` API field -- not production's `/no_think` text-prefix convention. Real A/B testing (`data/experiments/editor_reliability_retest_20260906/`) reproduced the exact hypothesis: `edit_sentence_for_requirement` at its old settings (`/no_think` prefix, `max_tokens=300`) failed 0/5 (reproducing #73's live 9/9 failures); `max_tokens=700` alone (still `/no_think`) improved to 4/5 but stayed slow (26-55s); the real `think:false` field via Ollama's OpenAI-compat endpoint (confirmed empirically to be honored on this Ollama version, 0.33.3 -- a documented 2026-08-31 finding on 0.33.2 said neither `think` nor `reasoning_effort` had any effect via that route, so this was verified fresh rather than assumed stale) fixed reliability (3/3, then a control test without the field regressed to 2/4, isolating the field's real effect) but stayed slow; Ollama's NATIVE `/api/chat` with the same field was both reliable (5/5) AND ~10x faster (3-4.5s vs 30-55s) -- confirms the compat shim strips `<think>` from the response rather than skipping its generation. **Two production fixes shipped**: (1) `llm.py`'s `_try_openai_compat` now sends `"think": false` as a real JSON payload field for any qwen entry, replacing the message-scanning/prefix-injection mechanism outright (simpler code, no positional dependency to have bugs in). (2) `edit_sentence_for_requirement`'s default `max_tokens` raised 300 -> 700 (still fails 0/6 at 300 even with `think:false`, per the qwen3 budget-headroom gotcha, CLAUDE.md #7). A real bug in fix (1) was caught by end-to-end testing before shipping: removing the old prefix-injection code left a stale `req_messages` reference in the diagnostic-logging call, crashing (`NameError`) any local call that had ALREADY succeeded -- silently discarding good generations. Fixed (`messages` directly) and re-verified: real `edit_sentence_with_retry` calls against real Mavis bank sentences + real requirement text now succeed reliably (3/3 in the final check, one empty-content response along the way correctly absorbed by the existing retry+fallback safety net). **Native-Ollama fast path (user: "go ahead and pursue it")**: new opt-in `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_OLLAMA_NATIVE` env var (default off) adds `LLMClient._try_ollama_native`, dispatched from `_try_entry` only when `entry.provider == "local"` and the flag is set. Deliberately opt-in, not default-on: `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` is documented (module docstring) as "llama.cpp / Ollama compatible" -- a llama.cpp server has no `/api/chat` route, so defaulting this on would break that configuration for anyone using it. Mirrors `_try_openai_compat`'s retry/timeout/connect-error handling but drops the quota/billing/auth status branches (429/402/400/404) that only apply to hosted providers; any non-timeout/connect HTTP failure (e.g. the opt-in is set but the endpoint isn't really Ollama) falls back to `_try_openai_compat` for that SAME entry rather than raising, so a misconfigured flag degrades gracefully instead of breaking calls. Verified end-to-end on the real `edit_sentence_with_retry` path with the flag enabled: 5/5 succeeded, every one on the first attempt (no retries needed), latency ~2s per call after the first (cold-load) call at 38s. Enabled in this machine's `~/.applypilot/.env` (`APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_OLLAMA_NATIVE=1`) since this environment genuinely runs Ollama. 6 new tests in `tests/test_llm_cascade.py` (`TestOllamaNativeFastPath`): default-off still uses compat (regression guard), opt-in hits the native URL with `/v1` stripped and correct payload shape, empty content correctly falls through to the next chain entry, an HTTP error on the native path falls back to compat for the same entry, non-qwen local models don't get the `think` field forced. 2 pre-existing tests that hardcoded the old `/no_think` text-prefix behavior were rewritten to assert the new `think` field instead (folded into one test since the new mechanism has no message-order dependency to regression-guard separately). 314 passed (up from 309), full targeted suite (`test_llm_cascade.py`/`test_degraded_mode_realization.py`/`test_local_llm.py`) green; full suite not yet run this session (handed off per the user's standing preference). |

Triggered by a naive deterministic-scoring spot-check (see #76) landing suspiciously well on jobs titled "GNC Senior Software Engineer" / "Lead Software Engineer" that a real fit_score=8 called good matches, directly contradicting the current rubric's own explicit seniority rule. Traced to real root cause, not assumed: `git log` on `scorer.py` found commit `035484a` (2026-08-27, "Fix scoring resume source and ethics fallback") -- **before that date, scoring compared every job against a stale, hand-maintained `resume.txt` with a FABRICATED "Senior Software Engineer" identity**, not the real `profile.json` every other stage already used. Live-measured scope: of 6,225 genuinely LLM-judged (non-pre-filtered) scored jobs, 4,893 (78.6%) predate the fix; of 392 real `fit_score>=8` jobs, 356 (91%) are pre-fix/fabricated-identity, only 36 (9%) are trustworthy. **Investigated whether this was already cleaned up** (it mostly was, via a route not originally suspected): `revalidate_seniority --dry-run` and `revalidate_stale_scores --cutoff 2026-08-28 --dry-run` (both pre-existing tools, decision #64) reported 0 matches each -- not broken, just already moot: 357 of the 369 stale-and-still-`fit_score>=8` rows were independently archived by a SEPARATE title-pattern-reject sweep (title alone was enough to catch them, regardless of scoring date), and `revalidate_stale_scores`'s target state set (`{"scored","tailored","tailor_failed","cover_failed"}`, deliberately excludes `"archived"` as a no-op-safe design) correctly has nothing left to do for those. **What WASN'T already caught, found by checking state distribution directly rather than trusting the dry-run 0s**: 2 rows sitting past the title-sweep's reach because they'd progressed further in the pipeline -- (a) `https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/twilio/jobs/7946610` ("Software Engineer (L2)", scored 2026-08-17, `fit_score=8`) has `state='applied'`/`apply_status='applied'` -- **a real application was already submitted** based on the fabricated-identity score; this is literally the same job decision #64's revalidate_stale_scores docstring names as a known-unresolved example from 2026-08-25, still live a day and a half of story-time later. Irreversible -- user's explicit choice was to leave the historical record as-is (no DB mutation), not paper over that it happened. (b) `https://www.simplyhired.com/job/...` ("Software Engineer - AI Trainer", scored 2026-08-21, `fit_score=8`) was stuck in `state='applying'` with `apply_attempts=0`, last touched 2026-08-21 -- confirmed orphaned (no live worker process, `tasklist` clean) rather than genuinely in-flight before touching it. User's explicit choice: archived via `transition_state(..., force=True, reason="stale_score_orphaned_applying_lock: ...")`, not silently released back to `ready_to_apply` (which would have let it re-attempt on the same untrustworthy score). **A second, unrelated, real bug found while auditing enrichment health** ("go over all the data... everything from enrichment up," per user instruction): 88 rows (81 WeWorkRemotely + 7 Intel, out of 32,582 total -- 0.27%) have `full_description` containing generic ad-banner/careers-landing-page boilerplate ("Replace All Your Work Tools... PROMOTED" / "Intel's official careers website...") instead of the real job posting -- a generic-HTML-extraction failure on these two sites, most recently reproduced 2026-09-01 (Intel) so still live in the current scraper, not just historical. Scoped but explicitly NOT fixed this session (small volume, separate root cause from the scoring-staleness thread, no dedicated per-site scraper module found for either site to patch quickly -- likely routes through a generic jobspy/enrichment fetch path that would need its own investigation). Both audit findings excluded from decision #76's clean-dataset tests (`scored_at >= '2026-08-28'` AND `full_description NOT LIKE` both boilerplate patterns) so the scoring-architecture experiments weren't contaminated by either bug. |
| 76 | Deterministic/local scoring fallback: extensive same-night exploration for Gemini-quota-outage resilience -- three failed approaches, then a real, validated architecture (81% gate agreement / 0.83 recall / 0.73 precision at n=58), still NOT wired into production | 2026-09-06. Triggered by a live incident: a real `applypilot run score` batch hit Gemini's daily quota wall mid-run (~4.1h cooldown, since confirmed to reset faster than a full day -- see below), freezing the entire pipeline (nothing downstream can proceed until a job is scored, and scoring has no deterministic fallback at all). Full arc, in order: **(1) Naive keyword-matching fallback -- failed.** Reused `schemas.py`'s literal-keyword requirement/evidence matcher (the same engine tailoring uses) as a scoring proxy: 8% exact match, 59% agreement on the real `>=8` gate against 40 real (job, fit_score) pairs, failing in the worst direction (hid the candidate's own best real matches, e.g. a real 7-Eleven "Multi Skilled Maintenance Technician I" job the LLM correctly scored 9 got 0/8 supported requirements deterministically). Root cause: that matcher is deliberately precision-tuned (decisions #68/#71) for a stricter, different job (never let tailoring fabricate a bullet-to-requirement link) -- wrong bias for a coarser "plausible occupation fit" judgment. **(2) Plan-mode design, frame semantics -- failed, real research consulted first.** Entered formal plan mode (`EnterPlanMode`/3 parallel `Explore` agents/1 `Plan` agent); real research (`WebSearch`) confirmed the standard approach for this problem class is hybrid hard-constraint-plus-semantic-similarity, never either alone. Built two candidate deterministic signals: `schemas.py`'s existing FRAME semantics (`select_frame`, ~14-entry situational ontology, e.g. `diagnostic_support` covers both "brake repair" and "beverage equipment repair" -- the exact generalization the naive matcher lacked) computed on BOTH job-requirement and profile-evidence text (an unused-but-ready combination, `select_frame(_evidence_own_text(item))`). Tested honestly against the real 6,225-job labeled corpus with a stratified train/held-out split: **AUC ~0.50 -- genuinely zero signal**, confirmed by comparing raw distributions (mean coverage-ratio 0.30 for real good-fit jobs vs 0.311 for poor-fit, i.e. statistically indistinguishable), not just a naive accuracy number (a first pass showed 93-94% "gate agreement" that turned out to be 0% recall on the actual positive class -- an artifact of the 93.7% base-rate imbalance, not real signal; this recall/precision-on-the-rare-class discipline was applied to every subsequent test after this). Real, root-caused conclusion: FRAME captures *cognitive activity type* (diagnosing, automating), not *domain/seniority* -- a senior software job and an entry-level repair job can both hit `diagnostic_support`, which is exactly the axis this problem needs and frame was never built to encode. **(3) Embeddings -- also failed on honest (clean) data; the encouraging first number was contamination, not signal.** `semantic_match.embed_texts` (Ollama `all-minilm`) cosine similarity between job title/description and profile evidence: initial AUC ~0.61 looked like real, if weak, signal -- but this is directly downstream of decision #75's fabricated-identity discovery, made WHILE investigating this same result: re-run on the properly cleaned dataset (`scored_at >= 2026-08-28`, n=23 positives / 440 negatives) collapsed to **AUC 0.44-0.51 -- also no real signal**. The original 0.61 was the SAME confound biasing both sides (stale scores favoring generic tech-keyword-heavy postings; embeddings also naturally rating tech-keyword-heavy postings as "similar") -- not independent evidence, an artifact hiding in plain sight until the ground truth itself was audited. Also tested `center_embeddings` (a real, once-before-tested de-anisotropy correction, decision #66) as a variant -- made things WORSE both times (AUC 0.46-0.49), a second, different-problem confirmation that this particular correction doesn't earn its keep in this codebase. **(4) Decomposed "qualify then quantify," per real LLM-as-judge research and the user's own architectural correction -- the winning approach, after two more real failures on the way.** Research (`WebSearch`) confirmed decomposing into narrow criteria + deterministic aggregation is published best practice, and directly explained a live finding: a single real test showed qwen3:1.7b correctly IDENTIFYING a disqualifier in its own words ("does not meet the required seniority level") but failing to APPLY it (scored 6, not 1-2) -- a qualify-vs-quantify failure, not a comprehension failure. First attempt (v1, tier A/B/C/D letter choice) still bundled multiple facts into one compressed judgment -- real bug found via raw-output inspection: **complete mode collapse, every one of 10 real jobs (regardless of real quality) got "TIER: B"** (recall 0.00, worse than doing nothing). A "reasoning-first" fix (forcing one sentence of comparison before the letter) fixed engagement (verified via raw output: the model's reasoning correctly named real, job-specific facts) but not the collapse -- a real, distinct letter-position bias, not fixed by more headroom alone. **User's direct architectural correction, quoted because it's the actual fix**: "why is the LLM still having the final say instead of just filling in check-boxes a deterministic layer can compute" -- v2 still asked the model to blend occupation-family + experience-gap + degree into one choice; the fix is asking only ONE genuinely atomic fact (occupation family, via a single narrow classification prompt) and computing everything else (years-required, degree-required, via regex reusing `tailor.py`'s `_SENIOR_YEARS_RE` as structural precedent) deterministically, then combining via a plain Python lookup table -- the same "regex for what's mechanical, model only for what's genuinely semantic" split `_check_ineligible` already uses one level up, applied one level deeper. **Real iteration at proper scale (n=58 = all 23 real positives + 35 sampled negatives, not the misleadingly-good small n=10 sample)**: v1 (one broad `hands_on_support` family bucket) -- 74% gate agreement, 0.91 recall, 0.62 precision; traced 12 false positives to one exact shape (`family:hands_on_support years:None -> 9` on Mechanical Assembler/QC Lab Technician/Structural Analysis Engineer/etc. -- occupations that sound hands-on but don't share the candidate's specific transferable background). Narrowed to 5 families (`it_or_tech_support`/`hands_on_repair_or_trade`/`customer_facing_or_sales`/`software_engineering`/`specialized_or_other`, the last a deliberately conservative catch-all) -- first pass ALSO over-corrected the sales family's default based on a single anecdote, dropping recall to 0.57 (10 new false negatives, e.g. suppressing real 9-10 "Greeter/Counter Desk Attendant"); reverted that one specific change (evidence-based, not a guess -- the larger run directly showed the anecdote wasn't representative) -- **final: 81% gate agreement, 0.83 recall, 0.73 precision, ~10-25s/call latency (roughly 15x faster than the compound whole-rubric prompt)**. **Escalation to qwen3:8b, tested and found NOT to work as designed**: user's proposed architecture (fast model judges first, escalate to the slow/larger model only when uncertain) implemented via self-consistency (ask qwen3:1.7b the SAME question twice, escalate on disagreement) -- real negative finding: only 1/58 jobs (2%) escalated, because the model's errors on hard cases (e.g. Mechanical Assembler) are **systematic and consistent, not randomly uncertain** -- it gives the same wrong answer both times, so self-consistency literally cannot detect the failure mode that needs catching. qwen3:8b alone, small spot-check: 5/5 correct on the exact hard cases 1.7b got wrong (real signal that more capacity helps) but ~77s/call (5-7x slower); a full n=58 run **crashed after 55 minutes with zero completed jobs** -- confirmed via `tasklist`/RAM checks to be real resource exhaustion after a very long session's cumulative model-loading, not a finding about the model itself. **Gemini-reasoning mining, per user request, done but inconclusive at this sample size**: word-frequency differential over real `score_reasoning` text (n=23 clean high-scoring rows) surfaced *repairs/handling/perfectly/excellent/entry/hand/repair/aptitude/service/equipment/alignment/technician/direct(ly)* as most over-represented -- confirms the atomic-facts table's existing axis (entry-level, hands-on, direct occupation match) is the right one; found no new undiscovered factor, but n=23 is too small to be a confident negative on that question either. **Real backlog/timing findings, addressing live user questions**: 20,134 jobs pending scoring; the clean fresh-rubric pool is only 463 (5.0% score `>=8`, 9.3% `>=7`) -- projecting that rate onto the backlog suggests ~1,000-1,900 jobs would land `>=7/>=8` once scored, assuming similar composition (unverified assumption, flagged as such). Gemini's quota cooldown, checked directly against the real exhaustion-state file, had ALREADY EXPIRED by evening (a ~4.1h window, not a full day) and **6,737 real non-pre-filtered LLM scoring calls succeeded today in total** (mostly earlier in the day, before this session's batch happened to run into whatever quota remained) -- the backlog is a same-day-to-couple-of-days problem at real observed throughput, not the multi-day blocker initially feared. **Explicitly NOT done this session**: nothing wired into production (`score_job`/`scorer.py` untouched; the whole architecture lives in `data/experiments/deterministic_score_fallback_20260906/run_atomic_facts_scorer.py`, tested via ad hoc scripts, not `pytest`/`tests/`); the original plan file's `deterministic_fallback.py` module, `score_method` DB column, explicit CLI command, and embedding-cache infrastructure were never built (the plan predates the pivot away from embeddings to the atomic-facts architecture, superseded but the file is still at `C:\Users\phili\.claude\plans\frolicking-wibbling-starfish.md` for reference); qwen3:8b was never validated at real scale; a smarter escalation trigger (not self-consistency) was never found. |

A real bug caught and fixed before trusting results: the low-score-side ranking took the LEAST-negative z-scores instead of the most-negative (sorted-list slicing bug -- the tell was every result clustering at an implausibly uniform -0.07). One incidental real finding: a `uncertainty/adjustment/claim/pays` word cluster on the poor-fit side traced to `scoring/compensation.py`'s existing `compensation_score_adjustment` (a small ±1/-2 penalty for unstated pay) already firing in real Gemini reasoning text -- confirmed this function was NOT wired into the atomic-facts scorer at all despite being free (pure Python, already tested); folded in when building the production module below. Also discovered live (not fixed, noted): WeWorkRemotely appears to soft-rate-limit/serve degraded generic content under rapid repeated automated requests within a short window -- self-inflicted by tight-loop diagnostic scripts, not a production concern given `scrape_site_batch`'s existing `delay=2.0` between requests, but worth knowing if enrichment ever seems to silently degrade under a much shorter delay. **(4) Atomic-facts scorer promoted to production**, per the user's explicit "we'd need more information... so maybe we figure that out with the research before [building]" -- items 3/1 above supplied that information (8b genuinely works; feature set already covers Gemini's real signal). New `scoring/deterministic_fallback.py` (ported from the experiment script, now with real unit tests, `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py`, 37 tests) plus two new CLI commands, `score-deterministic-fallback` and `revalidate-deterministic-fallback-scores`, and a new `score_method` DB column (`_ALL_COLUMNS`) so a fallback-scored row is visibly distinguishable and revalidation-eligible. `_flush_score_batch` (scorer.py) gained an optional `score_method` parameter (reused, not duplicated, matching this codebase's "one source of truth" convention) rather than reimplementing the archived-guard/eligibility/transition-state logic a second time. Model choice: **defaults to qwen3:8b**, not 1.7b -- this path is only ever explicitly invoked by a human already accepting slower scoring over nothing progressing, so the precision gain (0.86 vs 0.73) is worth the latency given the user's own "slow is better than useless" framing for this exact use case. **A real near-miss caught during verification, not swept under the rug**: a "smoke test" invocation with no `--limit` against the real production DB found **7,041 real jobs genuinely stuck on quota cooldown** -- at qwen3:8b's real ~76s/call, an unbounded run would have taken multiple DAYS, kicked off by what looked like routine post-build verification. Caught via the fallback-chain log line appearing with no further output for 45+ seconds (a real red flag given known latency), confirmed via a direct DB count, and stopped immediately via `TaskStop` before any local-model call completed (zero DB writes occurred -- the function only committed once at the very end of the whole batch at the time, so nothing was lost, but that same all-or-nothing design meant a legitimate long run would have been unsafe to interrupt either). Fixed two ways: (a) `limit` now defaults to a small `DEFAULT_LIMIT = 10` instead of 0/unlimited, requiring an explicit `limit=0` to process everything -- the module's own "explicit invocation only" safety principle applied one level deeper (an explicit call still needs an explicit scope); (b) writes now flush after EVERY job (one `_flush_score_batch` + commit call per job) instead of batching until the end, so a long run shows real-time DB progress and is safely interruptible without losing already-scored work. Re-verified end-to-end after the fix with `--limit 1` against a real quota-cooldown-stuck job (a Chile-based, non-US "Software Engineer" posting): scored correctly (family=software_engineering, the compensation-uncertainty penalty fired), tagged `score_method='deterministic_fallback'`, transitioned to `low_score` correctly; then `revalidate-deterministic-fallback-scores` correctly found and reset it back to `enriched` for a real future LLM re-score, real dry-run/live round-trip both confirmed. **(2) A real escalation trigger, found from data already on hand -- no new model calls needed**: rather than guessing at a new heuristic, directly diffed the REAL per-job results already collected from the 1.7b (n=58) and 8b (n=52) validation runs on their 52 common jobs. Found 13/52 real family-classification disagreements, 6 of which actually flip the `>=8` gate decision; of those 6, qwen3:8b was correct 5 times (1.7b once) -- and critically, the disagreement titles cluster tightly and nameably around "Technician"/"Maintenance"/"Assembler"/"Composites"/"Field Service"/"Embedded"/"Infotainment", exactly the manufacturing/hands-on-adjacent zone this decision's own item (3) already flagged as the scorer's weak spot. A title-keyword trigger built directly from these observed titles (`_AMBIGUOUS_TITLE_RE`) catches 5/6 of the gate-flipping disagreements while only escalating 17/52 (33%) of jobs to the slow model -- hybrid gate agreement 85% vs 1.7b-alone's 79% on this same n=52 sample, closing most of the gap to pure-8b's 87% at roughly 1/3 the latency cost. Shipped as `score_job_deterministic`'s opt-in `escalate_model` parameter (direct substitution when the title matches, not a tiebreak -- matching what was actually validated) and a new `--escalate-model` CLI flag on `score-deterministic-fallback`, deliberately NOT the default: the keyword list was built FROM the exact n=52 sample it's validated against -- the same small-n-overfitting trap this very decision's item (1)'s predecessor already got bitten by once (the sales-rep single-anecdote overcorrection, decision #76) -- needs revalidation on a fresh, independently-sampled batch before being trusted as a default. Self-consistency (decision #76's own prior attempt) remains confirmed non-working; this is a genuinely different, validated signal, not a retry of the same idea. | 78 | Future Work item 8 closed: "troubleshooting"/"equipment"/"hands-on"/"servers" added to `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS`, plus a real second bug found and fixed in the mechanism itself -- ambiguous terms were silently vouching for each other | 2026-09-08. Direct follow-up to decision #71's explicit deferral (documented in `local_tailor.py`'s `_GENERIC_EVIDENCE_TERMS` comment, ~line 946): these four words were flagged as genuinely ambiguous (cross-domain collision, same shape as "alignment"/"installation") rather than generic, but never actually added. Verified the risk was real before touching code, via a real escalating-batch audit (`data/experiments/ambiguous_terms_20260908/audit_candidate_terms.py`, same methodology as decision #71's `scan_escalating.py`: 500->2,000 tiers, 95% CI to decide whether to escalate, 99% CI on the final tier) against the live 31,640-job corpus: Mavis's automotive evidence hit "supported" on 0.75% of a random n=2,000 sample (99% CI [0.39%, 1.44%]) driven SOLELY by some combination of these four words -- real examples spanning RF Engineer, Electrical Engineer, Lab Technician, and IT/networking postings sharing no other vocabulary with automotive repair. **A naive port of the existing "alignment"/"install" pattern (just add the words to `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS`) does NOT work, confirmed by direct testing before shipping**: "equipment" and "troubleshooting" are an ordinary maintenance-vocabulary collocation that co-occurs in BOTH Mavis's genuine evidence and unrelated postings, so once both are in the table, each word's own local context (`_local_context_words`) legitimately contains the OTHER word -- letting two unverified ambiguous terms silently vouch for each other's sense instead of being checked against real, independently-verified shared vocabulary. Concretely: a real "Prin Sys Engr - RF Engineer" posting's "...laboratory and field test equipment" shares only "equipment" with Mavis's evidence for the term "troubleshooting," and "equipment" being a member of the same unverified table isn't real corroboration. **Fix**: `_local_context_words` now also excludes any word that is itself an `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS` member from counting as shared context -- the same conservative "an unproven word is not evidence" philosophy the function already applies to generic terms (the "performance" fix, decision #59-era), just applied to a second, distinct source of false corroboration. Re-ran the identical audit after the fix: 0/2,000 (99% CI [0%, 0.33%]) -- non-overlapping with the 0.75% before-rate, real signal, not noise. Final result after all four rounds: AMP Smart 26.55%->0.70%, Photography 19.75%->0.05%, Alex Prosperity Group 2.65%->1.50% (run-to-run sampling noise around the same true rate, not a regression) -- all measured against fresh random n=2,000 draws each round, never the same sample reused to manufacture an improvement. | 81 | Automatic quota-reset scoring resume scheduled; escalation trigger bootstrap-revalidated using only existing data, finding wide overlapping CIs and one real redundant keyword ("technician") | 2026-09-08, same session as #78-80. User asked three things given Gemini's daily quota re-exhausting mid-session (~23.9h cooldown reported): (1) automate resuming scoring when quota resets so the clean ground-truth pool keeps growing unattended, (2) validate the opt-in escalation trigger from decision #77 and improve it if possible, (3) then run local scoring for a few hours to dent the backlog. **(1) Auto-resume, NOT via the general `run-continuous` scheduler**: investigated `scheduler.run_continuous`/`run_once` first since it looks purpose-built for exactly this, but found a real, would-have-been-live bug in using it here: its backoff (`_next_sleep_seconds`) paces on Claude Code CLI's own auth/quota status (for the Tier-3 apply worker), NOT on Gemini's exhaustion state -- so it would poll the score stage every `poll_interval` (default 60s) regardless of Gemini's real ~23h cooldown. Traced the actual consequence: `scorer.py`'s per-job exponential backoff (5/20/80/320/1440 min, ~28.4h total before `MAX_SCORE_RETRIES=5` gives up) is normally comfortably long enough to survive a 23h outage, but a live DB check found ~25 jobs already sitting overdue on STALE retry timers (some dating back to 2026-08-25, from earlier unrelated quota incidents that were never continuously serviced) -- launching a poll-every-60s scheduler now would immediately re-attempt all of them, and 7 already at their 4th (second-to-last) attempt would very likely hit `MAX_SCORE_RETRIES` and permanently `score_failed` HOURS before Gemini's real reset, losing them rather than rescuing them. **Fix chosen: a precisely-timed one-shot Windows Scheduled Task instead of a polling loop** -- reads the real Gemini reset timestamp directly from `~/.applypilot/llm_exhaustion_state.json` (max across the three currently-exhausted flash-tier entries, 2026-09-09T17:06:29 UTC / 13:06:29 local), adds a 15-minute safety buffer, and registers `ApplyPilot_AutoResumeScoring` (`Register-ScheduledTask`, one-time trigger at 2026-09-09 13:21:29 local) running a new wrapper script (`scripts/auto_resume_scoring.ps1`) that invokes `applypilot run score --limit 5000` (default `--max-age-days 14` funnel policy respected, not overridden -- 8,578 of the 20,026 pending jobs currently fall inside that window) and logs to a timestamped file under `~/.applypilot/logs/`. This completely avoids the premature-retry-exhaustion risk (nothing touches the score stage between now and the scheduled fire time, so every job's own backoff timer stays exactly where it was) while still delivering genuinely automatic resumption. **(2) Escalation trigger validation, using ONLY data already collected -- no new LLM/model calls, no Gemini quota needed** (`data/experiments/ambiguous_terms_20260908/validate_escalation_trigger.py`): reconstructed the exact n=52 overlap between the prior 1.7b (n=58) and 8b (n=52) runs and reproduced decision #77's point estimates exactly (78.8%/86.5%/84.6%), confirming the reconstruction is faithful. Two new findings: (a) a 10,000-resample bootstrap 95% CI on this same n=52 is WIDE and heavily OVERLAPPING across all three configurations (1.7b-alone [67.3%,88.5%], 8b-alone [76.9%,94.2%], hybrid [75.0%,94.2%]) -- the hybrid's apparent edge over 1.7b-alone is not statistically distinguishable from noise at this sample size; the point estimates were accurate all along, just previously reported without any sense of how much to trust them. This is now the QUANTIFIED reason (not a vague "small n" caveat) the trigger stays opt-in rather than becoming the default. (b) A genuinely fresh cut of the SAME data (checking which specific keyword alternative in `_AMBIGUOUS_TITLE_RE` actually earns its firings, not just re-fitting on aggregate accuracy) found "technician" -- the single most frequent alternative, firing on 11/52 titles -- contributes ZERO unique disagreement catches: every real fast/slow disagreement it matches ("Early Career Field Service Technician," "Composites Technician") is ALSO independently matched by a strictly more specific alternative already in the same list. Verified directly by simulation before touching code: removing "technician" drops escalation volume 17/52 -> 13/52 (-23.5% fewer slow-model calls) with IDENTICAL hybrid gate agreement (84.6%, unchanged both before and after). This is a structural redundancy elimination, not a re-fit to this sample's noise -- explicitly safe to make without fresh data because it makes no new claim about what generalizes, only that one pattern's coverage was already a strict subset of the others'. Shipped: `_AMBIGUOUS_TITLE_RE` narrowed to `maintenance|assembler|composites|field service|embedded|infotainment`; 1 new regression test (`test_bare_technician_no_longer_escalates`) plus the CLI help text updated to match. **Noted but deliberately NOT acted on**: "field service"'s own single unique catch ("Early Career Field Service Technician") is a case where escalating actually HURTS -- 1.7b was correct (fast=9, matching real=9) and 8b was wrong (slow=3), one of 8b's own documented `specialized_or_other` blind spots from decision #77 -- left in place since n=1 for this specific pattern is too little to act on in either direction, flagged for the next real revalidation pass. 38/38 tests in `test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py` pass; broader targeted suite (`test_scorer_prefilter.py`/`test_eligibility_false_positives.py`, 152 passed) confirmed no regressions. **(3) Local scoring for backlog progress**: proceeding next, per the user's explicit go-ahead, now that (1) and (2) are done. |
| 82 | Real accuracy spot-check of a live 3-hour local scoring run found a confirmed false-positive (years-requirement stated as a section HEADER, not inline "required" text) affecting ~3% of top-scoring output; fixed and 10 already-scored jobs corrected with zero new LLM calls | 2026-09-08, same session as #78-81. User asked "did it score accurately" after the 3-hour local backlog run (310 jobs, real throughput ~112/hour -- see the review this decision follows). Rather than repeat the historical n=52 validation numbers, did a real stratified manual spot-check (3 jobs per score bucket, 15 total) against the actual job postings scored that night. ~14/15 held up as reasonable (Desktop Support Technician=7, IT Coordinator=9, entry-level Software Development Engineer roles=5 all matched the candidate's real background sensibly); one FULL-TEXT read revealed a confirmed real miss: a Sourcegraph/Tenable "Security Engineer" posting scored 9/10 despite its own text stating "MINIMUM QUALIFICATIONS \n\n Bachelor's degree with 8+ years of hands-on experience with Tenable.io..." -- a hard seniority disqualifier that should have zeroed this out. Root cause, precisely traced: `extract_years_required`'s `_REQUIRED_CONTEXT_RE` only recognizes years-requirements stated INLINE next to the literal words "required"/"must have"/"minimum of" within a 60-char window -- it has no concept of a requirement established by a SECTION HEADER ("Minimum Qualifications", "Required Qualifications", "Basic Qualifications") with the actual number listed separately underneath, an extremely common real-world posting structure. Verified this wasn't a one-off before fixing: a live scan of the same batch's `fit_score>=7` jobs found 4/125 (3.2%) had this exact header+years-mention pattern silently missed -- a meaningful chunk of the highest-value output (jobs that would proceed to tailoring/cover-letter/application). **Fix**: new `_required_section_span()` finds a Minimum/Required/Basic-Qualifications-style header and marks the span from just after it to the next "Preferred/Desired/Nice-to-have"-style header (or a bounded 1500-char window) as an implicit required-context zone -- `extract_years_required` now counts a years-mention if it falls in EITHER that zone OR the original inline-phrase window. Deliberately excludes preferred-only sections: verified directly against a real "Sales Engineer" posting in the same batch that lists "5 years industry experience, preferred" under its OWN "Preferred Qualifications:" header -- correctly still returns None, confirming the fix discriminates rather than just widening the net (this posting was one of the original 4 flagged by a cruder diagnostic check that didn't distinguish which section a years-mention actually fell under; re-checked with the real fix and confirmed it was never actually a bug). 5 new tests in `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py::TestExtractYearsRequired` covering the header-based cases (Minimum/Required/Basic Qualifications, real Tenable text verbatim), the preferred-section exclusion, and a boundary case (a years-mention far past the required section's window must not be swept in just because a qualifications header exists earlier in a long posting). 43/43 tests pass; broader targeted suite (`test_scorer_prefilter.py`/`test_eligibility_false_positives.py`, 157 passed) confirmed no regressions. **Already-scored rows corrected with ZERO new LLM/model calls**: since only the deterministic `extract_years_required` function was buggy (family classification and cs_degree extraction were both already correct, and `deterministic_combine`'s own combination logic is unchanged), wrote a targeted correction script (`data/experiments/ambiguous_terms_20260908/fix_years_required_regression.py`) that re-derives each `deterministic_fallback`-scored row's final score using the ALREADY-STORED family/cs_degree values plus a freshly-recomputed years_required and compensation adjustment -- no local model re-invocation needed, since only one input to an otherwise-unchanged deterministic pipeline had been wrong. Deliberately NOT done via the existing `revalidate_deterministic_fallback_scores` tool, which unconditionally resets EVERY fallback-scored row (all 362) back to pending regardless of whether it was actually affected -- that would have thrown away ~350+ already-correct scores just to fix ~10 bad ones. Dry-run first, then applied: **10 real corrections**, all moving DOWN (never up, confirming the fix only removes false credit, never adds it) -- 4 of them were `fit_score>=7` and would have proceeded to tailoring: Cloud Engineer 9->5, Network Engineer (xAI Battery Storage) 7->3, Security Engineer (the original Tenable regression case) 9->5, Technical Systems Engineer (Hybrid) 9->5; the other 6 were already-low software-engineering-family scores that moved further down (3->1, 4->2, 5->3, etc.) without crossing any decision threshold. Each corrected row's `state` was transitioned consistently with the normal scorer's own `scored`/`low_score` logic and its `score_reasoning` carries a `-- CORRECTED 2026-09-08 (decision #82...)` audit trail showing the prior score/years_required for anyone reviewing history later. |
| 83 | Two more real scoring bugs found by extending #82's spot-check across the full 2026-09-08 batch: clinical-license titles (Physician, Medical Assistant/LPN) mis-family'd as customer_facing_or_sales despite the prompt already forbidding it; CS-degree section-header gap (the sibling of #82's years-required fix) plus a Unicode curly-apostrophe miss | 2026-09-09. Follow-up user request: "look at all the scored jobs from the run, report how well it scored." A fuller spot-check (all 121 fit_score>=7 titles, not just 15) found two NEW bug classes beyond #82's years-header fix. **(1) Clinical titles**: `_FAMILY_SYSTEM` already explicitly says clinical work belongs in `specialized_or_other`, but qwen3:1.7b ignored its own instruction on a Sana "Physician" and an Eagle Family Medicine "Medical Assistant or LPN" posting (both scored 9) -- likely because heavy patient/compassion language reads like customer-service vocabulary to the small model. Fixed with a deterministic override (`_CLINICAL_LICENSE_TITLE_RE`, matching physician/nurse practitioner/RN/LPN/LVN/PA-C/physician assistant/medical assistant/dentist/pharmacist/veterinarian/physical or occupational therapist/psychiatrist) that short-circuits `classify_family` straight to `specialized_or_other` for these titles, skipping the LLM call entirely -- same design precedent as scorer.py's `_TS_SCI_PATTERN`/`_CLEARANCE_REQUIRED_PATTERN`. **(2) CS-degree gap**: a real Cisco "Cloud Engineer" posting states "Minimum Qualifications \n\n Bachelor's degree in computer science, Computer Engineering, or a related technical field" and scored 9 -- `extract_cs_degree_required` never got #82's section-header treatment (only the sibling `extract_years_required` did), so a degree stated under a header with no inline "required" word was missed. the real posting uses a Unicode curly apostrophe ("Bachelor's"), which broke the `\s+degree` boundary immediately after "Bachelor" and silently failed to match even in plain inline-"required" phrasing -- this was a pre-existing bug independent of the section-header gap, now also fixed (`bachelor(?:['’]s)?`). 8 new tests in `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py` (`TestClinicalLicenseTitleOverride`, plus 3 new cases in `TestExtractCsDegreeRequired`) using the real Sana/Eagle/Cisco text;
