the fallback `fit_score>=8` pool for this batch dropped from 59 to 54. **Also investigated but NOT fixed, deliberately**: a Sourcegraph "Security Engineer" (generalist SecOps/Go/SIEM/GCP role) and a Boeing "Senior Communications & Networks Systems Engineer" (title says "Senior" only in the description body, not the scraped title field, and not as a numeric years phrase) both still score 9 with no matching degree/years pattern to correct -- these are genuine `it_or_tech_support`-family-bucket-too-coarse cases, not a mechanical regex miss, and weren't patched with a one-off keyword rule per the standing small-n-overfitting caution (decisions #76/#81); flagged as a real, known limitation of the family-classification prompt rather than papered over. |
| 84 | Fixed real model-switch thrashing in the hybrid escalation scorer (grouped into two passes instead of interleaved); GitHub-pull correction (Money Machine = CAP Predictor, not a new repo); pinned a reputational-review requirement for future GitHub ingestion; Seattle roadmap flagged for future genericization | 2026-09-09, same session as #83. **(1) Escalation thrashing, found from real log timing**: user asked to confirm the hybrid scorer (`score-deterministic-fallback --model qwen3:1.7b --escalate-model qwen3:8b`) processes "one model then the other" rather than switching back and forth per job. It did NOT -- `run_deterministic_fallback_scoring` processed jobs in whatever order the DB query returned them, and `score_job_deterministic` decides per-job whether to escalate, so an ambiguous-title job could land anywhere in the sequence. Decoded the real 2026-09-08 log (`manual_backlog_scoring_20260908_183028.log`, UTF-16) and computed per-call deltas: escalated (qwen3:8b) calls cost 139-420s EACH, recurring throughout the run rather than warming up once and settling into the ~76s/call steady state a dedicated, non-interleaved qwen3:8b run shows (decision #77) -- and the calls immediately after an escalation were also elevated (e.g. a "fast" call right after an escalated one took 31-74s vs. the normal 5-15s baseline). This is consistent with Ollama evicting/reloading a model every time the requested model differs from what's currently loaded on this machine's constrained RAM, not a one-time cold-start cost. **Fix**: `run_deterministic_fallback_scoring` now partitions `jobs` into two groups by the same `_AMBIGUOUS_TITLE_RE` check `score_job_deterministic` already uses, and processes ALL `model` jobs before ANY `escalate_model` job -- the local model now switches at most once per run instead of potentially once per escalated title scattered through the batch. Per-job DB flush/interruptibility (the 2026-09-07 near-miss safety property) is unchanged; only the processing ORDER changed. 2 new tests in `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py` (`test_fast_and_escalate_jobs_are_grouped_not_interleaved` -- seeds jobs interleaved, asserts every `model` call happens before every `escalate_model` call regardless of DB order; `test_no_escalate_model_processes_in_original_order` -- confirms no behavior change when escalation isn't used). 53/53 pass. **(2) GitHub-pull correction**: the user's own memory corrected a finding from this session's earlier GitHub exploration -- "Money Machine" (name-dropped in the `PhiliptheGinger.github.io` portfolio README as "a Python project for analyzing financial market trends," with no discoverable standalone repo) is NOT a new/missing project; it's the informal/marketing name for the ALREADY-catalogued "CAP Predictor" (`cap-predictor` repo, already in `profile.json`'s `project_inventory`, described there as bridging expert-knowledge and public access to prosperity-related predictions -- consistent with a "financial market trends" framing). No new evidence to add; retracts that specific lead from the earlier exploration. **(3) Reputational-review requirement, pinned per explicit request ("let's put a pin in this")**: before any future GitHub-repo-derived evidence is added to `profile.json`, the user wants each repo double-checked for recruiter-visibility risk -- explicitly named examples: a repo that scrapes resumes/automates job applications (like ApplyPilot itself), anything "semantically vulgar," or anything "pragmatically against authority" (e.g. piracy/circumvention tooling). Flags from both sources are unioned, never intersected -- either source alone is enough to surface a concern. **Sparse-repo filter** (`is_sparse_repo`) generalizes the real `greensboro-data-coop` precedent from decision #84's exploration (README < 200 chars, or an explicit stub marker like "initial draft"/"work in progress"/"placeholder" in the first 500 chars) into a reusable rule. **Review loop** (`review_repos_interactively`): every repo gets an explicit `Confirm.ask`, no repo is ever silently auto-included or auto-excluded -- but the *default* answer is context-sensitive (`True` only for a repo that's neither sparse nor flagged), so a clean repo is fast to accept (Enter) and a risky one requires a deliberate override, without ever removing the user's final say either direction (a flagged repo the user explicitly confirms IS kept, not silently dropped -- verified by test). **Draft entry builder** (`build_draft_project_entry`) is deliberately simple/deterministic, not an LLM extraction pass -- name + description + top languages + a GitHub-Pages note when applicable + a standing generic overclaim-guard constraint -- explicitly scoped as "a starting point for the user to review/edit," not a finished, schema-quality entry (the harder categorization-quality problem stays open per Future Work item 10b). **Wiring**: a new opt-in "Step 7" in `wizard/init.py`'s `run_wizard()` (off by default, since it makes network + LLM calls) for first-time setup, AND a standalone `applypilot import-github <username>` Typer command for a user (like this session's default), and the review loop's default-vs-override behavior for clean/sparse/flagged repos including the "user overrides the default" cases in both directions. | 86 | Real Gemini-vs-local comparison run (30 of the 362 jobs) found a persistent-503 exhaustion gap (fixed) and three compounding real bugs in `extract_years_required` affecting EVERY occupation family, not just software engineering -- 37 more already-scored rows corrected, all moving down | 2026-09-09, same session as #83-85. Direct follow-up to "let's see how it compares to what we got from the local model yesterday" -- rather than run the full 362 blind against a Gemini service showing real instability, ran a bounded 30-job slice first (an autonomous-loop tick's own call, given the user's unanswered three-way question at the time; reversible, no DB writes, directly continued the already-authorized comparison). **(1) 503-exhaustion gap, found immediately**: `gemini-3.6-flash` hit its real daily quota within the first 2 calls; `gemini-3.5-flash` and `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` both showed persistent, unpredictable 503s (33-262s per job in earlier single-job testing) that were NEVER remembered between calls -- unlike 429/rate_limit handling, a persistent 503 fell through to the next model for that one call but left nothing marked, so every subsequent job in the same batch re-paid the full multi-retry cost against the same currently-dead model from scratch. Fixed: `llm.py`'s 503 fall-through branch now calls `self._mark_exhausted(entry.name, time.time() + 60)`, mirroring the existing transient-`rate_limit` case's own 60s mark exactly. 1 new regression test in `test_llm_cascade.py`; 82/82 pass. **(2) Three real, compounding bugs in `extract_years_required`, found from the comparison data itself**: of 30 jobs, 27 got a real Gemini score and REASONING text; the reasoning routinely quoted an explicit, real stated years-requirement from the SAME posting that the local extractor had returned `None` for, on 19 of those 27 -- not a software-engineering-specific issue, every family the deterministic fallback scorer covers was affected. Root-caused against real posting text (not guessed) to three distinct gaps: **(a)** a spelled-out number repeated parenthetically -- "at least eight (8) years of professional experience" (Clear Street) -- broke the digit-then-"years" adjacency `_YEARS_MENTION_RE` required (")" sat in between); fixed with an optional "word (" prefix / ")" suffix around the captured digit. **(b)** "N+ years" with no "experience" word anywhere nearby -- "5+ years working on complex systems" (Cash App) -- the old regex hard-required "...experience" in the trailing window regardless of a "+"; fixed by adding a `+`-only alternative (verified this doesn't reopen the PTO-style false-positive class the "experience" filter existed to avoid -- a bare "N years" with no "+" still requires it, unchanged). **(c)** real requirement-section headers other than "Minimum/Required/Basic Qualifications" -- "Requirements:" (Clear Street), "About You:" (Vercel), "You Have" with no colon at all (Cash App), and a bare "Qualifications" with no prefix or colon (a BuiltIn front-end role) -- none matched `_REQUIRED_SECTION_HEADER_RE`. Fixed by switching the header check from an unanchored phrase search to a whole-line match (`^...$` with MULTILINE) covering all five header shapes -- deliberately NOT colon-anchored (real postings are inconsistent about the colon) and deliberately requiring the ENTIRE line rather than just the bare word, verified against the two realistic false-positive shapes this would otherwise risk ("there are no formal requirements for this role" / "system requirements: 8GB RAM" -- both correctly still return no match, since neither is the sole content of its own line). **A fourth, separate real bug found while fixing (c)**: one real case (a BuiltIn "Software Engineer, Front-End" posting) still failed even with headers fixed -- its bare "Qualifications" header sits at character 3196 of a 6208-char description (a long company-intro/responsibilities section precedes it), past the 3000-char window every extraction function in this module used. Bumped to a new `DESCRIPTION_WINDOW = 6000` module constant (three call sites updated) to match `scorer.py`'s own real LLM-scoring-prompt window, which had never been aligned with this module's. 9 new tests in `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py::TestExtractYearsRequired` covering all three real regression cases plus the two false-positive guards and the window-size fix; 62/62 pass in that file, 258/259 (1 pre-existing skip) across the broader targeted suite. **Corrected already-scored rows with zero new LLM calls** (`data/experiments/gemini_vs_local_20260908_comparison/fix_years_extraction_20260909.py`, same re-derive-from-stored-inputs pattern as decisions #82/#83's scripts): **37 real corrections, all moving DOWN**, spanning every family the scorer covers, not just software_engineering -- several `it_or_tech_support`/`customer_facing_or_sales` rows dropped straight from 9 to 5 (Site Reliability Engineer x2, Systems Administrator x2, Network Engineer x2, Desktop Support Engineer, Customer Support Specialist x2, Maintenance Technician). The fallback `fit_score>=8` pool for the original 362-job batch dropped from 54 (after decisions #82/#83's earlier corrections) to 41. **A real bug in the correction SCRIPT itself, caught before applying**: the script's reasoning-text parser doesn't distinguish `score_job_deterministic`'s pre-filter ineligibility short-circuit (a bare `"Ineligible: ..."` reasoning string, which never calls `classify_family`/`extract_years_required` at all) from a real family-classified row -- one "Full Stack Software Engineer" row (rejected for an ethical-exclusion keyword match, correctly scored 2) would have been wrongly "corrected" upward to 5 by treating its blank family/years fields as `family=None` (deterministic_combine's neutral branch) rather than recognizing it was never classified in the first place. Fixed by explicitly skipping any row whose reasoning starts with `"Ineligible"` before the dry-run was ever applied. **Not done this session**: the fuller 332-job remainder of the original 362-job batch was never re-scored by Gemini (the bounded 30-job slice was deliberate, given the service instability found); Gemini REASONING/KEYWORDS distillation at this larger n and the escalation-trigger revalidation (decision #81's blocker) are both still open, now with 27 fresh, real (if family-skewed toward software_engineering) data points banked in `results.json` for whenever that's picked up. |
| 87 | Follow-up to #86, same day: generalized `extract_years_required` away from a verb-inventory dependency per explicit user request, added typo/format tolerance, and fixed a real range-extraction bug (upper bound taken instead of lower) plus a real false-negative the generalization itself introduced | 2026-09-09, same session as #86. User pushed back on #86's "+"-only relaxation: "I wonder if there's a broader way to catch 'working on...' without just creating an inventory of words." Correct instinct -- redesigned to DECOUPLE mention-detection (now maximally permissive: any "N(+)? years/yrs", no trailing-context word needed) from qualification (unchanged: only a recognized required-section header or inline required/must-have/minimum-of phrase counts), so "5+ years working on X" now qualifies purely via section-header anchoring, with no verb list at all -- "working on" was never special-cased, it's just an example of the infinite phrasings this no longer needs to enumerate. **Real regression caught before shipping**: a naive first pass added `has_experience`/`has_plus` as INDEPENDENT qualifying conditions, which broke the pre-existing "preferred" exclusion (`test_preferred_years_not_counted`) -- "3 years of experience preferred" would have wrongly qualified since "experience" is present regardless of "preferred." Fixed by keeping the qualifying logic exactly as before; only detection broadened. **The real false-positive class this broadening opens**: a bare "N years" mention that has nothing to do with experience (license renewal cadence, warranty periods, tenure/anniversary benefits) sitting inside an otherwise-recognized required section. Per the same "no word inventory" instruction, closed with a SHORT NEGATIVE list instead (renew/valid-for/expire/warranty/tenure/anniversary/PTO) rather than trying to positively enumerate every experience-verb -- scoped to the mention's OWN LINE (not a flat character radius, which a real two-bullet test showed can wrongly bleed context from an adjacent unrelated bullet). **A real false-NEGATIVE this same negative list caused, caught before shipping**: the first version included bare `licen[cs]\w*`/`certificat\w*` as triggers; a real Boeing "Machine Repair Mechanic" requirement -- "1+ years of related experience OR A COMPLETED TECHNICAL CERTIFICATE / post-secondary degree" -- uses "certificate" as an ALTERNATIVE-credential noun in the same bullet as the real years requirement, and got wrongly excluded. Fixed by keying the negative list on the RENEWAL/VALIDITY-PERIOD framing only (verbs/phrases: renew, valid for, expire, warranty, tenure, anniversary, PTO), dropping the bare credential nouns entirely. **Typo/format tolerance added per explicit request**: "yrs" abbreviation, hyphenated "5-years" separator. **A separate real bug found while re-verifying against the full batch, not previously suspected**: an explicit range -- "Entry Level / 1 – 3 years in the role" (real Truist "Wealth Support Specialist I" posting, en dash) -- was extracting the UPPER bound (3) because the single-number alternative's match attempt at the first digit fails (the range separator isn't "years") and re-matches starting at the second digit instead. This systematically under-credits a candidate who meets the range's actual floor, inconsistent with the function's own "smallest qualifying number" design intent. Fixed with a dedicated range alternative (tried first) capturing the LOWER bound, handling hyphen/en-dash/em-dash separators. **Cutoff removed entirely, not just raised again**: user asked "should we have a character cutoff... it may make more sense to keep description(s) even after they cut off" -- correct observation that these two functions are pure regex, not LLM calls, so there's no token-cost reason to bound them at all (unlike `classify_family`'s real LLM prompt, which stays at `DESCRIPTION_WINDOW=6000` for genuine cost reasons). Removed the character limit from `extract_years_required`/`extract_cs_degree_required` entirely rather than raising it again or building a chunked-continuation mechanism (which would only make sense for the LLM-facing call, and wasn't attempted -- no evidence classify_family needs it, since occupation-family signal is almost always evident early in a posting, unlike years-requirements which cluster later). 12 new/revised tests in `tests/test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py::TestExtractYearsRequired` (bare-years-under-header without any verb list, yrs abbreviation, hyphenated separator, license-renewal exclusion, own-line scoping, the alternative-credential-noun false-negative regression, both range cases, no-length-limit proof past 6000 chars); 70/70 pass in that file, 184/185 (1 pre-existing skip) across the broader targeted suite. **Corrected 7 more already-scored rows** (re-running the same #86 correction script, which skips already-`CORRECTED` rows): all moving down except one (`Wealth Support Specialist I` corrected twice in sequence as successive fixes landed -- 8→4 with the first, wrong "upper bound 3" extraction, then 8→6 once the range fix gave the correct floor of 1 -- both real, both applied in order, final state is the accurate one). Fallback `fit_score>=8` pool: 41→40. Total corrected across all of decisions #82/#83/#86/#87: 60 rows. |

All the real disagreement clustered on postings with NO explicit requirement stated -- Claude gave more benefit-of-the-doubt to the absence of a number; Gemini consistently scored lower by weighting IMPLICIT signals the written rubric never mentions at all: (a) numbered job-title levels ("Software Engineer II" read as inherently non-entry regardless of stated requirements), (b) qualitative seniority language ("track record of building in production," "architecting enterprise-level applications") treated as functionally equivalent to a hard experience gate, (c) explicit competitive-market reasoning (a real Notion "Software Engineer, Early Career" posting stating "0-2 YOE, not a new-grad role" -- textbook rubric 7-8 by the letter of the criteria -- got Gemini's LOWEST-confidence score of the batch, 3, reasoning the candidate is "non-competitive against the likely applicant pool of CS graduates and interns," a consideration with no basis in the written rubric at all). None of these three signals exist anywhere in `SCORE_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` or `deterministic_combine`'s lookup table today -- a real, previously-invisible gap in what the rubric and the scorer are told to weigh, found only by having a second independent reasoner apply the SAME explicit rubric and diverge anyway. **A possible Gemini reliability concern, not just a scoring disagreement**: for a Mastercard "Software Engineer II" (Remote Payment team) posting with no explicit years/degree number anywhere in its own text, Gemini's reasoning stated "typically requires 2-5 years of professional software engineering experience" -- that exact phrase is the literal wording of a DIFFERENT, superficially similar Mastercard "Software Engineer II - Decision Management" posting in the SAME batch, which genuinely does say "2-5 years." Looks like cross-posting contamination in Gemini's reasoning text, not something grounded in the actual posting given to it -- worth remembering before ever treating Gemini's REASONING field as unconditionally trustworthy ground truth for future distillation work. **A separate, real, non-hallucination-related gap surfaced independently**: `deterministic_combine`'s `software_engineering` branch maps ANY `years >= 1` to the same base score (3), never distinguishing "1 year required" from "10+ years required" the way the `it_or_tech_support`/`hands_on_repair_or_trade`/`customer_facing_or_sales` branches already do (`years==1` -> 7, `years>=2` -> 5) -- a real coarseness gap in the lookup table, independent of anything found this session about extraction itself, not yet fixed. **Also newly noticed, not yet fixed**: a decimal years mention ("1.5+ years," a real Affirm posting) isn't matched by the current integer-only `\d{1,2}` pattern -- a minor, single-observed-instance gap. **Not done**: no DB writes (this was a pure research/comparison exercise, mirroring decision #86's non-destructive approach); no code changes from any of these findings yet -- all four (implicit-signal gap, Gemini-reliability caution, lookup-table coarseness, decimal-years gap) are flagged for a future session, not fixed this one. |

| 90 | Larger, more diverse (5-family) batch scored directly by Claude found the most severe bug of this whole multi-day effort -- non-US/foreign-language postings scoring 7-9 -- plus 8 more real extraction gaps, one of them a real false positive introduced while fixing another; ~38 more rows corrected, fallback `fit_score>=8` pool dropped 40->27 | 2026-09-09, same session as #86-89. Direct follow-up to "run a larger batch to see if we notice anything more" -- deliberately pulled 40 jobs stratified across all 5 occupation families (8 each: software_engineering/specialized_or_other/it_or_tech_support/customer_facing_or_sales/hands_on_repair_or_trade) from the untouched remainder of the 362-job batch, since decision #88's 27-job sample was entirely software_engineering, a real blind spot. Scored the first 32 directly (same method as #88: real rubric, real profile, no Gemini this time given its known instability). **By far the most severe finding**: FOUR real Accenture/Salesforce postings (Madrid, Buenos Aires, Brussels, Warsaw) scored 7-9 despite being non-US roles -- one entirely in Spanish. Root-caused: `_check_ineligible`'s description-language patterns (shared by both the real Gemini scorer and the deterministic fallback) only recognize specific ENGLISH phrasings ("based in Spain," "right to work in the UK") -- never the posting's own text actually being written in a non-English language, and the `location` DB field was blank for all four real cases so the separate location-pattern check had nothing to check either. This is categorically worse than a fit-degree miscalibration (the earlier extraction bugs) -- these are jobs the candidate literally cannot take. Fixed with four specific, confirmed-real signals added to `scorer._INELIGIBLE_DESC_PATTERNS` (benefits BOTH scoring paths, not just the fallback): Accenture's own recurring Spanish EEO boilerplate ("Declaración de igualdad de oportunidades..."), the generic Spanish phrase "años de experiencia," the literal phrase "Polish law" (a real Warsaw posting's own EEO clause), and a "fluency in <language> is required" pattern (a real Brussels posting requiring French/Dutch). 5 new tests in `test_scorer_prefilter.py` including a false-positive guard (an ordinary US posting merely listing Spanish as a nice-to-have skill must NOT trigger). **Eight more real extraction gaps found from the same batch, all confirmed against real posting text**: (a) spelled-out number words -- "Two years"/"One year," a real, RECURRING Truist template across multiple branch postings, no digit at all so no numeric pattern could ever match; added a `_NUMBER_WORDS` map (one-twelve) as a third `_YEARS_MENTION_RE` alternative. (b) "N or more years" (Boeing, "9 or more years") as a natural-English equivalent of "+" that the old pattern never recognized. (c) Boeing's own recurring template, "Basic Qualifications (Required Skills/Experience):" -- appeared in multiple real Boeing postings and never matched since the header pattern required NOTHING else on the line; now allows an optional parenthetical annotation. (d) three more header variants: "What We're Looking For" (distinct from the already-handled bare "What We Look For"), "What You'll Bring," "What We Require," "Typically requires:". (e) "minimum N years" with no "of" (RTX, "minimum 5 years") -- but a first attempt (broadening the shared `_REQUIRED_CONTEXT_RE` to bare "minimum") caused a REAL FALSE POSITIVE caught before shipping: a Sherwin-Williams posting's "Minimum Requirements:" SECTION HEADER (an unrelated line) sat within the wide 60-char symmetric window of a completely unrelated "eighteen (18) years of age" mention and wrongly vouched for it as an experience requirement. Fixed properly with a SEPARATE, tightly proximity-scoped `_MINIMUM_PREFIX_RE` (requires "minimum" immediately before the number, not just present anywhere in a wide window) plus an independent second safety net (added "of age" to the negative-exclusion list, so an age requirement can never count regardless of proximity coincidences). The single most severe individual case fixed this round: Boeing's "Systems Engineer (Comm & Networks Sys Engrg)" -- explicitly a Senior/multi-tier role requiring 3-14+ years depending on level -- previously scored 9/10 (the maximum) because NEITHER the compound-header fix nor the "or more" fix existed yet; now correctly scores 5. 13 new/revised tests; 82/82 pass in `test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py`, 309/310 (1 pre-existing skip) across the full targeted suite. **Corrected rows via three separate passes** (years-extraction re-run twice -- once before the age/minimum-proximity false-positive was caught and fixed, once after, to avoid shipping that regression -- plus a new `fix_non_us_20260909.py` for the description-language fix, since the existing correction scripts only re-derive `deterministic_combine`'s output, never re-run `_check_ineligible`): 32 years-extraction corrections + 7 non-US corrections applied (2 of the non-US ones moved slightly UP, 1->2 -- both already firmly in reject territory, just normalizing to the standard ineligible-tag score, not a real regression). Fallback `fit_score>=8` pool: 40 -> 27, the largest single drop of this entire multi-session effort. Total corrected across all of decisions #82/#83/#86/#87/#89/#90: 104 rows. **Not done**: only 32 of the 40 selected jobs were actually read/scored (a representative, sufficient sample given how quickly the pattern became clear); the remaining 8 weren't reviewed. |
| 91 | Cross-section bleed-through in `extract_years_required`'s inline-context window: a "Required Equipment" header on the NEXT line wrongly vouched for an unrelated years-mention one line above it -- fixed by bounding the trailing side of the window to the mention's own line | 2026-09-09, same session as #86-90. Found while reviewing a real SimplyHired "Now Hiring IT and Telecom Field Technicians" posting (one of the remaining hands_on_repair_or_trade jobs from decision #90's 40-job batch): its text reads "...5+ years verifiable field experience...\nRequired Equipment & Qualifications" -- the word "Required" starting a DIFFERENT, unrelated section on the NEXT line sat inside the flat `m.end()+60`-char trailing window `_REQUIRED_CONTEXT_RE` checks, wrongly vouching for the "5+ years" mention even though that mention has no real qualifying context of its own. The posting's actual relevant fact -- a separate "1 year" mention satisfiable via the candidate's real CompTIA A+ certification -- got no credit either way, so the bug wasn't just a false positive, it was crediting the WRONG mention. Root cause: the window's trailing bound was a flat character count with no awareness of line boundaries, unlike the mechanism's leading bound and unlike the already-existing `_NON_EXPERIENCE_YEARS_CONTEXT_RE` check (decision #87), which already scopes to the mention's own line for exactly this reason -- an inconsistency between two related checks in the same function, not a new design gap. **Fix**: `extract_years_required`'s window construction changed from `text[max(0, m.start()-60) : m.end()+60]` to `text[max(0, m.start()-60) : min(m.end()+60, le)]`, where `le` is the end of the mention's own line (from the existing `_line_span` helper). Verified against the real posting before shipping: `extract_years_required` now correctly returns `None` instead of `5` (the "1 year" mention doesn't independently qualify either, since it has no required-context phrase or header of its own in this particular posting -- a real, if modest, information gap in the posting itself, not a scorer bug). 2 new regression tests (`test_inline_required_context_does_not_bleed_from_next_line_header`, same-line trailing "required" still counts as a non-regression check) confirm same-line and earlier-line required-context still work exactly as before; only next-line bleed is excluded. 84/84 pass in `test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py`; 203 passed, 1 skipped across the broader targeted suite (`test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py`/`test_scorer_prefilter.py`/`test_eligibility_false_positives.py`). **Corrected**: re-ran `fix_years_extraction_20260909.py` -- 1 real correction, the SimplyHired posting itself, moving UP from 5 to 9 (years 5 -> None) -- the only row in the whole 362-job batch affected by this specific bug shape. Fallback `fit_score>=8` pool: 27 -> 28. Total corrected across decisions #82/#83/#86/#87/#89/#90/#91: 105 rows. |

Fixed by adding optional `\*{0,2}` on both sides of the header phrase -- still a strict whole-line match otherwise, so it can't match a header phrase merely mentioned mid-sentence for emphasis. **(2) Two real false positives this fix immediately exposed, caught by verification before applying corrections rather than after**: re-running the correction script surfaced two suspiciously large extracted values (30 and 18) that turned out to be genuinely wrong, both only reachable now that Markdown-wrapped headers newly matched. (a) A real Rooms To Go "Furniture Service Tech" posting has no "Preferred Qualifications"-style closing header, so `_required_section_span`'s 1500-char fallback window swept in an unrelated "**About Rooms To Go**" company-history blurb ("Founded in 1991... Fixed with a new whole-line-anchored `_ABOUT_COMPANY_HEADER_RE` (same discipline as the required-header regex) that now also ends the required-context span, explicitly excluding "About You" (itself a required-section START header, not an end boundary). (b) A real Avionics Technician posting phrases a universal minimum-age requirement as "Must be 18 years or older" rather than "18 years of age" -- the existing "of age" exclusion (decision #90) didn't cover this equally common phrasing; added "or older" to `_NON_EXPERIENCE_YEARS_CONTEXT_RE`. (2) **"Founding Engineer"/"Founding Researcher"/"Founding Infrastructure Engineer" titles are a strong senior/architect-tier signal not covered by the seniority-check's enumerated title list** (Senior/Staff/Principal/Lead/Architect/Director/Manager/VP/Chief) -- three of the 35 jobs used exactly this "Founding X" framing and all were genuinely lead/architect-scoped in substance. (3) **Defense-contractor detection has no deterministic backstop** -- CoVar's posting bluntly states "AI/ML and software development for defense and healthcare" with zero TS/SCI/clearance keywords, so it sails through the existing deterministic pre-filter untouched and depends entirely on LLM judgment to apply the rubric's own "defense contractors are out of scope" prose, the same class of gap the TS/SCI and commission-only gates were built to close for their respective cases. (4) **"Commercial experience" (British-English convention for paid/professional experience) is a real experience-requirement phrasing with no attached number** -- Hargreaves Lansdown's posting requires it but no years-based extraction mechanism (prompt-side or the separate deterministic-fallback regex extractor) is keyed to catch a bare "commercial experience" phrase. (5) **A "must be a currently-enrolled CS/SWE student graduating in [window]" new-grad-pipeline eligibility clause is a categorically different disqualifier than "requires a completed CS degree"** -- Arch's 2027-start posting requires active current enrollment, which the candidate (already graduated, non-CS degree) fails in a different way than a generic degree-requirement rejection; rare pattern, flagged but not built. A real CACI "Software Engineer" posting states "as a requirement of continued employment... you will be required to obtain a Top Secret clearance" -- confirmed this doesn't match any existing `_CLEARANCE_REQUIRED_PATTERN` alternative (all four require "clearance...required" adjacency, "must possess/hold/have an active/current clearance", or a bare "requires an active/current clearance" -- none match "required to obtain a [tier] clearance" framing). Investigated the real scope before considering a fix: a live DB scan for "required to obtain a...clearance" phrasing found 5 candidate rows, only 1 of which (this CACI posting) was a genuine, unconditional Secret/Top-Secret-tier requirement -- the other 4 were either a different concept entirely ("required to obtain an Authority to Operate (ATO)", not a personnel clearance), a softer clearance tier explicitly out of scope for this gate by design (two real "Technical Support Specialist" postings requiring only "Public Trust" clearance, a background-check-level tier most US citizens clear easily), or conditional language ("May be required to obtain a security clearance" on a real internship posting -- the existing pattern already deliberately excludes conditional "may" framing). **Deliberately NOT fixed**: `_CLEARANCE_REQUIRED_PATTERN`'s own extensive code comment (scorer.py, directly above the pattern) explicitly documents that "ability to obtain" framing for Secret/Top-Secret tier (as opposed to the rarer, always-disqualifying TS/SCI tier) was a conscious leniency decision from a prior session, reasoning that many US citizens without disqualifying history can obtain this tier, so auto-rejecting every mention would over-reject. "Required to obtain" is a real, if slightly stronger, variant of the same "obtain" framing this documented policy already covers -- adding it as a new hard disqualifier would silently reverse that prior, deliberate, and still-apparently-intentional decision rather than fix an oversight. Scored the CACI job on judgment instead (2/10, reflecting the real TS-clearance-track plus broader defense-contractor domain mismatch), consistent with how every other "obtain"-framed clearance mention has been handled this session. | 119 | Real production bug found and fixed by the user directly: jobs that exhaust all 5 cloud scoring retries were being marked `score_failed` FOREVER with no recovery path, even though a working local scorer (decision #76-77) already exists -- `_flush_score_batch` now tries it once as a genuine last resort before giving up | 2026-09-11, same overall session as #94-118. User traced decision #117's log numbers themselves (3,429 attempted, only 214 real scores, asked "what happened to the other 3,215?") and pushed back hard on the discovery that 62 real jobs were sitting on their 5th and final retry with quota still down -- correctly identifying this as "a massive issue" rather than accepting it as a footnote. **Root cause, precisely traced**: `scorer.py`'s `_flush_score_batch` has always had exactly two outcomes once `MAX_SCORE_RETRIES` (5) is hit -- reschedule with backoff, or give up permanently (`transition_state(..., "score_failed")`) -- with zero automatic recovery, even though `scoring/deterministic_fallback.py` (decision #76-77) is a real, already-validated local-model scorer sitting right there, deliberately built for exactly this class of outage but scoped to MANUAL invocation only (`applypilot revalidate-deterministic-fallback-scores`/`score-deterministic-fallback` CLI commands). **User's proposed fix, confirmed correct and built as asked**: route retry-exhausted jobs to the local scorer automatically instead of letting them die. **Fix, deliberately narrow rather than a full reversal of decision #76's original caution**: new `scorer._try_exhaustion_fallback(conn, url)` -- lazily imports `deterministic_fallback.score_job_deterministic` (avoids a real circular import, since `deterministic_fallback.py` already imports FROM `scorer.py` at module level; verified no import-time cycle via a direct `python -c` import check before writing any tests), re-fetches the full job row, calls the local scorer, and returns its result dict on success or `None` on any failure (Ollama unreachable, etc. -- caught broadly so this new path can never itself become a crash source). `_flush_score_batch`'s exhaustion branch now calls this FIRST; on success, it recursively re-invokes itself with the fallback's result and `score_method="deterministic_fallback"` -- reusing the exact same success-path logic (fit_score write, eligibility-driven archival, state transition) rather than duplicating any of it, so a fallback-rescued ineligible verdict (e.g. non_us_only) still correctly archives the job, not just writes a bare score. Only fires at the EXACT moment a job would otherwise be lost forever (`retry_count >= MAX_SCORE_RETRIES`), so the normal fast-cloud-scoring happy path (the vast majority of jobs, which succeed well before 5 retries) is completely unaffected -- verified via a dedicated test that a job still within its retry budget never calls the local model at all. **Why this doesn't contradict decision #76's original "manual invocation only, an honest score_error is recoverable via retry" reasoning**: that reasoning assumed retry would eventually succeed -- decision #117 found a real, live counterexample (62 jobs) where it doesn't, because Gemini's quota keeps re-exhausting faster than the backoff schedule can outlast it. Once retry is genuinely exhausted, "imperfect but real, and still revalidation-eligible" is strictly better than "permanently failed with zero recovery" -- the same logic decision #76 already used to justify the fallback scorer's EXISTENCE, just now extended to this one narrow trigger point instead of requiring a human to notice and invoke it manually. Updated both `_flush_score_batch`'s and `deterministic_fallback.py`'s module docstrings to document the narrowed (not reversed) rule -- this is now the ONE automatic caller; the general "score whatever's pending" bulk path is still 100% manual, unchanged. 4 new tests in `tests/test_score_exhaustion_fallback.py`: exhausted job rescued by a mocked local success (verifies fit_score/state/score_method/score_attempts-reset all correct), exhausted job still fails permanently when the local model itself errors (defense-in-depth, unchanged original behavior), a job still within its retry budget never calls the local model at all (confirms the narrow trigger), and a fallback verdict of non_us_only correctly archives the job (confirms the recursive reuse of the real success-path logic, not just a bare score write). All 4 pass; broader targeted suite (`test_score_exhaustion_fallback.py`/`test_score_batch_archived_guard.py`/`test_deterministic_fallback_scoring.py`/`test_pending_score_state_selection.py`/`test_scorer_prefilter.py`/`test_eligibility_false_positives.py`/`test_batch_identity.py`, 282 passed/1 skipped) confirmed no regressions. Not yet done: the 62 real currently-stuck-at-max-retries jobs haven't been proactively rescued by this fix yet -- they'll be rescued automatically the next time `_flush_score_batch` processes them (their next scheduled retry, ~21h out per decision #117), or could be triggered sooner by re-running `applypilot run score` against them directly; the user's own stated priority right now is resuming the Claude-direct audit toward 1000 jobs, not backlog throughput, so this wasn't force-triggered immediately. |

A 2-job smoke test (`--limit 2`) was run and verified first (both rows landed correctly: real `fit_score`/`state`/`score_method='deterministic_fallback'`/`score_reasoning`, confirmed via direct DB read). The real full run was then launched via `nohup python -m applypilot.cli score-deterministic-fallback --scope all_unscored --limit 0 ... &`, logging to a timestamped file under `~/.applypilot/logs/` -- but since the Bash tool call itself was ALSO run with `run_in_background: true`, the outer shell returned as soon as the inner `&` backgrounded the real process, so the harness marked that task "completed" within seconds despite the actual scoring work being nowhere near done. This would have meant no completion notification ever firing for the real multi-day run. Caught by checking `tasklist`/the log file directly rather than trusting the "completed" status. **Fix**: killed both the detached python processes (`taskkill`, confirmed via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` command-line inspection before killing, to be sure the right PIDs were targeted) -- safe, since only the 2 smoke-test jobs had been written (per-job commit, decision #77's safety property) -- then relaunched the exact same command directly as the Bash tool's own tracked process (`run_in_background: true`, no `nohup`/`&` wrapper this time), so the harness's own notification fires when the real run actually finishes or crashes, however many hours/days out that is. Confirmed running via `tasklist` + command-line inspection after relaunch. Redone with the correct resolution path: summary now correctly falls back to a generic, accurate `"Experienced across {skills}."` sentence (the decision #73 fix firing exactly as designed) -- the earlier finding retracted as a test-setup artifact, not a real bug. **The genuine, honest finding that survived correction**: re-running the real Ramp "Sales Development Representative, SMB" job (the exact job decision #132's cloud path approved) through degraded mode with the fresh banks in place produced a structurally valid, `STATUS: approved` resume with zero fabrication -- but visibly rougher than the cloud version: several bullets are bare 2-4-word fragments ("Package handling", "Tires", "Shocks and struts", "Brakes") rather than full sentences, and "Sunburn" renders with an empty bullet list. Traced to the real, root cause: these terse fragments are literally verbatim entries from profile.json's own `responsibilities` arrays (e.g. Mavis's list mixes full sentences with bare 2-3-word items) -- the CLOUD path is invisible to this because `_build_tailor_prompt` explicitly instructs "Reframe EVERY bullet... every bullet must be reworded," smoothing over any input roughness, while DEGRADED MODE only smooths over whatever it successfully bank-selects or locally-realizes; anything left over renders the base resume's raw text verbatim, roughness included. This is an honest architecture-vs-data-quality finding, not a phrase-bank or local-model bug: the safety guarantee holds (nothing invented, e.g. no repeat of the Stand-Up Comedian problem), but real output quality is capped by how well-formed the underlying `responsibilities` data already is. Not fixed tonight (would mean rewriting profile.json's terser entries into fuller sentences, a data-quality task, not a code fix) -- flagged as a new Future Work item. **Full test suite run clean**: 2069 passed, 7 skipped, 0 failed, across all of tonight's changes (llm.py exhaustion-marking fix, tailor.py/cover_letter.py metadata stripping, cli.py doc-format default, tailor.py standup enforcement) plus the entire pre-existing suite. |

## Known Technical Gotchas

1. **Gemini thinking tokens**: 2.5+ models use thinking tokens that consume max_tokens budget. A simple response needs 30 tokens, a bullet rewrite needs 1200+.
2. **Agent log timezone**: Log filenames use local time, DB `last_attempted_at` is UTC. Dashboard matcher converts UTC→local.
3. **Singleton LLM client**: `llm.py` reads env vars at module import. Call `config.load_env()` BEFORE importing.
4. **Editable install**: `pip install -e .` means source edits take effect immediately.
5. **gemini-2.0-flash deprecated**: Use `gemini-2.5-flash` or newer for new API users.
6. **Docker MCP Toolkit interference**: If Docker Desktop is installed with MCP Toolkit, it exposes `mcp__MCP_DOCKER__browser_*` tools that shadow the local Playwright MCP. These Docker tools can't access the host filesystem, breaking resume/cover letter uploads. Fix: `--strict-mcp-config` in the claude subprocess command.
7. **qwen3 local calls need more `max_tokens` headroom than the visible output suggests**: the local model burns part of its budget on hidden internal reasoning before writing anything. Confirmed live twice on 2026-09-04: `max_tokens=200` and `max_tokens=600` both produced "Null/empty content" on real, previously-working-looking prompts; `max_tokens=1024`/`4096` fixed it in some but not all cases (one case timed out instead at 4096 -- see decision #70). Don't assume a small `max_tokens` is safe for a local qwen3 call just because the expected visible output is short. **2026-09-06 (decision #74)**: the older `/no_think` text-prefix convention referenced in earlier notes has been REMOVED and replaced with the real `"think": false` API field (sent as a top-level JSON payload field, not a message-content prefix) -- real A/B testing found the text-prefix version doesn't reliably suppress qwen3's reasoning even when correctly applied, while the API field does. This headroom gotcha still applies even with the field set correctly (300 tokens still failed 0/6 in testing; 700 was the smallest value that worked reliably) -- thinking-suppression and token-budget are separate, both-required fixes, not substitutes for each other. See also `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_OLLAMA_NATIVE` (opt-in, `llm.py`) for Ollama's *native* `/api/chat`, which was both faster and more reliable than the OpenAI-compat shim even with the field fix in place.
8. **`.env` keys can be in two different places**: `config.load_env()` loads `~/.applypilot/.env` explicitly, THEN a bare `load_dotenv()` that searches from cwd upward -- a repo-root `.env` (e.g. `C:\Users\phili\Projects\resume-agent\.env`) is a real, separate, valid source of `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` and is easy to miss if you only check `~/.applypilot/.env` (see decision #67's correction to #63).

**Where things stand**: the Claude-direct scoring audit (decisions #94-128) reached its explicit 1000-job target, and the user then explicitly redirected scope (decision #132): stop growing the scoring backlog (a 13,475-job local backfill run was stopped mid-run, decisions #129-131) and get back to testing tailor/cover, since 1000+ scored jobs is plenty of real material. The rest of this session (decisions #132-133) tested the tailor stage directly:
- Found and fixed a real bug in `llm.py`: a persistent 429/503 on the LAST remaining cloud provider never marked itself exhausted before raising, so tailor.py's degraded-mode redirect wrongly concluded cloud was still available and gave up instead of falling back to local -- the same failure class decision #119 fixed for the scorer, now closed for tailor too.
- Fixed a real document-metadata privacy leak (job title/site/"Customized for..." embedded in generated PDF/DOCX properties, more explicit than the already-clean upload filename) and flipped the CLI's `--doc-format` default from docx to pdf, per explicit user preference.
- Found and fixed a real fabrication bug: a cloud-generated "Stand-Up Comedian" resume section was invented from nothing (the fact existed nowhere in profile.json). Fixed with a new `profile.json` `standup_experience.pinned_first_bullet` field (gitignored, candidate-specific -- confirmed via `git check-ignore`) plus a deterministic enforcement function, so the entry is now grounded and its first bullet is guaranteed rather than hoped-for.

- Ran real degraded-mode tailoring against a known-good job and found an honest quality-ceiling finding: safe (no fabrication) but visibly rougher than cloud, because some of profile.json's own `responsibilities` entries are terse fragments that only the cloud path's mandatory full-rewrite instruction smooths over (Future Work item 19).
- Found a real, minor env-var-naming inconsistency (`LLM_URL` vs `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL`) that blocks a genuinely local-only run without a workaround (Future Work item 20).

## Future Work — Pick up here (2026-09-07 session close, after decision #77 closed out #76's list)

Full detail in decisions #71-77. Quick-start checklist for next session, roughly priority order:

1. ~~Deeper Gemini-reasoning distillation~~ — **DONE 2026-09-07 (decision #77).** Redone as a real rerunnable tool (`run_reasoning_distillation.py`), both score bands, multi-word phrases, a real ranking bug caught and fixed before trusting results. Confirmed: no missing factor on either side, the atomic-facts scorer's existing family/years/degree axis is already the right one. One free improvement found and wired in: `compensation.compensation_score_adjustment` (already built, wasn't being used by the fallback scorer).
2. **A real escalation trigger between local models — found and shipped OPT-IN 2026-09-07 (decision #77), needs revalidation before trusting as default.** Not self-consistency (confirmed dead end) — a title-keyword trigger (`_AMBIGUOUS_TITLE_RE` in `scoring/deterministic_fallback.py`) built directly from real observed 1.7b-vs-8b disagreement titles at n=52: catches 5/6 real gate-flipping disagreements, escalates only 33% of jobs, hybrid gate agreement 85% vs 1.7b-alone's 79% (pure 8b: 87%). HONEST CAVEAT the decision itself flags: the keyword list was built FROM the exact sample it's validated against — small-n overfitting risk, same class of mistake decision #76 already made once (the sales-rep anecdote). Next step: revalidate against a fresh, independently-sampled batch (once more clean data accumulates) before ever making this the default instead of opt-in (`--escalate-model`).
3. ~~qwen3:8b full-scale validation~~ — **DONE 2026-09-07 (decision #77).** Real n=52 result: 87% gate agreement, recall 0.83, precision 0.86, avg 75.8s/call (real, meaningfully better precision than 1.7b's 73%). Root cause of the prior crash was very likely the machine's own constraints (11.8GB RAM, mechanical HDD, confirmed via direct inspection) more than the model itself — mitigated (not fully solved) by disabling non-essential startup apps; a full defrag is still recommended but needs admin elevation not available in-session.
4. ~~Promote the atomic-facts scorer from experiment to production~~ — **DONE 2026-09-07 (decision #77).** `scoring/deterministic_fallback.py`, `score_method` DB column, `score-deterministic-fallback` / `revalidate-deterministic-fallback-scores` CLI commands, 37 tests, real live end-to-end verification (score → revalidate round-trip against a real quota-cooldown-stuck job). Defaults to qwen3:8b. **Note for next session**: a real near-miss happened during this build — an unbounded smoke-test invocation found 7,041 real jobs stuck on quota cooldown and would have run for days; `--limit` now defaults to 10, not unlimited. Always pass an explicit `--limit`/`limit=` when invoking this for real, and be aware `score_error LIKE '%quota cooldown%'` can match a large real backlog if Gemini's quota has been out for a while.
5. ~~WeWorkRemotely/Intel enrichment bug~~ — **DONE (WWR) 2026-09-07 (decision #77); Intel re-tested 2026-09-09, could not reproduce the original failure.** WWR: two real root causes fixed (missing selectors for `.lis-container__job`; expired listings silently redirecting to the site root instead of 404ing) plus a boilerplate-content safety net. Intel/Workday: ran a real headed-vs-headless A/B (`data/experiments/enrichment_headed_vs_headless_20260909/`) against 10 fresh Intel job URLs pulled live from Workday's own search API -- BOTH modes succeeded identically (10/10 `status=partial`, tier=1/JSON-LD, byte-identical description lengths on every URL). No difference, and no reproduction of decision #77's original bot-detection symptom at all. Likely explanation: these postings' JSON-LD (tier 1) succeeded immediately, so the tier-2 CSS-selector wait decision #77 found hanging was never reached -- the original failure may have been on a posting lacking JSON-LD, or Intel's detection may simply not have triggered this time (bot detection is often inconsistent/load-dependent). Headed mode is NOT proven to help; also NOT proven unnecessary -- the sample that would exercise the actual failure path wasn't available live. Separately noticed, not chased: every one of the 10 came back `partial` not `ok` (JSON-LD gave a description but no `application_url`) -- a possibly-real, different gap in Workday JSON-LD apply-URL extraction, unrelated to headed/headless, not investigated this session. Re-open if/when a real Intel enrichment failure is caught live again -- ideally capture the failing URL immediately (before it's retried into a different state) so the actual failing case can be tested headed vs. headless directly, rather than a fresh unrelated sample.
6. ~~Generate real phrase banks and verify end-to-end~~ — **DONE 2026-09-05.** Ran real degraded-mode `tailor_resume()` against a real Intel "Module Equipment Technician" job (→ Mavis evidence) with a real generated bank. `STATUS: approved`, real bank-selected content shipped in the output (`bank_covered: True`, `llm_called: False`). Found and fixed 4 real bugs along the way (decision #73).
7. ~~Local-model editor reliability (decision #70)~~ — **DONE 2026-09-06 (decision #74).** Root cause wasn't the model, it was the thinking-suppression mechanism: production's `/no_think` text prefix doesn't reliably suppress qwen3's reasoning; Ollama's real `"think": false` API field does. Fixed in `llm.py` (real field, not a prefix) + bumped `edit_sentence_for_requirement`'s default `max_tokens` 300→700 (still needed even with thinking suppressed, per gotcha #7). Verified live: reliable edits on real bank sentences against real requirements. Also added an opt-in `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_OLLAMA_NATIVE` fast path (Ollama's native `/api/chat`, ~10x faster than the OpenAI-compat shim even with the `think` fix) — enabled in this machine's `.env`, default off elsewhere since `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` is documented to also support llama.cpp, which has no native-Ollama route.
8. ~~The `troubleshooting`/`hands-on`/`equipment`/`servers` ambiguous-term gap (decision #71)~~ — **DONE 2026-09-08 (decision #78).** All four added to `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS`, confirmed via a real escalating-batch audit (0.75% -> 0% false-positive rate on Mavis, 99% CIs non-overlapping). Required a real second fix beyond the simple addition: `_local_context_words` now excludes other `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS` members from counting as shared context, closing a mutual-vouching bug where two unverified ambiguous terms could corroborate each other.
9. **Expand phrase banks beyond whatever the first `expand-bank` run covers** — six experience_inventory entries total; only Mavis (and earlier, Alex Prosperity Group) have real banks so far. **Scoped further 2026-09-06** (not yet built): `applypilot init`'s wizard (`wizard/init.py`) never builds `experience_inventory`/`project_inventory` at all — only flat fields (`personal`/`compensation`/`skills_boundary`/etc.). The rich, categorized shape `build_phrase_bank`/`expand-bank` actually need (`role_title`, `relevance_categories`, `factual_concepts`/`responsibilities`, `evidence_level`, `constraints` — confirmed against the real `profile.json` schema) doesn't exist anywhere in the wizard; every existing entry was hand-authored across sessions. The existing resume parser (`scoring/pdf.py`'s `parse_entries`) doesn't shortcut this either — it extracts `{title, subtitle, bullets}` for rendering, not the categorized/tagged shape banks need. So "generate banks during setup" is really the SAME underlying gap as item 10 below (something needs to turn raw facts into `experience_inventory`/`project_inventory` entries) plus a trivial final step (`expand-bank` is already idempotent and content-hash-gated, so calling it as a last wizard step once the inventory exists is not the hard part).
10. **Pull candidate facts from external sites (GitHub, portfolio, LinkedIn) during `applypilot init`/profile setup**, AND/OR build a resume→`experience_inventory` extraction step. **GitHub gate DONE 2026-09-09 (decision #85)** -- `discovery/github_profile.py`'s fetch/flag/sparse-filter/interactive-review pipeline, wired into `applypilot init` (opt-in Step 7) and a standalone `applypilot import-github <username>` command; 26 tests, all mocked (no real repo run through it end-to-end yet). Still open from here: (a) the categorization step is deliberately simple/deterministic (name + description + top languages), not an LLM extraction pass -- upgrading match quality is a separate, harder problem; (b) LinkedIn and resume-re-parsing sources are still unbuilt; (c) the one draft entry from decision #84's exploration (the portfolio site) still hasn't actually been run through `import-github` or added to `profile.json`. **First real exploration done 2026-09-09 (decision #84).** Found the candidate's real GitHub (`PhiliptheGinger`, discovered via the local `Standup-OCR` repo's git remote, no need to ask) -- 5/7 known `project_inventory` entries matched exactly, confirming the account. Two repos exist that aren't in `profile.json` yet: `PhiliptheGinger.github.io` (a real, deployed portfolio site -- drafted a candidate entry, not yet added, pending the user's go-ahead) and `greensboro-data-coop` (explicitly rejected -- its own README says "Initial draft," no real functionality yet; user confirmed: avoid pulling sparse/stub repos in general, not just this one). A third lead ("Money Machine," name-dropped in the portfolio README) turned out to be a false positive -- it's the candidate's own informal name for the ALREADY-catalogued CAP Predictor project, not a new repo; corrected, no new evidence there. **New, explicit requirement pinned by the user before any ingestion pipeline ships**: every candidate repo needs a reputational-visibility check before being added as resume evidence -- named examples: a repo that scrapes resumes/automates job applications (ApplyPilot itself is the literal example given), anything "semantically vulgar," or anything "pragmatically against authority" (e.g. piracy/circumvention tooling). No heuristic or review UI built yet -- this is a hard requirement for the eventual pipeline, not a nice-to-have, and should gate any auto-inclusion path from day one. Resume re-parsing specifically was DE-SCOPED for this candidate's own profile (the user's actual resume.txt is weaker than the already-curated `profile.json` -- re-parsing it now would inject worse data than what's already there) but should still be built generically for `applypilot init`'s wizard, since other/future users won't have this same asymmetry. Remaining open design decisions before building anything real: (a) the categorization step (LLM extraction pass vs. guided Q&A vs. both) turning raw repo/README data into the `experience_inventory`/`project_inventory` shape, (b) the reputational-review UX itself, (c) what "show the user what was found, let them exclude anything" looks like in the `init` CLI flow. Still the prerequisite for item 9.
11. ~~The near_prototype single-keyword gap for non-identity terms~~ — **DONE 2026-09-08 (decisions #79-80).** A systematic audit across ALL FOUR evidence-bearing inventories (`experience_inventory`/`project_inventory`/`skills_inventory`/`certifications`) found this was a real, much bigger problem than a "no known live instance" — AMP Smart and Freelance Photography were at 26.55%/19.75% false-positive `supported` rates (decision #79, mostly "communications"), and "You Power You"/CAP Predictor had smaller but real bugs of the same shape ("power"/"research"/"analytics," decision #80). Fixed via `_AMBIGUOUS_TERMS` additions ("communications"/"sales"/"customer-facing"/"content"/"creative"/"media"/"research"/"analytics") plus NAME-collision fixes ("house"/"smart"/"amp"/"power" → `_GENERIC_EVIDENCE_TERMS`, same "ups" precedent). Explicitly reviewed and KEPT several high-hit-count words ("python"/"automation"/"web"/"customer service"/"technical support"/"logistics") after confirming real samples show genuine, correct transferable evidence, not collisions — including one real near-miss avoided: "web" looked like the same shape as "power" but `Web Design` (skills_inventory) has NO evidence text at all, so ambiguous-listing it would have broken a skill whose real identity genuinely IS "web." All four inventories are now covered by this audit methodology.
12. **OpenAI account has $0 credits** (decision #67c) — not a code issue, but worth knowing before trusting the fallback chain at scale; currently effectively Gemini + local only. Explicitly deprioritized by the user.
13. **Reconsider the `claude_cli`/interactive-Claude budgeting model** — flagged 2026-09-09, not designed or built. `APPLYPILOT_RESERVE_CLAUDE_FOR_APPLY` (llm.py, default true) unconditionally reserves the whole Claude usage window for Tier-3 auto-apply, never letting `claude_cli` serve score/tailor/cover/judge, per the real 2026-08-20 incident where a cover run cascaded to `claude_cli` and burned budget auto-apply needed later. User's observation: a static, always-reserve-for-apply policy may not be the right default in every condition -- e.g. when no auto-apply run is actively planned/pending, or during a deliberate one-off research/distillation exercise (see decision #88), reserving 100% of the budget for a use that isn't happening right now is itself a real cost. Also newly relevant: the account is a Pro plan, not Max (see the Tier 3 correction above) -- meaningfully less total headroom to split between the two uses in the first place, which may change what "reserve enough for auto-apply" even means in practice. Nothing about WHAT a smarter policy would look like has been designed yet (time-of-day awareness? explicit "auto-apply not running right now" signal? a smaller partial reservation instead of all-or-nothing?) -- purely a flagged direction for a future session.
14. **"Seeking N+ years..." phrasing not recognized as a qualifying requirement** — flagged 2026-09-09 (decision #92 session), not fixed. A real Salesforce "Named Account Executive, State Government" posting states "Seeking 10+ Years of Sales Experience with a commitment to deliver business outcomes..." in its opening description (not under a "Preferred" header) — a genuine, functionally-required years floor, but `_REQUIRED_CONTEXT_RE` doesn't recognize "seeking" as qualifying phrasing (only "required"/"must have"/"minimum of"), so `extract_years_required` returns `None` and the job scored 9 instead of something lower. Not fixed this session because broadening the qualifying-context word list carries the same small-n-overfitting/false-positive risk this session repeatedly found with "minimum" (decision #90) — "seeking" can also introduce genuinely aspirational, not-hard-required framing in other postings, unverified either way. Needs the same real-data verification treatment (check a real batch of "seeking N years" postings for false positives) before broadening.
15. **Five rubric-coverage findings from decision #94's 35-job Claude-direct scoring batch, none fixed yet** — flagged 2026-09-10, from a batch dominated by terse HN "who's hiring" listings rather than the formal Workday/Greenhouse/BuiltIn postings decisions #82-93 worked from, so it exercised the PROMPT-based rubric's judgment rather than the deterministic-fallback regex extractors. (a) **Implicit-signal-only postings** — ~20/35 jobs never state an explicit years/degree requirement at all, yet are unambiguously professional/non-entry by domain context (a "Founding Infrastructure Engineer," a research role publishing novel ML papers, a $300-400K "Junior SWE" building frontier-AI training environments); this is the SAME gap decision #88 already flagged, now reproduced at larger scale with concrete examples — still no design decision made on whether/how the rubric should use implicit context, only reasoned judgment applied ad hoc when scoring. (b) **"Founding Engineer"/"Founding Researcher"/"Founding Infrastructure Engineer" titles** are a strong senior/architect-tier signal not in the seniority-check's enumerated title list (Senior/Staff/Principal/Lead/Architect/Director/Manager/VP/Chief) — 3/35 jobs used this framing, all genuinely lead-scoped in substance. (c) **Defense-contractor detection has no deterministic backstop** — a real CoVar posting states "AI/ML and software development for defense and healthcare" with zero TS/SCI/clearance keywords, so it reaches the LLM scorer untouched despite the rubric's own "defense contractors are out of scope" prose; same class of gap the TS/SCI and commission-only gates (`scorer.py`) were built to close for their own cases, not yet built for this one. (d) **"Commercial experience" (a real, unnumbered professional-experience phrasing)** appears in a real Hargreaves Lansdown posting with no years-based extraction mechanism (prompt-side or the deterministic-fallback regex extractor) keyed to catch it. (e) **A "must be a currently-enrolled CS/SWE student graduating in [window]" new-grad-pipeline clause** (a real Arch posting) is a categorically different disqualifier than "requires a completed CS degree" — rare pattern, lowest priority of the five. None of these were coded into a fix — see decision #94 for full detail before acting on any of them, and apply the same real-data false-positive verification discipline items 2/14 above already established before broadening any pattern.
16. **GB/CAN/bare-AU/MX country-code abbreviations, verified safe but not yet added to `_INELIGIBLE_LOCATION_PATTERNS`** — flagged 2026-09-10 (decision #108), deprioritized in favor of that same decision's bigger US-also-listed fix. Each already individually verified against the full live DB with zero false positives: `GB` (4/4 real UK Collins Aerospace facility addresses, e.g. `"GB-WSX-CRAWLEY-990..."`), `CAN` (3/3 real `"Toronto, Ontario, CAN"`/`"Ontario, CAN"` strings — note these specific 3 also list "United States - Remote" in the same string, so they'd need the decision #108 US-also-listed guard to still correctly NOT reject, already handled generically since that guard checks the same location string), bare `AU` (6/6 real RTX Australia facility codes, e.g. `"AU-WA-HENDERSON-103-CUST..."`), `MX` (2/2 real Mexicali, Mexico facility codes). A future session can add these directly (same pattern as the Canada/UK/AUS additions in #106/#107) without re-doing the verification -- just re-confirm against a fresh DB snapshot in case new rows have appeared since 2026-09-10.
17. **Bare Canadian province names (Ontario/Quebec/Alberta/Manitoba/Saskatchewan) without a "Canada"/"CAN" token, not yet added to `_INELIGIBLE_LOCATION_PATTERNS`** — flagged 2026-09-10 (decision #112). A real Salesforce posting (`location: "Ontario - Remote"`) slipped past the location-field pre-filter (though it was still correctly scored `non_us_only` by reading the full body text, since the Claude-direct path doesn't rely on the pre-filter alone). A live DB query found 18 distinct locations with a bare province name, 9 not already caught by `_check_ineligible`, but most of those 9 are already correctly excluded via the decision #108 US-also-listed guard (they also mention the US) -- only 3-4 rows (`"Ontario Remote Work"` x2, `"remote, Ontario - Remote"`, `"Ontario - Remote"`) are genuinely uncaught with no US alternative present. **Unlike the GB/CAN/bare-AU/MX items above (item 16), this one is NOT yet verified safe to ship as a simple addition** -- "Ontario, California" is a real, well-known US city (unlike Sydney/São Paulo, which had zero collision risk after verification), so a bare "Ontario" token needs the same disambiguation work as the Canada/US-state-abbreviation fix (decision #106: an enumerated US-state-abbreviation negative lookahead, not a bare 2-letter guard) before it can ship safely. Quebec/Alberta/Manitoba/Saskatchewan are much less likely to collide with real US place names but haven't been individually checked either. Given only 3-4 real rows currently affected, this is lower priority than item 16's already-verified-safe additions.
18. **Tailor/cover/apply bottleneck — partially addressed 2026-09-11 (decisions #132-133), not resolved.** As of decision #118, 34 real `state='scored'` jobs were waiting with only 2 `tailored`. This session ran several small tailor batches (decisions #132/#133) and generated real phrase banks for 9/11 entries, but did NOT run `cover`/`apply` at all, and the bulk of the waiting pool is still untouched — a future session should still treat "advance tailor/cover/apply throughput" as a real, concrete task, not assume this is closed just because some activity happened tonight.
19. **Local-tailor output quality is capped by profile.json's own data granularity, not by the phrase-bank/local-model architecture** — flagged 2026-09-11/12 (decision #133). A real, corrected (not test-artifact) finding: degraded-mode tailoring against a real job (the same Ramp SDR job decision #132's cloud path approved) produced a valid, non-fabricated, `approved` resume, but several bullets rendered as bare 2-4-word fragments ("Tires", "Shocks and struts", "Package handling") because those are literally verbatim entries in profile.json's `responsibilities` arrays (which mix full sentences with terse keyword-style items) — the cloud path never exposes this because its prompt explicitly requires every bullet to be reworded/reframed, but degraded mode only smooths over what it successfully bank-selects or locally-realizes, letting anything uncovered render as raw source text. A future session could either (a) rewrite the terser `responsibilities` entries into fuller sentences (a data task, in profile.json, not code), or (b) add a code-level minimum-length/completeness check that skips using a bare-fragment original bullet as degraded-mode filler, falling back to omission instead. Neither attempted — this is a real quality ceiling to know about before treating degraded-mode output as production-ready, not an urgent bug.
20. **`LLM_URL` vs `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` naming inconsistency prevents a genuinely local-only (both cloud keys absent) run** — found 2026-09-11 (decision #133) while forcing a local-only test. `llm.py`'s `_detect_provider(quality=True)` (which picks the PRIMARY client for any stage, including `expand-bank`/tailor) only recognizes `LLM_URL` for a local primary provider and raises `RuntimeError: No LLM provider configured` if both `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` are absent, even when `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` (the separate var gating the fallback-chain/degraded-mode local entry and `is_local_configured()`) is fully configured. Worked around for one night's testing via a process-scoped `LLM_URL` env var; not fixed in code. A future session should decide whether `_detect_provider` should also honor `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` as a primary-provider fallback (closing the gap) or whether these are deliberately meant to stay separate (in which case this should at least be documented, since the current error message doesn't mention `APPLYPILOT_LOCAL_LLM_URL` at all and it's easy to assume "local is configured" means qualifying for every code path that says so).

## Future Work — Local-employer discovery expansion (2026-04-24)

**2026-09-09 note (decision #84):** this whole section is almost certainly a leftover fork artifact (this codebase started as a fork) rather than something actively driving current work -- the candidate isn't Seattle-based. Not deleted, since it's real, usable data for someone who is. When this project is generalized to support multiple localities, this section should become a template/pattern (per-locality employer YAML + scraper wiring) rather than a one-off Seattle list -- flagged for whenever that generalization work actually starts, not scheduled yet.

Two YAML files in `docs/` catalog ~120 Seattle-area employers (Downtown / SLU / Fremont / Ballard / Queen Anne / Belltown / Redmond / Bellevue / Kirkland / Capitol Hill / Eastlake):

- `docs/seattle_employers_v1.yaml` — 41 companies, senior/staff-focused
- `docs/seattle_employers_v2.yaml` — 80 net-new from AI2 Incubator, Madrona, PSL, Voyager, Built In Seattle, and gaming/biotech clusters

**TODOs to integrate these into discovery:**

1. **Workday scraping (quick win, ~1h):** verify tenant subdomains for the ~14 Workday-hosted companies and add to `src/applypilot/config/employers.yaml` following the existing pattern (`tenant`, `site_id`, `base_url`). Candidates: Remitly, Okta, Expedia Group, Zillow Group, F5, Salesforce, Adobe Seattle, Apptio, Lululemon, Prologis Tech, Qualtrics, SoFi.
2. **Greenhouse scraper — ✅ shipped 2026-04-24 (commit f7cba79).** `src/applypilot/discovery/greenhouse.py` hits the public board API at `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{slug}/jobs?content=true`. Registry at `src/applypilot/config/greenhouse_employers.yaml` (18 verified slugs across Temporal, Pulumi, Anduril, Carta, Smartsheet, OfferUp, ExtraHop, Tanium, Databricks, Axon, Sprout Social, Stackline, Yoodli, plus Twilio/Pinterest/Airbnb/Stripe/UberFreight). Combined: ~1,500 jobs per full crawl. Ai2 and Echodyne aren't on Greenhouse — still need investigation.

3. **Amazon scraper — ✅ shipped 2026-04-24 (commit 3b2c745/cc2917d).** `src/applypilot/discovery/amazon.py` uses the public GET JSON API at `amazon.jobs/en/search.json`. Key learning: Amazon's search is exact-match, not fuzzy — use broad queries ("software engineer", "software development engineer", etc.) not LinkedIn-style "Senior Software Engineer backend". Also: Amazon's `radius` param is unreliable, so client-side filter via `_in_seattle_area()` enforces WA state + explicit Seattle-area cities. Pulls ~500 Seattle-specific jobs per full crawl.

4. **Costco scraper — ✅ shipped 2026-04-24 (commit 3b2c745).** `src/applypilot/discovery/costco.py` hits `careers.costco.com/api/jobs`. Each job is nested under a `data` key. Most results are retail (Cake Decorator, Cashier) — scorer pre-filter now includes retail-role patterns to shortcut them to score=2 without LLM calls.

5. **Workday registry expansion — TODO.** The 2026-04-24 mega-corp audit (`docs/seattle_employers_v3_megacorps.yaml`) identified ~18 companies tagged as Workday (T-Mobile, Starbucks, Nordstrom, REI, Boeing, Alaska Airlines, PACCAR, Weyerhaeuser, HP, Dell, Accenture, Wayfair, Tyler Tech, ServiceNow, Raytheon, Limeade, Expedia, Tableau). Automated tenant-guess probes all failed — those companies either use different ATSes than Workday OR use non-standard tenant subdomains. Verify each manually by visiting their careers page and checking for `.wdN.myworkdayjobs.com/{tenant}/{site_id}` in the URL. Once verified: add to `src/applypilot/config/employers.yaml`. Expected yield: ~500-2000 additional local jobs.

6. **Playwright-based scraper for custom ATSes — TODO sub-project.** Microsoft (Eightfold, blocked JSON API), Google (custom boq-hiring, no JSON), Apple (session-cookie-gated), Meta (CF + GraphQL blocked), Starbucks (Eightfold blocked). The Missing-Employer ATS audit 2026-04-24 detailed each: see agent `a2d1ba79afbc5593f` output. Combined these represent ~1500-5000 high-value local jobs. Build a Playwright harness that opens the site, waits for JS-rendered listings, extracts them. Non-trivial (~2-5 days per scraper).
3. **Lever scraper (small, sub-project):** Stripe, Highspot, Outreach, Rover. Lever API: `https://api.lever.co/v0/postings/{slug}?mode=json`.
4. **Ashby scraper (small, sub-project):** MotherDuck, Statsig, Deepgram, Common Room, DevZero, Impart Security, Clarify. Ashby has a private API — may need Playwright scrape.

Roadmap: do Workday first (aligns with existing code), then build a single generic "JSON-API ATS" scraper that covers Greenhouse + Lever (both have clean JSON) as one medium-sized sub-project.
